# Cross-cutting findings (unapplied)

Five critics each read all eleven work-stream specifications **together** and reported
conflicts their individual authors could not see. Their unanimous headline was the same:
*the specs are individually strong and do not compose* — eleven streams independently
re-decided every shared artifact, and three different phase-numbering schemes are in
active use across the set.

> **Status: these findings are NOT yet applied to the specs.** The workflow stage that
> would have absorbed them into each document died on a session limit. Every finding
> below is still open. Treat the eleven specs as excellent drafts that disagree with
> each other on the points listed here.

**231 findings** — 75 critical, 118 major, 38 minor.

This is the *union of two independent critic passes* over the same eleven specs (the
workflow re-ran its critic stage). Exact duplicates were removed, but the two passes
phrased overlapping problems differently, so near-duplicates remain — read them as two
reviewers agreeing rather than as two separate defects. A single pass reported 115.

## Critical (75)

### C1. `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** There is no master document. `docs/observability/` contains eleven work-stream specs (22,203 lines) and nothing else — no `00-blueprint.md`, no README, no index (verified: `ls docs/observability/` returns the eleven files plus `_drafts/`). Consequently there is no single place holding the phase plan, the cross-stream decision register, the shared-name registry, or a roll-up estimate. Every contradiction below exists because each spec had to re-decide shared facts locally and no reviewer before now saw two of them side by side. Symptomatically: 91 cross-references across five docs point at superseded draft filenames (`tenancy.md`, `read-path.md`, `ops.md`, …), 24 of them explicitly at `_drafts/`, and exactly one reference in the entire corpus points at a numbered final doc.

**Fix.** Write `00-blueprint.md` before any further spec work. It carries: (1) the phase plan with entry/exit criteria; (2) a decision register for the twelve cross-stream decisions listed in these findings, each with one owner and a close-by date; (3) a name registry (constant, database, env var, tRPC namespace, Prisma model) that every spec cites rather than restates; (4) a roll-up effort table that de-duplicates the overlaps; (5) a rule that cross-references use the numbered filename and a section anchor, never a draft name or a line number.

### C2. `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** There is no master plan document. `ls docs/observability/` returns eleven work-stream specs and a `_drafts/` directory — no `00-blueprint.md`, no ADR, nothing that owns a cross-cutting decision. Each spec is individually excellent (better sourced than most production code), and each one independently re-decides the shared artifacts. The result is that the eleven documents do not describe one system. They describe between two and four systems that share a vocabulary. Every critical finding below is a symptom of this one absence, not an independent defect. Concretely, the following artifacts have two or more incompatible definitions across the set: the ClickHouse database name, the compose service name, the gigapipe URL/auth/db env vars, the tRPC router namespace, the persistence shape of a metric report, the zod schema of a metric query, the Prisma models for alert state, the `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse` implementation, the per-signal ingest stamping rules, the log ingest wire protocol, and the `by (op_project_id)` compiler invariant that the security boundary's only runtime detector depends on. Additionally, docs 03, 04, 06, 07 and 11 cite the *superseded* `_drafts/*.md` files as their authoritative dependencies (31 such references in 06-traces, 37 in 11-testing), so several finals are specified against decisions that the finals of those streams have since reversed.

**Fix.** Do not start implementation. Write `00-blueprint.md` first, as a decision record with exactly one named owner per shared artifact, and make it the only place those are defined: (1) ClickHouse database name and env var; (2) the complete GIGAPIPE_*/TELEMETRY_* env surface; (3) compose service names; (4) tRPC router namespace; (5) how a metric report is persisted; (6) the single `zMetricQuery`; (7) the alert Prisma models; (8) the deletion function signature and call sites; (9) per-signal strip/stamp rules; (10) whether the compiler always emits `by (op_project_id)`; (11) the log ingest wire protocol. Then rewrite each of the eleven to *consume* those rather than restate them, and replace every `_drafts/*.md` reference with the numbered final. Budget a week. It is cheaper than any one of the conflicts below.

### C3. `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** There is no master plan. The directory contains eleven work-stream specs (25,270 lines) and a _drafts/ folder, and no 00-blueprint, no decision log, no cross-document index, no critical path, no phase-gate table and no cut list. Every cross-cutting conflict in this review is a conflict that exists precisely because there is no document whose job is to hold the seams. The specs themselves are aware of this in fragments — 04 D1 files 'an action for the P0 owner', 11 Q1 says the package question blocks 'before anyone writes a test file', 07 Q1 says its schema question 'blocks §1, §3, §9.2 and §12' — but each is filed as that document's open question rather than anywhere a single reader would see all of them together.

**Fix.** Write docs/observability/00-blueprint.md before any further work-stream editing, and give it exclusive ownership of exactly the things that must be decided once: (1) the phase gates and the critical path, with P1 explicitly named as the serialising dependency; (2) the canonical zMetricQuery, its file and its owner; (3) the log-ingest topology; (4) the Report.dataSource decision; (5) the env-var and kill-switch namespace; (6) the ClickHouse client convention and the package location; (7) the P0 gate list, including the ClickHouse profile and the three ClickHouse/snappy experiments; (8) an explicit cut list and a stated MVP. Add a rule that a work-stream doc may not restate a blueprint decision, only cite it — the drift in this review is entirely restatement drift.

### C4. `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** There is no master plan. `ls docs/observability/` returns 01–11 plus `_drafts/`; there is no 00, no README, no index, and a repo-wide grep for `00-`, "master plan", "blueprint" or "the parent plan" finds only 05-logs.md:1802 referring to a "parent plan" that is not a file. Nothing owns cross-stream sequencing, the dependency graph, the program-wide MVP, or the aggregate cost. Three concrete consequences, all verified. (a) The phase vocabulary collides: 11-testing uses the brief's global P0–P6; 10-ops uses P0–P8 as its own *local* numbering (P7 = scoped ClickHouse user, P8 = retention tiering); 08-schema's section headers say "### 4. P4/P5/P7 — telemetry metering" and "### 6. P8/P9 — the metric alert tables" while 08's own Sequencing table (line 2535) assigns those same migrations to P2 and P5. A reader cannot tell what "P5" means without knowing which document is speaking. (b) Nobody has summed the effort. The eleven Effort sections total roughly 70–80 engineer-weeks (01 ≈4.9w; 02 ≈5–7.5w; 03 ≈7–9w; 04 ≈6.4w; 05 ≈13.5w; 06 28–34d; 07 18–21d; 08 ~11.75d; 09 8–9w; 10 ≈7–8.5w; 11 28.5d), and every one of them says "one engineer" — there is no staffing plan, no parallelism model and no critical path. (c) Four documents (03, 04, 05, 10) each define their own "minimum shippable slice", and they are different slices; nobody has checked that they compose into one thing a customer can use. 04's "one metric chart renders" (2.8w) silently requires 01's Tier 1 (4.9w), 02's P1a (3.5–5.5w), 10's P0 and 08's migration 22.

**Fix.** Write `00-blueprint.md` owning four things and nothing else: (1) one phase vocabulary, P0–P6 as the brief defines it, with every document's local numbering rewritten to it or explicitly namespaced (`10-P7` not `P7`); (2) a dependency graph and critical path across the eleven streams; (3) the summed effort with a staffing and parallelism model, stated as engineer-weeks and calendar weeks separately; (4) one cross-stream MVP — the smallest set of slices from 01/02/04/08/10 that gets a real metric chart in front of a design partner — with everything else explicitly after it. Owner: tech lead. ~3–5 days to write, then a standing artifact updated per phase.

### C5. `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** There is no master plan document. `docs/observability/` contains eleven work-stream specs and a `_drafts/` directory, and nothing else (verified by listing). No document rolls effort up by phase, names an owner per phase, states how many engineers the plan assumes, or de-duplicates cross-stream work. Consequently: (a) summing the eleven Effort sections gives ~78–92 engineer-weeks (01: 7.4–8.4 w; 02: 5–7.5 w; 03: 7–9 w; 04: 6.4 w; 05: ~19 w; 06: ~7.8–9 w; 07: 3.6–4.2 w; 08: 2.35 w; 09: 8–9 w; 10: 7–8.5 w; 11: 5.7 w), a number no document states; (b) P0 is budgeted at `1 w` in 10-ops, the only document that gives P0 a number, while P0 as a phase is at minimum 10-ops 1 w + 05 §7.1's bootstrap 1.5 w + 11's workspace wiring and harness 0.7 w + 08's migration 22 / telemetry-client / TTL DDL ~0.6 w ≈ 3.8 w; (c) 05's Effort explicitly states its 1.5 w bootstrap is 'a real 1.5 w removed from P3 and added to P0', but 10-ops's P0 row does not contain it and 10-ops's total is unchanged — the week is subtracted from one document and added to none.

**Fix.** Write `00-blueprint.md` carrying: a phase × work-stream effort matrix with one named owner per phase; the assumed engineer count and which lanes run concurrently; a de-duplicated test budget (see the double-counting finding); and a single canonical phase vocabulary. Every stream's Effort section then states only its own rows and cites the blueprint for the rollup.

### C6. `00-blueprint.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** The security runbook depends on an operational control plane that no document owns and that the repository does not have. 01-tenancy-and-security.md §Remediation (line 1747) makes step 1 of the breach response "**Read kill switch.** A per-signal feature flag (`telemetry.read.metrics|logs|traces`) that fails telemetry reads **closed**" and step 2 an "**Ingest kill switch**, global and per-project". 04-read-path.md §14 (line 2071) states the opposite as fact: "The repo has **no feature-flag mechanism** — no flag column, no constants table, no helper. `GIGAPIPE_URL` unset is therefore the only off switch, and it is per-deployment" — and 03-metrics-engine.md:1946 and 10-ops-retention-billing.md:1870 both repeat that `GIGAPIPE_URL` is *the* kill switch. I verified 04 is right: `grep -rn "featureFlag|feature_flag|FeatureFlag" --include=*.ts --include=*.tsx --include=*.prisma apps packages` returns zero hits, and there is no settings/flag model in `packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma`. So the first two steps of the response to a confirmed cross-tenant leak are unimplementable. Separately, 02-ingest-gateway.md §11 does define Redis keys `telemetry:killswitch:global` and `telemetry:killswitch:project:{projectId}` and its Interfaces table (line 1656) assigns their owner as "admin UI / runbook" — but no document contains a work item for that admin UI or CLI, `admin/src/commands/` gains no `set-telemetry-killswitch`, and 09-ui-surfaces.md does not mention kill switches at all. `TELEMETRY_ENFORCE=warn|block` (02:1392) has the same problem: it is an env var, so flipping it during an incident requires a deploy.

**Fix.** Give one document (00-blueprint, or a twelfth stream) the whole control plane: the Redis key schema, the read-side per-signal switch 01 requires, the per-project ingest shed, the enforcement mode, and the operator surface that sets them (an `admin/src/commands/telemetry-controls.ts` in the style of `admin/src/commands/lookup-client.ts` is the cheapest form and needs no dashboard work). Make the read switch a Redis key checked by `observabilityProcedure` with a short cache, not a feature-flag framework — 04's refusal to build a general flag system is correct, but a four-key Redis control plane is not one. Then either 01 §Remediation cites the real mechanism or 04 §14 is amended; today a reader of either doc is misinformed. About 3–4 days including the admin command and a runbook page, and it blocks P1 GA because it is the response half of a security boundary.

### C7. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Log ingest is specified two mutually exclusive ways and neither document knows about the other. 02 D3 / §6.2 decodes OTLP protobuf, strips at scope+record, stamps the resource, re-encodes protobuf and forwards to gigapipe's POST /v1/logs; 01 §4.8's table and tests T1.5/T1.6 codify that. 05 D1 rejects exactly that design and pushes Loki JSON to /loki/api/v1/push instead. 05 is right, and I verified it: writer/utils/unmarshal/otlplogs.go:22-58 folds resource, scope AND record attributes into one attrsMap and then appends trace_id and span_id as labels, and the fingerprint is computed over the whole surviving label set (unmarshal.go:250-270) — so under 02's design one trace id is one new fingerprint, i.e. one time_series row and one time_series_gin row per label per stream per day, at ~10k new series/s for a busy customer. There is no configuration that disables it. 02 also declares `/loki/api/v1/push` explicitly not exposed and tells the schema stream that `type ∈ {1,2}` is safe 'only because this gateway does not expose /loki/api/v1/push' (02 §1, §Interfaces), while 05 §4.1 exposes POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push to customers and uses the Loki push route internally.

**Fix.** Adopt 05 D1 as the plan's log-ingest topology and rewrite 02 §6.2, 02 §1's not-exposed table, 02's Interfaces guarantee about `type`, and 01 §4.8's OTLP-logs row against it. Delete the OTLP-logs protobuf re-encode leg from 02's P1a (it is ~1 of the 4 rewrites and part of the 4–6 d line item) and move it into 05's P3.4 where it already exists. Re-derive 01's T1.5/T1.6 against the Loki-JSON mapper rather than a forwarded OTLP body. Record in the blueprint that the `type ∈ {1,2}` invariant is now enforced by OpenPanel's own envelope writer never emitting value index 2, not by a route not existing — and add the regression test 05 §1.2 implies.

### C8. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Three incompatible definitions of zMetricQuery exist across five documents, and the plan only knows about two. 07 Q1 correctly identifies a conflict between 01's version (filters[{name,operator:eq|neq|re|nre}], fn with 11 members including irate/deriv, aggregation including topk/bottomk/quantile, k) and '03's version'. But the version 07 Q1 quotes as 03's — {metricType: counter|gauge|histogram, fn 5 values including 'value', matchers[{name,op:=|!=|=~|!~}], fill, seriesLimit} — is not what 03 §2 actually specifies today. 09 D3 quotes the same non-existent schema and declares it 'consumed verbatim'. Verified by grep against 03: `seriesLimit` appears 0 times, `'value'` as an fn appears 0 times; 03 uses `filters` with `operator: eq|neq|re|nre`, has `scale` and `summary` (which 09 omits), and its `fn` enum has eleven members. So 09's entire nuqs key table (§4.2 `mt`, `w`, `max`), its picker defaults (§4.3 `fn: 'value'` for gauges), its four-way operator adapter (D9) and its MetricMatchers component are built against a schema that does not exist, and 07's `query: zMetricQuery` line cannot be written.

**Fix.** Pin one definition in the blueprint, name its file and its owner, and have 07, 09 and 01 re-derive against it in a single pass rather than three. 03's is the right one to keep: it is the only one whose fn set is proved to be a subset of gigapipe's accelerated `rangeFns`/`aggFns` (03 §0, T-C6), it is the object the chart builder persists, and it has already removed topk/bottomk. Concretely: delete 01's zMetricQuery block (01 §7.2) and replace it with a pointer; rewrite 09 D3, §4.2's table, §4.3's defaults table and D9's operator mapping against `filters`/`operator`/`scale`/`summary`; resolve 07 Q1 as 'P2's, and it is the current 03 §2 body, not the one Q1 quotes'. Add a CI check or a doc test that the schema appears in exactly one document.

### C9. `00-blueprint.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** Load-bearing decision #1 — "gigapipe is the storage + query engine; every write and every read passes through apps/api first" — has been quietly abandoned by the individual streams, and nobody has re-derived what that means for the dependency. 04-read-path D4 moves all metric-name, label-key and label-value enumeration to direct ClickHouse SQL against gigapipe's tables (six good reasons, all verified). 06-traces T1 and T2 move *all* trace reads — search and by-id — to direct ClickHouse SQL, and 01-tenancy D4 had explicitly rejected exactly that ("it puts OpenPanel's read path onto gigapipe's internal schema, which contradicts the plan's decision that every read goes through gigapipe") after building `compileTraceQL`, `filterTraceToProject`, the `tempoSearch`/`tempoTraceById` allow-list entries and tests T1.36–T1.39, T1.62. 05-logs L1 removes the OTLP log write path. What is actually left going through gigapipe's reader is PromQL `query_range`/`query` and LogQL `query_range`. That is a real and defensible use — I verified gigapipe embeds `prometheus/prometheus v0.314.0`'s actual `promql.NewEngine` (reader/router/prometheus_query_range.go:28) behind ~12k lines of ClickHouse transpiler, and re-deriving counter-reset handling, `histogram_quantile` interpolation and staleness/lookback in SQL is not a weekend — but it is a much smaller thing than the plan's premise describes, and the tenancy apparatus, the AGPL posture, the shared-ClickHouse contention, the three-container compose ballet and the irreversible partition-key pre-creation are all priced against the larger thing.

**Fix.** Restate decision #1 honestly in the blueprint: "gigapipe is the PromQL and LogQL execution engine and the metrics/logs write path. Trace reads and all metadata enumeration are direct ClickHouse SQL from apps/api against gigapipe's schema; log writes are translated to Loki JSON at the gateway." Then re-run the buy-vs-build decision against that reduced surface, explicitly, before P0 — because the reduced surface is one engine and one write path, and the direct-SQL precedent that 04 and 06 have already set makes the alternative (own the samples schema outright, with a real `project_id` column, and implement the five v1 functions in SQL) a live option rather than a hypothetical. Also record the coupling that direct SQL creates: 06 and 04 now depend on gigapipe's *internal* table layout, so every image bump is a schema-compatibility review, which only 04 §13 currently budgets.

### C10. `00-blueprint.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** The privacy and legal artifacts are named as required by three documents and owned by none. OpenPanel publishes a real DPA at `apps/public/content/pages/dpa.mdx` — §6 is a sub-processor table, §9 "Data retention and deletion" states "Analytics events are retained for as long as your account is active" — and a privacy policy at `apps/public/content/pages/privacy.mdx` with a "What we do not collect" section, an enumerated "Types of Data Collected" list built around "aggregated, anonymized data", and a right-to-delete statement at line 150. Telemetry invalidates all of it: 02:2095 calls OTLP log bodies "the most PII-dense data the product has ever" carried, and 01 §12's settled position is that **per-subject erasure inside telemetry is not offered** — project deletion is the only erasure path (05:1503) — which directly contradicts privacy.mdx's deletion-rights text. 05 open question 12 ("nothing scrubs an IP or a token from a body") is explicitly deferred as "a product decision. Out of scope for P3". 01 §12 states the requirement plainly — "That must be stated in the product's data-handling documentation before telemetry is generally available, not discovered in a DPA review" — and then no document lists `dpa.mdx` or `privacy.mdx` as a file it touches.

**Fix.** Assign an owner (founder/product, with counsel) and a deliverable list: edits to `dpa.mdx` §9 for the telemetry retention window and to its sub-processor table (self-hosted gigapipe adds none, which is a selling point worth writing down); edits to `privacy.mdx` covering free-text log bodies and span attributes as a new data category and correcting the deletion-rights text to match 01 §12; a public data-handling page stating the erasure SLA 05 already specifies ("within 24 hours of the deletion job running, bounded by mutation completion"), that telemetry is not backed up (10 D22), and that body redaction is the customer's responsibility; and a decision on 05 OQ12. Ship-blocking for a paid EU product. ~3–5 days of writing on top of a real legal review.

### C11. `00-blueprint.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** No document owns reconciling the cross-stream contracts, and the two ingest documents contradict each other head-on about how logs reach gigapipe. 02-ingest-gateway.md §1's route table (line 55) says `/telemetry/v1/logs` accepts `application/x-protobuf` only, returns 415 for JSON, and forwards to `POST {GIGAPIPE_INTERNAL_URL}/v1/logs`. 05-logs.md L1 (line 21) says the opposite in its title: "apps/api decodes OTLP itself and pushes **Loki JSON**; it never forwards OTLP to gigapipe", explicitly rejecting the forward-to-`/v1/logs` model on a cardinality argument (one `trace_id` per fingerprint at 10k lines/s), and 05 line 553 lists `/telemetry/v1/logs` as accepting `application/x-protobuf | application/json`. 05 also introduces a route 02's table does not contain at all, `POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` (05 lines 554, 2150, and effort item P3.3 at line 2247), which 05 §10 then depends on for OpenPanel's own self-instrumentation. Whichever is right, the other document's design is wrong downstream: 02's payload-rewrite (§4), protobuf codec (§5), log metering (`logBytes`) and error semantics (§9) are all built on the forward model. There is no arbitration document, no interface-conflict register, and no phase in which someone diffs the eleven `## Interfaces` sections against each other.

**Fix.** Add a `00-blueprint.md` whose job is the contract register, not prose: one table of every cross-stream interface (route, function signature, env var, Redis key, label name, error shape) with exactly one owning document, and a CI-cheap check that each row appears in its owner's `Exposed` section and in every consumer's `Consumed` section. Settle the logs write path first — 05 L1's cardinality argument is the stronger one and is verified against gigapipe source (`writer/utils/unmarshal/otlplogs.go:39-62`), so 02 §1, §4, §5, §9 and its metering should be rewritten to the decode-and-push-Loki-JSON model, with `/telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` added to 02's route table as a P3 row. Roughly 3–5 days to build the register plus 2–3 days of 02 rework; owner is whoever owns 00-blueprint, with 02 and 05 as respondents.

### C12. `00-blueprint.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** There is no documentation work-stream. The only new public page anyone owns is `apps/public/content/docs/self-hosting/observability.mdx` (10 §4.5, registered in `self-hosting/meta.json`). A grep for `.mdx` across all eleven documents returns fifteen references, all of them either that page or edits to existing self-hosting/api pages. Nothing owns: a product docs section under `apps/public/content/docs/meta.json` (which today has Introduction / Get started / Tracking / API / MCP / Dashboard / Self-hosting / Migration and would need an Observability entry); how a customer sends metrics, logs and traces; a reference for the query surfaces (`zMetricQuery`, `zLogQuery`, TraceQL subset); alert setup; the session/profile correlation setup that is the plan's differentiator; retention and quota explanation; an api-reference page for `/telemetry/*` beside the existing `api/track.mdx`, `api/export.mdx`, `api/insights.mdx` and `api-reference/rate-limits.mdx`; and pricing-page copy for telemetry units. Meanwhile 02 §16 contains finished, paste-ready OTel Collector, SDK-direct, Prometheus remote-write and Grafana Alloy snippets with no page to live on, and at least five documents say "the docs page says this" / "goes in the DPA-facing docs" without naming a file. 05's P3.9 budgets 1.25w that includes "docs" as one item among six.

**Fix.** Create a documentation work-stream with a named owner and a file manifest: a new `Observability` section in `apps/public/content/docs/`, registered in `meta.json`; pages for connect (hosting 02 §16 verbatim), metrics, logs, traces + correlation, alerts, retention and quota, and data handling; an `api/telemetry.mdx` beside the existing API pages; plus the self-hosting page 10 already owns. Each stream lead supplies content for their surface; one person owns the section's structure and shipping it. ~2–3 weeks, and it must be sequenced with the phases rather than trailing them.

### C13. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The set gives four mutually exclusive answers to whether an anonymous public share link can execute a PromQL query, and nobody owns the decision. Doc 03 §13.5 and doc 09 §5.7 ship it (with clamps); doc 11 tests it (rows 606, 645, 654); doc 04 D6 rejects `shareId` outright with FORBIDDEN; doc 08 blocks share creation for `dataSource='metrics'` and 404s pre-existing shares. If 03/09/11 land and 04/08 do not, the resulting surface is unauthenticated by design. I verified the three enabling facts at chart.ts:83-143 and :588-606: `chartProcedure` is a `publicProcedure`; on the share branch the caller still controls `range`, `startDate`, `endDate` and `interval` (`range: input.range ?? ctx.report.range` and the same for the other three); and `cacheMiddleware` keys on `trpc:${path}:` + `JSON.stringify(rawInput)` (trpc.ts:198-207), so every distinct `startDate` is a fresh miss. There is no `rateLimitMiddleware` anywhere in chart.ts. One share link therefore lets an anonymous caller issue unbounded distinct multi-second PromQL queries against the same ClickHouse that carries event ingest. Doc 09 §5.7 asks the API stream for an 11,000-step clamp; doc 03 §13.5 asks for a rate limit plus a window clamp plus negative caching; neither requirement is written into the document that owns `chart.ts`.

**Fix.** Record one decision in the blueprint and propagate it. The safe default is doc 08's: metric reports are not shareable in P2, share creation rejects `dataSource='metrics'`, and the read path 404s. If shares are wanted, all of doc 03 §13.5's three controls plus doc 09's step clamp must be a single owned work item in one document, not three requests made of an unnamed "API stream". Independently of that decision, `chartProcedure`'s share branch should assert `rawInput.projectId === ctx.report.projectId` (doc 03 §13.4 is right that the current key/value split is an accident, not an invariant) and doc 11's share-path rows must be conditioned on which answer wins so the suite cannot go green against a design that was cut.

### C14. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** `Report.dataSource` is simultaneously mandated and rejected. 08 S1 makes it a new Postgres enum `DataSource { events metrics }` plus a column, with a rejected-alternative argument against putting it in `Report.options`, and budgets 2 days for it plus nine whitelist sites. 09 D8 enumerates eight write paths that must all learn to write `dataSource` 'in the same PR as the column', and 09's failure modes F2/F4 are entirely about it. 03 §15.1 explicitly rejects the column — 'That last row is the point: no migration. Dispatch is on `series.some(s => s.type === \'metric\')` behind one helper (packages/db/src/engine/data-source.ts, getReportDataSource(series)), not on a Report.dataSource column' — and gives a cost argument for the rejection. Verified: `dataSource` appears 22 times in 08 and 19 times in 09, once in 03 (the rejection), and zero times in 01/02/05/06/07/10. Neither 08 nor 09 cites 03 on this.

**Fix.** Decide in the blueprint and make the losing document say so. 03's argument is stronger on the merits — the discriminator has to reach zReportInput regardless because ctx.report is null on every non-shared query (verified: packages/trpc/src/routers/chart.ts), so the column buys a filterable index and nothing else. If 03 wins, delete 08 S1 and its 2-day line item, and rewrite 09 D8's eight-site table as 'the union member must survive the eight projections', which is a different and smaller change. If 08 wins, 03 §15.1 must be rewritten and its P2.5 estimate raised. Either way the choice must be made before P2.5 code is written, because the deploy-ordering hazard in 03 §15.2 (an old pod rewriting a metric series to `unknown_event` and a user then saving it) is real under both designs and its mitigation differs.

### C15. `04-read-path.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** 04 §11 ('What this work-stream deliberately does not do') states as a principle: 'It does not create, migrate or ALTER anything in TELEMETRY_DB. gigapipe creates its own database and runs its own schema upgrades at boot (ctrl/ctrl.go -> maintain.go:40-49 -> shared.go:47-61, CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS). Duplicating that in packages/db/code-migrations invites drift against gigapipe's own migration ledger.' That is a direct argument against the single most load-bearing and most irreversible decision in the whole plan: 08 S6's `packages/db/code-migrations/22-telemetry-database.ts`, which pre-creates the gigapipe database and two of its tables so that `type` lands in the PARTITION BY, and 10 D4, which calls it 'the one irreversible choice in the whole work-stream'. Neither 08 nor 10 cites 04 on this, and 04 does not cite 08 or 10.

**Fix.** Resolve explicitly in the blueprint and edit 04 §11. 08/10 are right on the merits: PARTITION BY cannot be ALTERed, rotateTables unconditionally forces `ttl_only_drop_parts = 1`, and both tables are `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` — so first writer wins and per-signal retention is impossible without the pre-create. 04's drift concern is real but is answered by 08 S7's column-order and sorting-key contract plus the per-bump log.sql/traces.sql diff (08's CI harness line item), not by declining to pre-create. Change 04 §11 to 'this work-stream does not create or ALTER TELEMETRY_DB; 08-schema-changes.md's migration 22 owns the two pre-created tables and the per-bump schema diff', so the two documents stop contradicting each other in the one place a reader is most likely to look for permission not to do the irreversible thing.

### C16. `00-blueprint.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** No document owns the legal and compliance artefacts, and the plan as specified contradicts OpenPanel's published DPA in at least four checkable places. A repo-wide grep of all eleven docs for `dpa.mdx`, `privacy.mdx`, `privacy polic`, `sub-processor`, `subprocessor`, `data processing` and `terms.mdx` returns zero hits. Meanwhile `apps/public/content/pages/dpa.mdx` is a live contractual document that says: §7 "IP addresses are never stored" and "No cookies or persistent cross-device identifiers are used" — but OTLP traces routinely carry `client.address`/`net.peer.ip`/`http.client_ip` span attributes and log bodies carry IPs, and nothing in 02 or 05 strips them; §7 "Regular backups are performed", while 08-schema-changes.md §12 ("Backup scope") and 10 §13 ("Telemetry is not backed up (D15)") deliberately exclude `openpanel_telemetry` from every backup scope; §4 "At your choice, delete or return all Personal Data upon termination", while no document specifies any telemetry export and 01:1673 has `export-for-cloud.sh` explicitly excluding telemetry; §9 "Data retention and deletion" gives retention periods for analytics events and session replays and would need a third row. §6's sub-processor table would also need review if telemetry ever transits anything new. Separately, 08 §12 states a substantive legal position — "OpenPanel does not offer per-subject telemetry erasure. If a subject erasure request arrives, the answer is the project-level retention window" — which is a GDPR Art. 17 stance being taken inside an engineering spec, with no legal owner and no corresponding text in the DPA or privacy policy.

**Fix.** Add a compliance work item with a named owner covering: a diff of `apps/public/content/pages/dpa.mdx` §4, §6, §7 and §9 and `pages/privacy.mdx` against what the plan actually stores; a telemetry row in the retention schedule; an explicit, published statement of the no-backup posture and the no-per-subject-erasure posture (both are defensible, neither is currently disclosed); and a decision on whether the "IP addresses are never stored" claim is qualified to analytics or enforced for telemetry by a mandatory attribute drop at the gateway. The engineering half is small — one gateway attribute denylist — but the sequencing matters: these are contractual statements that customers rely on, so they must be amended before the first telemetry byte from a Cloud customer lands, not at P6. Roughly 2 days of engineering plus legal review; blocks Cloud GA, not self-hosted.

### C17. `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The single control standing between an unbounded telemetry query and the ClickHouse that serves the analytics product is phased three different ways and budgeted by nobody. I independently verified 05's I10: reader/registry/registry.go builds the reader's clickhouse.Options with `Settings: nil` — no max_execution_time, no max_memory_usage, no max_bytes_to_read — while writer/chwrapper/factory.go sets `max_execution_time: 60`. So every PromQL and LogQL read runs unbounded against the shared instance. 05 I10 calls the dedicated `gigapipe` ClickHouse user plus the `gigapipe_reader` settings profile 'a P0 dependency of the logs work-stream, not a P3 nicety' and 05 §1848 makes it a hard gate: 'gigapipe is not started against any ClickHouse instance until this has run'. 10 D16 says the opposite: 'gigapipe connects as `default` in v1' and the scoped user plus profile is a named P7 follow-up. 08 S16 declines to touch clickhouse-user-config.xml and points at 10 D16. 10's P0 effort row (1 w) contains no line for a CH user, grants or a settings profile. So under the plan as written, P0 through P6 — the whole ~70-week build — runs telemetry as an unrestricted `default` user with no per-query bound.

**Fix.** Adopt 05's position and make the profile a P0 gate in the blueprint. It is genuinely cheap: 10 D16's own stated blocker (the git-tracked, :ro-mounted clickhouse-user-config.xml) is already solved by the pattern quiz.ts uses for the gitignored Caddyfile, and 10 D16 admits 'it is deferred on risk budget, not on knowledge'. Add the line item to 10's P0 (~0.5 w), have 08 S16 own the `CREATE SETTINGS PROFILE` for the cloud path, and settle 05's open question 5 (does a 30 s max_execution_time break gigapipe's writer under a large insert batch) with the two-user split before P1 rather than after. Until it exists, no telemetry route should be enabled against a ClickHouse that also serves events.

### C18. `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Three incompatible phase-numbering schemes are in simultaneous use, and one document contradicts itself. (1) 10-ops uses its own P0–P8 where P3 = Polar catalogue/billing, P4 = email templates, P5 = the quota shed hook, P7 = Coolify/scoped ClickHouse user, P8 = tiering — none of which matches master P3 = logs, P4 = traces, P5 = alerts. (2) 08's section headings adopt 10-ops's numbering — `### 4. P4/P5/P7 — telemetry metering and the erasure ledger` (:523), `### 5. P6 — TelemetrySchemaState` (:810), `### 6. P8/P9 — the metric alert tables` (:894) — and directly contradict 08's own Sequencing table (:2535), which assigns `telemetry_schema_state` to **P0**, `telemetry_metering` to **P2** and `metric_alerts` to **P5**. (3) 07 labels an interfaces section `### Consumed from the read-path work-stream (P4/P3)` (:2539) and its Effort says 'P4's `packages/gigapipe` transport' — where P4 means *document 04*, the read path, not phase P4, traces. A scheduler reading 07 will believe alerting depends on the traces phase.

**Fix.** Adopt master P0–P6 as the only phase vocabulary. Rewrite 08's six section headings to match its own Sequencing table. Renumber 10-ops's internal phases into the master scheme (its P1 metering → master P1; P2 TTL reconciler → master P2; P3 billing and P5 shed → see the quota finding; P8 tiering → master P6). In 07, refer to sibling streams by filename only, never by number.

### C19. `00-blueprint.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Roughly 110 open-question rows (10 in 01, 13 in 02, 9 in 03, 11 in 04, 16 in 05, 11 in 06, 18 in 07, 12 in 08, 9 in 09, 13 in 10, 16 in 11) plus 61 `UNVERIFIED` markers are distributed across eleven documents with per-document ID spaces that collide and duplicate. `10 Q1` ("Does `TTL <expr> DELETE WHERE <cond>` parse on CH 24.3/25.10/26.1?") and `08 U3` ("multi-clause DELETE WHERE does not parse") are the same experiment with two IDs in two documents. `10 Q3` (does the production CH user hold CREATE DATABASE) and `05 OQ4` (can the Cloud CH user issue CREATE USER / CREATE SETTINGS PROFILE) are one `SHOW GRANTS` call. Several are hard P0 gates for more than one stream — 08 U1 (`MODIFY ORDER BY` on a byte-identical key) gates the partition key that 10 D4 calls "the one irreversible choice in the whole work-stream". 10 has a "P0 gates — run these before anything else in **this document** is built" section; nothing does that for the programme. There is no consolidated pre-flight list, no owner per question, and no due date.

**Fix.** In `00-blueprint.md`, consolidate every open question and UNVERIFIED marker into one table with a global ID, the owning document, the owner, the experiment that settles it, and which phases it blocks; de-duplicate the ClickHouse DDL experiments (`TTL … DELETE WHERE`, `MODIFY ORDER BY`, `materialize_ttl_after_modify`, grants) into a single P0 spike run once against all three pinned images (26.1.3.52 dev, 25.10.2.65 template, 24.3.2-alpine coolify) and publish the results back into 08 and 10. ~1 day to consolidate; the spike work itself is ~1 week and is currently budgeted only inside 10.

### C20. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Three different homes are specified for the gigapipe HTTP client and query compilers, and two of them are declared as sole owner. 01 §3.1 puts everything in a new workspace package: `packages/gigapipe/src/{labels,client}.ts`, `packages/gigapipe/src/ingest/*`, `packages/gigapipe/vendor/opentelemetry-proto/**`. 04 D1 accepts that package but restructures it ("`src/client.ts` as tenancy.md sketched it is **deleted**", `GIGAPIPE_ROUTES` moves to `src/routes.ts`) and calls the reconciliation "the highest-priority open question — two engineers building two incompatible `packages/gigapipe` is the single most expensive failure available here" (Q8). 03 §1 ignores both and puts the client in `packages/db/src/gigapipe/{client,types,errors}.ts`. 05 §6.1 also puts it in `packages/db/src/gigapipe/client.ts` and lists its own Open Question 9: "Where does the shared gigapipe HTTP client live?". 02 D9 puts the whole ingest half in `apps/api/src/telemetry/` with protos at `apps/api/src/telemetry/proto/`, contradicting 01's `packages/gigapipe/src/ingest/` + `packages/gigapipe/vendor/`; 05 §4.4 puts protos at a third location, `apps/api/proto/`. 11 §2 encodes the 02+04 split as fact, so its file layout is unbuildable against 03 and 05.

**Fix.** Settle 04's Q8 and 05's Q9 as one decision before any code. The coherent split is 04's layer split plus 02's gateway placement: `packages/gigapipe` owns transport, route allow-list, error hierarchy, tenancy primitives and all three query compilers (importable by worker and MCP, Prisma-free); `apps/api/src/telemetry/` owns the ingest gateway, the vendored OTLP protos and the rewrite modules (it is the only consumer and 02 D9's `deps.ts` seam is already designed for it); `packages/db` owns only what needs `ch`/Prisma — the metrics engine stages (03 §1's `engine/metrics/*`), the log envelope/parse helpers (05), and `telemetry-metadata.service.ts`. Rewrite 01 §3.1, 03 §1, 05 §6.1 and 05 §4.4 to that layout; delete 05's Open Question 9 and 04's Q8 once done.

### C21. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The tenancy label constant is defined under two names in two places and hardcoded as a literal in two more. 01 §1.1: `export const TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL = 'op_project_id'` in `packages/constants`. 02 §4.0: `export const OP_PROJECT_LABEL = 'op_project_id'` in the same file, and 02 says these live in `@openpanel/constants` "because the query compiler must inject a matcher on the identical string and must not carry a second copy of the literal". 04 §Interfaces consumes `TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL`; 06 §Interfaces and 11 §Interfaces consume `OP_PROJECT_LABEL` and say "two copies of any of these literals is a correlation that silently returns nothing". Meanwhile 03 §4.1 declares `const OP_PROJECT_LABEL = 'op_project_id'` locally in `compile.ts` and 07 §7 declares `const PROJECT_LABEL = 'op_project_id'` locally in the evaluator. Verified: `packages/constants/index.ts` today exports lowercase-object names (`chartTypes`, `intervals`, `operators`) plus SCREAMING_CASE scalars (`NOT_SET_VALUE`, `RESERVED_EVENT_NAMES`); neither proposed name exists.

**Fix.** Pick one name — `OP_PROJECT_LABEL` has three consumers (02, 06, 11) against two for `TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL` (01, 04) and matches 06's sibling constants `OP_SESSION_LABEL`/`OP_PROFILE_LABEL`/`OP_EXCEPTION_TYPE_LABEL`. Update 01 §1.1 and §Interfaces and 04 §Interfaces to `OP_PROJECT_LABEL`. Delete the local literals in 03 §4.1 and 07 §7 and have both import from `@openpanel/constants`; 11's finding-#1 handoff should be turned into a test that greps for the literal string `'op_project_id'` outside `packages/constants`.

### C22. `05-logs.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** The logs stream rejects the log ingest architecture that three other streams have already specified in detail. 05-logs L1 — "apps/api decodes OTLP itself and pushes **Loki JSON**; it never forwards OTLP to gigapipe" — is well argued (gigapipe's OTLP log decoder turns every attribute including `trace_id` into a stream label, one fingerprint per trace id, no configuration turns it off). But 01-tenancy §3.1 routes `POST /telemetry/v1/logs` → `POST {GIGAPIPE_URL}/v1/logs`, builds `enforceOtlpLogs`, and calls the record-attribute-overwrite problem "the single highest-risk ingest behaviour in this document" with tests T1.14–T1.17 and the alias matrix T1.15 "the single highest-value test in the suite". 02-ingest §4.2 specifies the same forwarding with a different stamping rule. 04-read-path §2.2 states it as a contract ingest "must guarantee". If L1 ships, none of that code path exists: the OTLP-logs protobuf encoder, the record-level stamping, the record-attribute regression tests and a meaningful chunk of 01's and 02's effort estimates are all dead. If L1 does not ship, 05's entire §4 ingest design, its envelope, its label allowlist and its `detected_fields` computation are dead. The three streams also disagree on the OTLP-logs stamping *level* independently of this: 01 D3 stamps resource only, 02 §4.2 stamps resource and record, 04 §2.2 requires resource and scope and record.

**Fix.** 05's argument is the strongest of the four and should win — the unbounded-fingerprint problem it identifies is real and has no configuration remedy. Adopt L1 in the blueprint, then delete the OTLP-logs forwarding path from 01 §3.1/§3.6 and 02 §4.2, delete the OTLP-logs protobuf encode requirement from both effort estimates (01's largest single line item is "vendoring OTLP `.proto` + protobufjs codecs for metrics/logs/traces", 8 days), and rewrite 04 §2.2's logs row as the Loki-push label contract. Note that this *increases* 01's importance rather than decreasing it: on the Loki push path `sanitizeLabels` truncates label values at 100 bytes and never de-duplicates, so 01's duplicate-label analysis and its `sanitizeLabelName` port become the primary log-path controls.

### C23. `05-logs.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** P1 is specified to ship a log ingest path that P3 forbids, and the resulting stored data is not repairable. Doc 02 (P1) routes `POST /telemetry/v1/logs` to gigapipe's `/v1/logs` as mutated OTLP protobuf; doc 01 §3.1 tables the same forward. Doc 05 L1 rejects that outright with a sound argument from source: gigapipe's OTLP log decoder turns every resource, scope **and record** attribute into a stream label including `trace_id` and `span_id` (`writer/utils/unmarshal/otlplogs.go:39-62`), so one trace id is one new fingerprint — "at 10k lines/s that is 10k new series/s", with no configuration that turns it off. Doc 05 therefore mandates that apps/api decode OTLP itself and push **Loki JSON** with a fixed envelope (L2) and a closed label allowlist (L3). Because `op_project_id` and every other label participate in the fingerprint (doc 01 D11, `writer/utils/unmarshal/unmarshal.go:250-270`), anything ingested under doc 02's design is stored under a key that cannot be rewritten in place — only re-ingested.

**Fix.** Remove log ingest from P1 entirely and move it into P3 as one stream that owns the write path and the read path together (doc 05 already sequences it this way: P3.1 compiler/envelope → P3.3 Loki push → P3.4 OTLP decode). P1 ships metrics ingest only. Amend doc 02's route table and §4 and doc 01 §3.1 to match, and delete the `/telemetry/v1/logs` → `/v1/logs` forward from both. This also removes doc 05 P3.4's OTLP-JSON normaliser conflict with doc 02 D8 (which rejects OTLP/JSON with a 415).

### C24. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** `02-ingest-gateway.md` D16 states the opposite of what the code does, and the control it claims does not exist. Verified at `apps/api/src/app.ts:109-125`: membership in `corsPaths` is the *restricted* branch (`{ origin: <dashboardOrigin>, credentials: true }`); everything else falls through to `return callback(null, { origin: '*', maxAge: 86_400 * 7 })`. D16 says "`/telemetry` is added to `corsPaths` **so a browser cannot reach it cross-origin**" — adding it makes the dashboard origin an *allowed* cross-origin caller with credentials, and omitting it makes `/telemetry` reachable from every origin on the internet, exactly like `/track`. `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §6 cites D16 as a settled control ("`/telemetry` added to `corsPaths` (D16)"). `05-logs.md` D11 independently catches this and proposes the correct third branch, but 05 and 02 are describing different mechanisms for the same route, and D16 is the one the tenancy document depends on. The practical exposure: the telemetry secret is the sole input to `op_project_id`, and the stated reason for the CORS control is to stop customers putting it in front-end JavaScript — under either of 02's readings there is no such backstop.

**Fix.** Adopt `05-logs.md` D11 verbatim: a third `corsDeniedPaths = ['/telemetry']` branch returning `{ origin: false }`, evaluated before the `corsPaths` check. Rewrite `02-ingest-gateway.md` D16 to say membership in `corsPaths` is *permissive-for-the-dashboard*, not a deny. Fix the `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §6 reference. Keep 05's router test asserting `OPTIONS /telemetry/v1/logs` with an arbitrary `Origin` is rejected while `/track` still gets `origin: '*'`.

### C25. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** The reserved-attribute predicate has two incompatible definitions, and doc 01's version deletes the plan's own differentiator. 01 §2: `function isReserved(name) { return name === TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL || name.startsWith(TELEMETRY_RESERVED_LABEL_PREFIX) }` with `TELEMETRY_RESERVED_LABEL_PREFIX = 'op_'`, and its worked table explicitly says "`op.latency` is dropped. That is the reserved namespace working as intended". 02 §4.0 instead uses an exact 3-member set: `OP_TELEMETRY_RESERVED_LABELS = new Set([OP_PROJECT_LABEL, OP_ORGANIZATION_LABEL, '__ttl_days__'])`. 06 §2.1 depends on the exact-set semantics: `const CLIENT_ALLOWED_SPAN_ONLY = new Set(['op_session_id','op_profile_id'])` … "Span-level `op_session_id` survives" (its test #5). Under 01's prefix rule, `enforceLabelPairs`/`isReservedAttributeKey` strips `op_session_id` and `op_profile_id` from every span, so the session→trace correlation lane in 06 §9.1 returns zero rows forever, silently. 01 §4.2 compounds it: `zMetricLabelFilter`'s `.refine((f) => notReserved(f.name))` makes it impossible for any user or UI to filter on `op_session_id` either. 05 §5.1 adds a third set, `RESERVED_LOG_LABELS = ['op_project_id','source','level']`.

**Fix.** Adopt 02's exact-set semantics as the single definition, and extend it with the trace correlation carve-out from 06: a `GATEWAY_OWNED` set (`op_project_id`, `op_organization_id`, `__ttl_days__`) stripped everywhere unconditionally, plus a `CLIENT_ALLOWED_SPAN_ONLY` set (`op_session_id`, `op_profile_id`) permitted on span attributes only and stripped at resource/scope level. Delete the `op_`-prefix rule from 01 §2 and the prefix refine from 01 §4.2's `zMetricLabelFilter`/`groupBy`. Keep 01's genuinely correct contribution — comparing the *sanitized* key under all three of gigapipe's transforms — but apply it to set membership rather than to a prefix. Fold 05's `RESERVED_LOG_LABELS` into the same module as a signal-specific extension, not a fourth list.

### C26. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `05-logs.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** P1 opens ingest routes for all three OTLP signals while the per-signal shaping rules for two of them are specified and costed in P3 and P4, and the resulting rows cannot be repaired. 02 §1's P1 table accepts `/telemetry/v1/logs` and `/telemetry/v1/traces`. But 06 T11 (the gateway strips `op_session_id`/`op_profile_id` from metric data-point attributes, metric resource attributes and log record attributes), T12 (the gateway zeroes `LogRecord.SpanId`, explicitly 'a requirement on the logs work-stream'), and T17 (correlation ids kept only on a span with empty `ParentSpanId` or marked `op_root`, capped at `TELEMETRY_MAX_CORRELATED_SPANS_PER_TRACE = 4`) are all gateway rules costed inside 06's P4 total. Verified: `op_session_id`, `op_profile_id`, `op_root` and `MAX_CORRELATED` appear **zero times** in 02-ingest-gateway.md. Meanwhile 01's Effort states a mis-stamped series 'is not repairable, only deletable', and 06 T21 states a span written without the project label is 'permanently invisible to every read **and** permanently undeletable by erasure and by project deletion'.

**Fix.** Pick one of two shapes and write it down. Either (a) pull 06's T11/T12/T17 rewrite rules into 02 §6 so they ship in P1 alongside `op_project_id` — adding ~2–3 d to P1a and removing the same row from 06's P4 total; or (b) restrict P1's route table to `/telemetry/v1/metrics` and `/telemetry/api/v1/write`, and let the logs and traces routes be opened by the phases that own their shaping. (b) is cheaper and matches the log-protocol resolution.

### C27. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** Adding `ClientType.telemetry` grants full analytics write access, and three of the four specs that enumerate the required fixes miss the one that matters. I verified `validateSdkRequest` (apps/api/src/utils/auth.ts:42-176) reads `client.type` **nowhere** — grep of lines 42-176 returns only `ignoreCorsAndSecret` and `project.cors` checks. It is the hook behind `POST /track`, `POST /event` and `POST /profile`. A telemetry credential lives in a collector config on every host a customer runs, is pasted into public docs (02 §13.1-13.4), and is the credential most likely to leak — and it will be able to forge analytics events for the project. Doc 01 §3.2 lists exactly four call sites (auth.ts:202, :237, mcp auth.ts:99, export.controller.ts:28) and omits it. Doc 05 §4.2's table lists the same four and omits it. Doc 08 §2 mentions it in a trailing paragraph but excludes it from "The five edits that must land in the same PR". Only doc 02 §2.2 lists it as required, and it is the doc least likely to be read by whoever writes the migration. Doc 08 makes this worse with its documented rollback: `UPDATE "clients" SET "type" = 'read' WHERE "type" = 'telemetry';` — I verified that turns every leaked ingest token into a credential that passes `validateExportRequest` (auth.ts:202 only denies `write`), `validateImportRequest` (:237, same), and `packages/mcp/src/auth.ts:99`, i.e. a full analytics **read** credential across MCP and export.

**Fix.** Make the ClientType conversion checklist identical in all four documents and add `validateSdkRequest` as item 1 with the note that it is the only one that grants write. State it as an allow-list: `if (client.type !== ClientType.write && client.type !== ClientType.root) throw`. Replace doc 08's rollback with `DELETE FROM clients WHERE type = 'telemetry'` (revocation, not demotion) and say why demotion to `read` is a privilege escalation. Add one test asserting a `telemetry` client is rejected by `/track`, `/event`, `/profile`, export, import, manage, MCP, and `export.controller`'s project scope — doc 02's test 4 is the model; docs 01 (T1.54), 05 and 08 should reference it rather than restate a shorter list.

### C28. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** Two mutually exclusive log-ingest architectures are specified for the same route, with different tenancy-enforcement surfaces and no shared owner. `02-ingest-gateway.md` §1/§6.2 rewrites the OTLP protobuf in place (strip `op_*` at resource+scope+record, stamp the resource) and forwards to gigapipe `POST /v1/logs`. `05-logs.md` D1/D2 rejects that outright, decodes OTLP itself, discards gigapipe's OTLP label model entirely, builds a closed allowlisted label set plus a JSON envelope, and pushes Loki JSON to `POST /loki/api/v1/push`. `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §4.4/§4.8 and the whole E12–E15 block of `11-testing-strategy.md` §3.2 are written against the 02 design — E12 is called "the single highest-value test in the document" and tests a record-attribute override that does not exist under 05's design. Conversely 05's actual enforcement points (`sanitizeAttrKey`, the envelope's reserved-key deletion at rule 4, the label allowlist) have zero test rows anywhere. Worse, 05 exposes `POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` to customers, which 02 explicitly refuses — and 02's refusal is the sole basis for the `type ∈ {1,2}` invariant that `08-schema-changes.md` S13/D10 and `10-ops-retention-billing.md` D10 build the per-signal TTL totality argument on ("That is a constraint this gateway must *enforce*, not assume"). A naive merge (02's route table + 05's `pushLogs`) applies 01's strip rules to a payload that is then thrown away and rebuilt, so the boundary is enforced in a place nobody specified or tested.

**Fix.** Pick one log write path before any P1 code is written, and name the owner. If 05's Loki-JSON design wins: retarget `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §4.4/§4.8's logs row to the constructed-stream model, replace `11-testing-strategy.md` E12–E15/E22–E24 with rows against `buildEnvelope`/`sanitizeAttrKey`/the label allowlist, and either delete 05's customer-facing `/telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` route or re-derive 08 S13's type-0 argument without the "gateway does not expose Loki push" premise. If 02's forward-in-place design wins: delete 05 D1–D3 and §3–§4.5 and re-cost 05's cardinality story against gigapipe's native OTLP label mapping.

### C29. `05-logs.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The log ingest path is specified two incompatible ways, and the downstream UI reads the wrong shape. 05 L1: "apps/api decodes OTLP itself and pushes **Loki JSON**; it never forwards OTLP to gigapipe" — gigapipe's OTLP log decoder turns every attribute into a stream label, "At 10k lines/s that is 10k new series/s". 01 §3.1 and 02 §1 both route `POST /telemetry/v1/logs` → `POST {GIGAPIPE_URL}/v1/logs`, and 02 §4.2 specifies per-record `op_project_id` stamping inside the forwarded OTLP payload; 11 §11.1 pins `/v1/logs` protobuf-only as the ingest path and §11.3 B2 asserts gigapipe's log-record-attribute precedence, a fact that is unreachable under 05. Downstream, 09 §6.1 reads correlation ids off stream labels — `log.labels.trace_id`, `log.labels.session_id` — while 05 §3.3 rule 3 puts `trace_id`, `span_id`, `session_id`, `profile_id` on a "reserved-deny list" that can never be a label, carrying them instead inside the JSON envelope (`tid`/`sid`/`attr.session_id`), surfaced as top-level `IServiceLogLine.traceId`/`spanId` fields (05 §6.5). 09's guards are therefore permanently falsy — 09's own failure-mode #5 describes exactly this bug for a different reason.

**Fix.** 05 owns the log data model and its cardinality argument is the strongest in the set; adopt L1. Rewrite 01 §3.1's route table row and 02 §1's `/telemetry/v1/logs` forwarding target to `POST {GIGAPIPE_URL}/loki/api/v1/push`, delete 02 §4.2's per-record OTLP stamping rule (the label set is constructed by us, so there is nothing to strip-and-stamp in a forwarded payload), and re-scope 11 §11.1's `/v1/logs` row and §11.3 B2 to "pin gigapipe's behaviour we deliberately do not use". Rewrite 09 §6.1's correlation block to read `log.traceId`/`log.sessionId`/`log.profileId` from 05 §6.5's `IServiceLogLine`, and delete 09's `logCorrelationId(labels, key)` helper — it guards against a spelling that cannot occur.

### C30. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The telemetry auth verification cache is specified three incompatible ways, and `02-ingest-gateway.md`'s version grants a rotated secret for up to five minutes. `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §6.1 keys on `telemetry:auth:${clientId}` alone, puts `{hash, digest}` in the *value*, and on every cache hit compares `entry.hash !== client.secret || entry.digest !== presented` before falling through to a full argon2 verify — explicitly rejecting digest-in-key because "no code path can reconstruct the key without the old plaintext, so nothing can clear it". `02-ingest-gateway.md` §2.2 does exactly what 01 rejects: key `telemetry:auth:${clientId}:${secretHash(clientSecret)}`, and on a hit it does `if (verifyCache.get(key)) return client` with no comparison against `client.secret`. Its justification — "a rotated secret misses it" — is inverted: the *new* secret misses, the *old* one hits its own entry and is granted for the 300 s Redis TTL and the 5-minute process LRU. `05-logs.md` §4.2 specifies a third variant and publishes 5 minutes as "the documented revocation SLA of a telemetry credential". `11-testing-strategy.md` A18 correctly separates the two SLAs (≤60 s for client deletion via `getClientByIdCached`, up to 5 min for secret rotation) and A17 flags that today's `apps/api/src/utils/auth.ts:165` builds `client:auth:${clientId}:${Buffer.from(clientSecret).toString('base64')}` — verified: a reversible plaintext ingest credential in the Redis keyspace, visible in `SCAN`, slowlog and RDB dumps.

**Fix.** Adopt `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §6.1's shape as the single specification and delete the code samples in `02-ingest-gateway.md` §2.2 and `05-logs.md` §4.2: key on the client id alone, store the stored hash and the presented digest in the value, re-verify on every hit when either differs, TTL 60 s, and export `clearTelemetryAuth(clientId)` called from `manage.controller.ts` and from tRPC `client.remove`. Name one owner for A17's `client:authv2:` migration of the existing SDK path — `11-testing-strategy.md` assigns it to "the ingest work-stream" but `02-ingest-gateway.md` never touches `validateSdkRequest`.

### C31. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** The reserved-prefix strip destroys the plan's own differentiator, and only one of the three specs notices. 01 D2 reserves the whole `op_` prefix and §5's `enforceLabelPairs` removes every key where `isReservedKey` is true; 02 §6.0's `isReserved` is `k.startsWith('op_') || sanitizeOtlpKey(k).startsWith('op_') || sanitizePromLabel(k).startsWith('op_')` and `scrubAttrs` drops every such attribute unconditionally, on every signal, with no restore hook. 06 T4 names the correlation keys `op_session_id` and `op_profile_id` and additionally introduces `op_root`, `op_exception_type`, `op_exception_message` — all of which begin with `op_`. 06 §4.1 handles this with an explicit ordered "snapshot then strip, then restore, bounded" sequence, but neither 01 §5 nor 02 §6.0/§6.3 mentions a restore step or a whitelist within the reserved namespace. If 02's `scrubAttrs` ships as written, every trace loses its session and profile correlation — the one thing the plan says no Grafana stack can do — silently.

**Fix.** 01 must add the exception to D2/D7 explicitly: the reserved namespace is stripped from *client-supplied* attributes, but a named, closed set (`op_session_id`, `op_profile_id`, `op_root`, `op_exception_*`) is re-attached by the gateway from the snapshot, at the levels 06 §4.1 step 2 specifies, subject to 06 T17's per-trace cap. 02 §6.0 must carry the same list and `scrubAttrs` must take the snapshot before it strips. Add a test to 11 §3.2 asserting a span arrives with `op_session_id` intact and a *log record* arrives with it removed (06 T11), because the two signals differ.

### C32. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** 01-tenancy's `ClientType.telemetry` allow-list checklist is incomplete in the one way that matters, and its omission is a privilege escalation. 01 §3.2 tables four sites (`auth.ts:202`, `auth.ts:237`, `packages/mcp/src/auth.ts:99`, `export.controller.ts:28`) and states that `validateManageRequest` "is already allow-list-shaped and needs **no** change". It never mentions `validateSdkRequest`. I verified: `grep -n "client.type|ClientType" apps/api/src/utils/auth.ts` returns exactly four lines — 3, 202, 237, 272 — and `validateSdkRequest` spans lines 42–176. It has **no `client.type` check at all**. It is the hook behind `POST /track`, `POST /event` and `POST /profile`. So adding `telemetry` to the enum grants every telemetry credential full analytics-event write access — and a telemetry credential lives in a collector config on every host a customer runs, which is a far wider blast radius than a browser SDK key. 02-ingest §2.2 catches this and calls it "the widening that matters most"; 08-schema D3 references "four check conversions" and says reviewer 1 correctly identified that the fourth "is not a deny-list at all". Nothing fails to compile and nothing fails a test — `apps/api/src/utils/auth.ts` has no test file, and the only test pinning `ClientType` mocks it as a literal object.

**Fix.** Add `validateSdkRequest` to 01 §3.2's table as row 0 with the required change (`reject anything that is not write/root`) and to test T1.54's assertion list. Make the blueprint state that the enum migration and all five conversions are one PR with a merge gate, per 08 D3, and that the deploy must complete before any `type='telemetry'` row exists — an old pod running the previous code fails open on every one of them, and a Postgres enum value cannot be dropped, so rollback is code-only.

### C33. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse` — a GDPR Art. 17 obligation and, per 01's own runbook, the prerequisite for step 4 of every breach remediation — is owned by three documents with three incompatible implementations, and the one that claims the guarantee misses a call site. 01 §6 issues chained subqueries (`ALTER TABLE … DELETE WHERE fingerprint IN (SELECT fingerprint FROM time_series_gin …)`) with `mutations_sync: '0'`. 08 D12 explicitly rejects that shape: "Project deletion materialises id sets in Node; it does not chain subqueries". 10 §11/P2 specifies a third shape (partition-scoped `ALTER … DELETE` with erasure ordering). 08's objection is correct and stronger than it states: OpenPanel sets `distributed_product_mode: 'allow'` globally (verified, packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts:114), so on a clustered deployment a plain `IN` subquery resolves per-shard and silently drops fingerprints whose gin row lives elsewhere — a deletion that reports success and leaves data. Separately, `deleteFromClickhouse` has **two** call sites, which I verified: `apps/worker/src/jobs/cron.delete.ts:46` and `admin/src/commands/delete-organization.ts:191`. 01 D10 wires only the cron. 10 correctly identifies the second as "the interactive support tool a GDPR erasure actually travels through". So as specified in 01, a support-driven erasure deletes analytics and leaves every metric, log line and span — including the `session_id`/`profile_id` labels that are the correlation feature, i.e. exactly the PII the request is about.

**Fix.** One owner (08, which owns persistence), one implementation, two call sites. Use 08's materialise-in-Node shape, add 10's erasure ordering and `TelemetryPurge` resumability from 01, and add `admin/src/commands/delete-organization.ts:191` to the wiring in all three documents. Add `GLOBAL IN` handling per 04 §2.4 or drop the subquery form entirely. Make 11-testing's D9 teardown call the single function, as it already specifies. Keep 09 D13's rule that the UI must not ship before this exists.

### C34. `04-read-path.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Four different tRPC router names for the same surface, and two architecturally opposed dispatch models. Names: 01 §4.1 `packages/trpc/src/routers/metrics.ts` with `metricsProcedure`; 03 §Interfaces the same file with `metricNames`/`metricLabels`/`labelValues` on `protectedProcedure`; 04 §6 `packages/trpc/src/routers/observability.ts` mounted as `observability` with `observabilityProcedure`; 05 §6.2 `packages/trpc/src/routers/logs.ts` mounted as `logs`; 09 §Interfaces `packages/trpc/src/routers/telemetry.ts` mounted as `telemetry` with `telemetry.metrics.*`/`telemetry.logs.*`/`telemetry.traces.*`. Dispatch: 03 D4/§13.3 routes metric charts *through* `chart.chart`/`chart.aggregate` via one `executeReport(input, {mode})`, and 08 §3 and 09 §5 and 11 §5 all build on that (11 tests `packages/trpc/src/routers/chart.test.ts` for "a `dataSource: 'metrics'` report dispatches to `MetricsChartEngine`"). 04 D6 explicitly rejects it — "Ship as a sibling `observability.*` router, not as a branch inside `chart.chart`" — and 09's own interface list contradicts itself, demanding both `telemetry.*` procedures and (item 3) "Server-side dispatch on `dataSource` inside **both** `chart.chart` and `chart.aggregate`".

**Fix.** Two decisions, taken together. (a) Metric *reports* dispatch inside `chart.chart`/`chart.aggregate` via 03's `executeReport(input, {mode})` — 04 D6's objection (the migration cost of `dataSource`) is work 08 and 09 are already committed to, and without it 09 §5's "seven renderers unchanged" payoff evaporates, which 09 itself calls "the single most important sentence in the API spec". (b) Everything that is *not* a saved report — metric/label/trace/log browsing, the log explorer, trace search, status/capabilities, live tail — lives in one new router. Pick `telemetry` (09's name, and it matches `ClientType.telemetry`, the `/telemetry` ingest prefix and the `TELEMETRY_*` env family) and rewrite 04 §6, 01 §4.1, 03 §Interfaces and 05 §6.2 onto it. 04 D6 must be rewritten from "reject" to "defer the metric-report branch until 08's migration lands".

### C35. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Three incompatible homes are specified for the gigapipe HTTP client, and each doc believes its own is the shared one. Doc 04 D1/§3 puts it in a new `packages/gigapipe` with an explicit rule "Nothing from `@openpanel/db`", and gates every socket write behind an enforcement function (D2) that refuses to open a connection for an unverified selector-bearing request. Doc 03 §1 puts it in `packages/db/src/gigapipe/client.ts` and states at §Interfaces that P3 and P4 "add methods, not a second client". Doc 05 uses `packages/db/src/gigapipe/` 22 times. Doc 01 §3.1 specifies `packages/gigapipe/src/client.ts`, which doc 04 D1 explicitly deletes. Doc 02 D9 puts the ingest codecs in `apps/api/src/telemetry/` instead. Doc 04's Q8 flags this as "the highest-priority open question" but frames it as a two-party disagreement with the tenancy stream only — it is in fact four-party. If docs 03 and 04 both ship, there are two gigapipe clients with two error hierarchies and two circuit breakers, and doc 04's enforcement gate — the mechanism that makes label injection a boundary rather than a convention — does not cover doc 03's client at all.

**Fix.** Settle the package layout in the blueprint before a line of P1 or P2 code is written; it is a one-meeting decision now and a rewrite in six weeks. Recommended resolution: adopt doc 04's `packages/gigapipe` with the layer split (tenancy owns `src/labels.ts`, `src/query/*`, `src/routes.ts`; read-path owns transport/errors/types/lease/read), because it is the only proposal in which the verifier and the socket write live behind one function. Then amend doc 03 §1 and §Interfaces and doc 05's 22 references to import from it, and amend doc 01 §3.1 to drop `src/client.ts`. Doc 02's `apps/api/src/telemetry/` may keep the Fastify plumbing but the protobuf codecs and the strip/stamp primitives must be the same modules the read path and the tests use.

### C36. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `07-alerting.md`

**Problem.** The response-side detective control that doc 01 declares mandatory does not exist in the spec that implements the first read path, and if it were implemented as written it would break every chart. Doc 01 D7 requires the compiler to append `op_project_id` to every `by (…)` list and never emit `without`, so `verifyResponseLabels` (§5) applies to every response; §Interfaces marks it "Mandatory on every proxied read response". Doc 03 §4.2's compilation table emits `sum by (a, b) (...)` with no `op_project_id`, §9's matrix-to-`ConcreteSeries` never calls a verifier, and `verifyResponseLabels` appears nowhere in the document. Doc 07 D2 requires the label in `by (...)` only in `mode: 'alerting'` — a mode doc 03 does not define. Doc 04's enforcement gate (§4.6) verifies the outbound query string and explicitly does not inspect responses. Net effect: on the P2 metrics path there is no response check at all; and if someone lands doc 01's `verifyResponseLabels` against doc 03's compiler, every grouped chart throws `verify_failed`, because PromQL aggregation output carries exactly the `by (...)` labels — doc 07 D2 derives this correctly from reader/controller/prom_query_range.go:312-336 and is the only doc that does. This is the control the plan relies on to catch F1/F2 and any compiler regression, and it is unimplementable as currently split.

**Fix.** Pick doc 07 D2's mechanism and make it unconditional, not a mode: `compileMetricQuery`/`compileAggregation` always append `op_project_id` to `by (...)`, and the engine strips it from the series identity after verification and before `format()` (doc 01 §5 already says this — doc 03 §9 must implement it, and its `metricSeriesId` must exclude the label). Then move `verifyResponseLabels` into doc 03's `fetch.ts` and doc 04's response parser as a named stage, add doc 07's "absence is a contract violation, abort — never drop" rule to it, and add the assertion to doc 11's P2 gate. Doc 03's D10 explicitly defers a fail-closed check to P6 on the grounds that no JS parser exists in P2 — that reasoning does not apply to a response-label check, which needs no parser.

### C37. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** The plan's only *runtime* detector for a tenancy bypass is specified out of existence by the engine that would trigger it. 01-tenancy D7 makes it a compiler invariant that "whenever the metrics compiler emits an aggregation, the tenancy label is appended to the `by (…)` list; `without` is never emitted", and §5's `verifyResponseLabels` is then applied to *every* response — 01 explicitly rejects a relaxed "aggregated" mode on the grounds that it would detect nothing, and pins the invariant with test T1.32. 07-alerting D2 independently rediscovers the same requirement and adopts it, but scopes it to "in alerting mode". 03-metrics, which owns the compiler, never emits it: §4.2's `<agg>` expands to `sum(...)` with no `by` clause when `groupBy` is empty and to `sum by (a, b)` otherwise, and every one of §19's four worked examples confirms it (`sum(rate(http_server_requests_total{op_project_id="op-demo",job="api"}[2h]))`, `max by (instance) (...)`). So as specified, `verifyResponseLabels` either throws on 100% of chart responses (fail-closed outage) or is quietly downgraded to the vacuous mode 01 rejected — at which point F1 and F2, the two fail-open modes 01 ranks first and second, have no runtime detection at all and rest entirely on the route-key client compiling correctly.

**Fix.** Decide in the blueprint and implement in one place. Adopting 01's invariant is right and cheap — the tenancy label is constant within a project, so it adds one grouping column of cardinality 1 and does not change gigapipe's aggregation pushdown cost — but it is not free of consequences 03 must handle: the label lands in the returned series identity and must be stripped after verification and before `format()`, before it reaches series naming, the breakdown picker, the legend and any share link. Add that stripping step to 03 §9 (matrix → ConcreteSeries), add the `by` clause to 03 §4.2's `<agg>` expansion and all of §19's examples, and drop 07's "in alerting mode" qualifier so there is one compiler behaviour.

### C38. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** 01 mandates a compiler invariant that 03 does not implement and 07 requests as an opt-in mode, and 01's runtime verifier rejects 03's output. 01 D7: "whenever the metrics compiler emits an aggregation, the tenancy label is appended to the `by (…)` list; `without` is never emitted", and 01 §5's `verifyResponseLabels` throws `TenancyError('verify_failed')` for any returned series where `s.metric[TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL] !== projectId` — "This applies to EVERY response because the compiler always emits `by (..., op_project_id)`". 03 §4.2 emits `sum by (a, b) (...)` with no `op_project_id` in the by-list, and `sum(...)` with no `by` clause at all when `groupBy` is empty; `op_project_id` appears nowhere in 03's aggregation rendering. PromQL drops every label not in `by`, so under 03's compiler every aggregated response fails 01's verifier — every metric chart 500s. 07 D2 independently discovered the same gap and asks 03 for `compileMetricQuery(..., { mode: 'alerting' })` to add it, listing "Will P2 accept `mode: 'alerting'`?" as an open question 03 does not answer. 04 §4.6 relies on `assertEnforced`, which checks selectors, not aggregation output, so it neither catches nor contradicts this.

**Fix.** Make it unconditional in 03, not a mode. Add `op_project_id` to the `by (...)` list of every aggregation 03 §4.2 emits (including the currently-bare `sum(...)` case, which becomes `sum by (op_project_id) (...)`), and have 03 §9's matrix→ConcreteSeries stage strip the label from the series identity after 01 §5's verification and before `format()`. That satisfies 01 D7 and 07 D2 with one rule, deletes 07's open question, and makes 01's strict verifier universally applicable as designed. Add the assertion to 03's golden compile table (§Test requirements item 4) and to 11 §3.4 row 8.

### C39. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `07-alerting.md`

**Problem.** Interfaces three specs import as hard dependencies are not in the owning spec's exposed table, and one of them has a different name and shape. `verifyResponseLabels(series, projectId): void` is consumed by 03 §9.0, 04 §4.4 ("There is **no stub** … if the tenancy layer ships later, `src/read/*.ts` does not compile") and 07 §Interfaces, all citing `01-tenancy-and-security.md:1423-1435` — but 01's own "Exposed by this work-stream" table does not list it at all. Worse, `compileAggregation(q, inner)` is consumed by 03 §4.1 (`aggd = compileAggregation(qWithLe, ranged)`), 07 §Interfaces and 04 §6.2, all citing 01 — but 01 exposes `compileGroupBy(q): string` returning only a `by (…)` fragment, not a function that wraps an expression. 03 additionally asks tenancy to "delete the `quantile` / `topk` / `bottomk` branches" of `compileAggregation` and its `q.k` reads — branches that exist only in 01's schema, which 03 is replacing.

**Fix.** 01 must add `verifyResponseLabels` and `compileAggregation` to its Interfaces table with exact signatures, or the three consumers must be rewritten against `compileGroupBy`. Recommend adding `compileAggregation(q, inner): string` to 01 (03's emission order in §4.1 needs the wrapping form, and putting the wrap in the tenancy layer is what keeps `op_project_id` in `by (…)` by construction) and deleting `compileGroupBy`. Also record 03's requested third parameter on `compileSelector(q, projectId, extraMatchers?)` and 07's requested `compileAlertQuery` in 01 and 03 respectively — both are currently requests with no acceptance.

### C40. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `04-read-path.md`
