**Problem.** `zMetricQuery` is defined four incompatible ways and every downstream spec compiles against a different one. 03 §2 (the owning spec): `{ type:'metric', metric, metricType: z.enum(['counter','gauge','histogram']) /* required, no default */, fn: 5 members, aggregation: 4 members, matchers: [{name, op: '='|'!='|'=~'|'!~', value}], groupBy, quantile, window, fill, seriesLimit, hideSeries }`. 01 §4.2: `{ metric, filters: [{name, operator: 'eq'|'neq'|'re'|'nre', value}], fn: 12 members incl. `irate`/`deriv`/`delta`, window: a 7-literal enum, aggregation: 8 members incl. `quantile`/`topk`/`bottomk`, groupBy, quantile, k }` — no `metricType`, no `type` discriminator. 09 D2: `zChartMetricItem = { type:'metric', name /* not `metric` */, filters: zChartEventFilter[], fn: 'none'|'rate'|'increase'|'delta', aggregation: sum/avg/min/max/count/p50/p90/p95/p99, groupBy }` — no `metricType`, no `seriesLimit`. 07 §7's worked example uses `{ metric, aggregation: 'rate_sum', window, groupBy, filters:[{label, operator, value}] }` — a fifth field name (`label`) and an aggregation value that exists nowhere. 04 §5.1 already notices the collision and resolves it the wrong way: it "withdraws" its own signature and adopts 01's `eq|neq|re|nre` alphabet, i.e. it binds to the non-owning spec.

**Fix.** 03 owns `zMetricQuery` (it is the only spec that derives the field set from the compilation table and from `format()`'s requirements) — declare that explicitly and make every other doc reference it rather than restate it. Concretely: delete the schema bodies from 01 §4.2, 09 D2 and 07 §2's `query:` field and replace each with `query: zMetricQuery` + a one-line note on what that stream constrains. 01's compilers must be re-specified against 03's field names (`matchers` not `filters`, `op` not `operator`, `=`/`!=`/`=~`/`!~` not `eq`/`neq`/`re`/`nre`) and against a required `metricType`. 04 §5.1's "adopted verbatim from the tenancy work-stream" paragraph must be re-pointed at 03. 07 §7's worked example must be rewritten with a real `aggregation` value.

### C41. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** Nothing owns "metric reports on the public share path", and the guarantee that telemetry is never share-reachable evaporates the moment `Report.dataSource` ships. `01-tenancy-and-security.md` D12/§7.1 and `04-read-path.md` D4/§7.2 put the guarantee in `observabilityProcedure`, which rejects `shareId` outright — but that only covers the `observability.*` router. `09-ui-surfaces.md` D5 routes *saved* metric reports through `chart.chart`, i.e. `chartProcedure`, a `publicProcedure` that serves anonymous viewers whenever `shareId` is present, and `03-metrics-engine.md` §15 (P2.5) makes that the plan of record. `03-metrics-engine.md` §12.5 states the prerequisite plainly — "P2.5 cannot ship the share path without `rateLimitMiddleware` on the metric procedure, keyed on `shareId` plus IP. That is a hard prerequisite" — and `09-ui-surfaces.md` §6, which specifies the share behaviour, does not list it; it requires only a wind-down/quota check. Verified: `packages/trpc/src/trpc.ts:206-211` keys the 60 s cache on `trpc:${path}:` + `JSON.stringify(getRawInput())` with no user component, and 09 §6 confirms `chart.chart` merges caller-supplied `range`/`startDate`/`endDate`/`interval` over `ctx.report`. So one public share link gives an unauthenticated caller window control, cache-key control, and an unmetered, unrate-limited query generator against a single Go process whose PromQL engine has a hardcoded non-configurable 30 s timeout and one global `MaxSamples` budget shared across every tenant (`reader/router/prometheus_query_range.go:31-32`). That is cross-tenant denial of service requiring no credential.

**Fix.** Assign one owner for the metric-report share path before `Report.dataSource` lands. Require, in that owner's document: (a) `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §7.1's four-field window allow-list (`range`, `startDate`, `endDate`, `interval`) with an explicit `isMetricReport(ctx.report)` rejection and no fallback to `input.series`; (b) `rateLimitMiddleware` keyed on `shareId` + trusted IP on every share-served chart procedure, not only the metric one; (c) a separate `withProjectLease` bucket for share traffic, as `07-alerting.md` D15 does for alerts, so anonymous readers cannot consume the authenticated budget; (d) a Tier-1 test that a share link cannot vary `series`, `filters` or `breakdowns`.

### C42. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Three incompatible `zMetricQuery` definitions are on disk and a fourth is implied by the tests. 01 §7.2 defines `{metric, filters:[{name,operator:'eq'|'neq'|'re'|'nre',value}], fn:(11 values incl. irate/deriv), window:(7 values), aggregation:(incl. quantile/topk/bottomk), groupBy, quantile, k}`. 03 §2 defines `{type,id,metric,metricType(4 values incl. summary),filters,fn:(11 different values incl. last_over_time, no irate/deriv),window:(11 values incl. auto),aggregation:(sum|avg|min|max|count),groupBy,quantile,scale,displayName,hideSeries}` and declares itself the owner. 09 D3 claims to consume 03 "verbatim" and then describes a third shape: "`metricType` (required, `counter|gauge|histogram`), `fn` from `{rate, increase, value, avg_over_time, histogram_quantile}`, `matchers: zMetricMatcher[]` with `op` in `{=, !=, =~, !~}` … `fill`, `seriesLimit` (1–200, default 20)". 11 §3.4 Q1 tests `zMetricMatcher`, which exists only in 09's version. 07 Q1 already flags two of these as blocking ("This blocks §1, §3, §9.2 and §12") but does not know about 09's.

**Fix.** 03 owns it and its version wins (it is the only one that removed `topk`/`bottomk` from `aggregation`, which D4/D8 depend on). Rewrite 01 §7.2's schema block to a pointer at 03 §2 rather than a second definition — 01 should keep only the reserved-prefix refinements. Rewrite 09 D3 field-for-field against 03 §2, and with it 09's nuqs key table (§4.2 has `mt` enumerating three metricTypes and `max` for a non-existent `seriesLimit`). Change 11 Q1–Q3 from `zMetricMatcher` to `zMetricLabelFilter`. Close 07 Q1 in the same PR.

### C43. `08-schema-changes.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** 08 names gigapipe's ClickHouse database differently from every other doc, and uses a different env var to configure it. 08 §9: `export const TELEMETRY_DATABASE = process.env.CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_DB || 'openpanel_telemetry'`, and every SQL statement in 08 §11 and §12 is written `openpanel_telemetry.samples_v3`. Everyone else uses `gigapipe`: 01 §6 `process.env.GIGAPIPE_CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE ?? 'gigapipe'`; 04 §2.3 `GIGAPIPE_DB="gigapipe"` plus a stated rule that "no environment variable may appear anywhere in this document that is not in this block. A CI grep enforces it"; 05 §8.2 `CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS gigapipe`; 06 §3 `GIGAPIPE_DB` → `TELEMETRY_DB`; 10 §5 `CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS gigapipe` and compose `CLICKHOUSE_DB: gigapipe`; 11 §8.1 `CLICKHOUSE_DB: gigapipe` and `GIGAPIPE_DB: 'gigapipe'`. Since gigapipe itself reads the name from `CLICKHOUSE_DB` on the container, a mismatch means OpenPanel's retention cron, deletion path and metadata queries all target a database that does not exist — and 08's own `assertTelemetryDatabase()` would throw on every run.

**Fix.** Standardise on `gigapipe` (six docs to one) and on the env var name `GIGAPIPE_DB` (04, 06, 11). Rewrite 08 §9 (`TELEMETRY_DATABASE`, `telemetryUrl()`, `assertTelemetryDatabase`), §11's TTL statements, §12's deletion statements, §10's GRANT block and §13's bring-up sequence. Delete `CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_DB` and `CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_URL` from 08 or re-point them at the `GIGAPIPE_*` family; 01's `GIGAPIPE_CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE` goes too. Note 04's §2.3 completeness rule and its CI grep will fail against 08's names as written.

### C44. `04-read-path.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** Doc 04 §6.3 routes `observability.traces.tagValues` at direct ClickHouse SQL over `tempo_traces_kv`, and that table cannot be project-scoped. I read ctrl/qryn/sql/traces.sql:34-42: the columns are `(oid, date, key, val_id, val)` — no `trace_id`, no `span_id`, no `fingerprint`. There is no column through which an `op_project_id` predicate can reach a row, and `oid` is always `''` (04 §1.6 states this itself). The MV at :44-51 populates it from `tempo_traces_attrs_gin` across all tenants. So a trace tag-value autocomplete served from this table returns every project's `http.route` values, `db.system` values, service names, and — because doc 06 §11 propagates `op_profile_id`, frequently an email — other tenants' user identifiers, to any authenticated member of any project on the instance. This needs no auth trick: it is what the picker does on every keystroke. Both doc 01 §6 ("`tempo_traces_kv` holds de-duplicated `(key, val)` tag pairs with no trace association", and it permanently un-allowlists gigapipe's v1 tag routes for exactly this reason) and doc 06 §1 Fact 4 ("it is **untenanted**") state the fact correctly; doc 04 §9.3.6 shows the correct `tempo_traces_attrs_gin` shape for tag *keys* and then names `tempo_traces_kv` for tag *values* two sections earlier without reconciling them.

**Fix.** Delete `tempo_traces_kv` from doc 04 §6.3 and from any read path. Tag values must come from `tempo_traces_attrs_gin` joined to the project-scoped `(trace_id, span_id)` set, which is exactly the query doc 06 §6 already writes — adopt it verbatim rather than maintaining a second one. Add `tempo_traces_kv` to a named "tables no read path may touch" list in doc 01 alongside the v1 tag routes, with the one-line reason (no tenancy column exists), and add a test asserting no generated SQL in `telemetry-metadata.service.ts` references it.

### C45. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** Three mutually exclusive designs exist for reading traces, and one of them is a leak the other two have already diagnosed. Doc 01 D4 chooses: proxy `GET /api/traces/{traceId}` and filter the decoded spans by `op_project_id`, and explicitly **rejects** "run a scoped TraceQL search for the trace id first and proxy the unscoped by-id fetch if it hits" because trace ids are client-supplied on ingest, so an attacker who ingests one span carrying the victim's trace id makes the scoped search return a hit and the gateway then returns every span of that trace from every project. Doc 04 §6 specifies exactly that rejected design — `observability.traces.get -> two-step: scoped search, then GET /api/traces/{traceId}` — and budgets 2.0 weeks of P4 for it plus `traces.search` over TraceQL. Doc 06 T1/T2 rejects both and goes to direct ClickHouse SQL over `tempo_traces_attrs_gin`/`tempo_traces.payload` (7 days), which doc 01 D4 in turn rejects as contradicting the plan's decision that every read goes through gigapipe, keeping it only as a documented fallback.

**Fix.** Pick one before P4 is scheduled; the security argument in doc 01 D4 rules out doc 04 §6's two-step immediately, so the real choice is doc 01's response-filtering proxy versus doc 06's direct SQL. Doc 06's case is the stronger one on cost (it removes an HTTP hop, gigapipe's hardcoded `LIMIT 2000`, and the truncation-versus-authorisation failure) and the plan's "every read through gigapipe" rule is already broken by doc 04 D4 for metadata, so accept the exception explicitly rather than by drift. Then delete doc 04's P4 line item (2.0 weeks) and its `assertTraceqlScoped` dependency, or delete doc 06 §4/§5 — but not both, and not neither.

### C46. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** `GIGAPIPE_ROUTES` — the object all three documents call "the actual control", the thing that makes unlisted endpoints unreachable by construction — is defined with two different contents. `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §7.7 lists nine read keys the read-path and traces specs have since deleted: `promSeries`, `promLabels`, `promLabelValues`, `lokiLabelValues`, `lokiSeries`, `tempoSearch`, `tempoTagsV2`, `tempoValuesV2` and `tempoTraceById`. `04-read-path.md` D3 removes the Prometheus/Loki metadata routes (naming their `panic(err)` at `query_abels.go:165`, their silent truncation at `:62,:67`, and their hardcoded `Limit: 10000`) in favour of direct ClickHouse, and D11 removes every Tempo route because `GET /api/traces/{traceId}` applies no project predicate at all (`reader/service/tempo.go:53-89`) — "given a trace id it returns another project's spans". `06-traces-and-correlation.md` T1/T2 confirms and states none may appear in `GIGAPIPE_ROUTES`. 01 keeps `tempoTraceById` with a §7.5 ownership pre-check that 04 D11 deletes along with the route. If 01's table is what gets written — and it is the tenancy document's own table — the client can reach the four untenanted endpoints the other two documents exist to keep it away from, guarded by one pre-check whose only home has been removed.

**Fix.** Make `04-read-path.md` §4.1's route table the single normative definition, since it owns `src/routes.ts`'s read keys per `01-tenancy-and-security.md` D11. Replace `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §7.7's block with a pointer plus the ingest keys only, and move its §7.5 trace-ownership SQL into `06-traces-and-correlation.md` §6.3 where the direct-SQL by-id read already lives. Keep `11-testing-strategy.md` Q23/Q24 as the enforcement, and add a test that the two documents' route sets are identical.

### C47. `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** The two documents that jointly own P0 target different ClickHouse databases. 10-ops §2 sets `CLICKHOUSE_DB: gigapipe` on both the `op-gigapipe-init` and `op-gigapipe` containers, and its `gigapipe-provision.sql` (§5) does `CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS gigapipe`. 08-schema opens with "creates the database gigapipe will own (`openpanel_telemetry`)", ships migration C1 as `packages/db/code-migrations/22-telemetry-database.ts` creating `openpanel_telemetry`, and writes every grant (§10), every retention `MODIFY TTL` (§11), the profiling assertion, and the deletion path against `openpanel_telemetry`. 01-tenancy and 04-read-path and 06-traces all assume `gigapipe`. Shipped as written, gigapipe writes to `gigapipe` while OpenPanel's retention cron, GRANT script, profiling assertion and project-deletion routine all operate on an empty `openpanel_telemetry` — silently, with no error, forever. Every one of them is a no-op that reports success.

**Fix.** Pick one name in the blueprint (`openpanel_telemetry` reads better and namespaces the shared instance) and one env var. Delete the other from all five documents. Add a P0 boot assertion that `SELECT count() FROM system.tables WHERE database = <name> AND name = 'samples_v3'` is non-zero before the retention cron is allowed to register, so a future divergence fails loudly instead of silently.

### C48. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The telemetry deletion path — the GDPR gate every stream calls a launch blocker — has a different name, file, statement kind and ordering rule in each spec. Name/signature: 01 §6, 08 §12, 10 §8.6 and 11 §9 all use `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse(projectIds)`; 05 §8.4 uses `purgeTelemetry({projectId, olderThanNs, signals})` and asserts "metrics and traces must extend its `signals` union rather than writing their own purge". File: `packages/db/src/services/telemetry-delete.service.ts` (01, 05) vs `packages/db/src/services/delete.service.ts` (08, 10). Statement kind: `ALTER TABLE … DELETE` (01, 05, 08) vs lightweight `DELETE FROM` (10 §8.6 — "erasure needs rows unreadable *now*", explicitly rebutting 08). Ordering: 01 chains `IN (SELECT … FROM time_series_gin …)` subqueries and deletes the gin tables last; 08 D12 says exactly that is broken ("mutations are asynchronous and unordered … zero rows are deleted, no error is raised, the job logs success") and materialises id lists in Node so order is irrelevant; 10 §8.6 materialises **and** mandates ordering with a bounded `system.mutations` poll between phases. Wiring: 01 and 08 call it from `cron.delete.ts` beside `deleteFromClickhouse`; 10 calls it *from inside* `deleteFromClickhouse` "so there is one place to forget rather than two" and notes the second caller `admin/src/commands/delete-organization.ts:191` that 01 and 08's cron-only wiring misses. 11 §9 row 2 tests 01's ordering rule; 11 §9 row 5 tests 08/10's naming.

**Fix.** Assign a single owner (08, which owns schema, or 10, which owns erasure SLA and both call sites) and write one specification the others reference. The merged correct version is 10's: `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse(projectIds)` in `delete.service.ts`, called from inside `deleteFromClickhouse` guarded on `GIGAPIPE_URL`, using lightweight `DELETE FROM` with materialised id literals (08 D12's correctness argument) *and* the samples-then-wait-then-index ordering with a `system.mutations` poll (10's argument, which 08 D12 misses because materialised literals still leave the index deletable before the samples mutation has read it). Rewrite 01 §6 and 05 §8.4 to consume it; 05's retention use case becomes an optional `olderThanNs`/`signals` argument on the same function rather than a second one. Fold 06 §11's four additional requirements in. Update 11 §9 rows 2 and 5 to the merged shape.

### C49. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** A single gigapipe boot flag is specified with opposite values in two documents, and the difference is a tenancy hazard. 01:1857 requires `ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES=true`; 05:1847 requires `ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES=false`. Verified in gigapipe: `reader/logql/logql_transpiler/clickhouse_planner/planner_stream_select.go:31-46` — when `OmitEmptyValues` is **false** the planner walks the selector and silently **removes** any matcher whose op is `=` or `=~` with an empty value, and any `=~".*"` matcher; when **true** it `break`s out of that loop immediately and keeps every matcher. Since the compiled `op_project_id` matcher is the only thing separating tenants on the LogQL read path (05's own invariant I3), the fail-safe value is `true`. Compounding it: 10-ops's compose service definition (§2) sets the variable at all — verified zero occurrences of `ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES` in 10-ops-retention-billing.md — so what actually ships today is gigapipe's default, which is the stripping behaviour.

**Fix.** Settle to `true`, add it to 10-ops §3's environment table with the `planner_stream_select.go:31-46` citation, set it in both gigapipe services and in 11's `test/telemetry/docker-compose.yml`, and add it to 11's W7 source-level compose assertions. Fix 05:1847 to match.

### C50. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** The fix for the plan's only unbounded cross-tenant *read* is specified in one document and absent from the document that owns Postgres schema. `01-tenancy-and-security.md` D5 establishes the concrete sequence: `project.delete` only sets `deleteAt` (`packages/trpc/src/routers/project.ts:207-231`); `cron.delete` hard-deletes the row via `deleteProjects` (`packages/db/src/services/delete.service.ts:15-37`); `getId` collides only against live rows (`packages/db/src/services/id.service.ts:19-23`); nothing deletes the ClickHouse telemetry under that `op_project_id`. So "Acme Prod" is deleted, anyone — including a different organisation — creates a project of that name, receives `id = 'acme-prod'`, and inherits the deleted project's metrics, logs and traces. An organisation with no `org:admin` member is hard-deleted with no `deleteAt` grace at all (`cron.delete.ts:17-19`), so the window can be zero. The remedy is a new Prisma model (`ProjectIdTombstone`), a migration, a backfill, a write inside `deleteProjects`' transaction, and a change to `getId` — a function on the project-creation hot path. `08-schema-changes.md` §0 enumerates eleven Prisma changes across six migration files and `ProjectIdTombstone` is not among them; its "non-migration code that must change with the schema" table does not list `id.service.ts` or `delete.service.ts`'s tombstone write either. 01 correctly notes the purge cannot be the boundary because `deleteFromClickhouse` runs with `lightweight_deletes_sync: '0'` (verified, `delete.service.ts:68`) and `ALTER … DELETE` is asynchronous.

**Fix.** Add `ProjectIdTombstone` to `08-schema-changes.md` §0's Postgres inventory as P11 with its own migration file, add `packages/db/src/services/id.service.ts` and the `deleteProjects` transaction write to its non-migration table, and give it a row in §16's rollback table. Add the P0 audit query `SELECT id, name FROM projects WHERE id !~ '^[a-zA-Z0-9_-]{1,100}$'` to `10-ops-retention-billing.md`'s P0 gate list, since 01 Q1 currently leaves it ownerless.

### C51. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** 11's must-gate 1.9 asserts a table that two other specs say is deliberately never deleted from, so the gate fails by construction. 11 I13: "every A row gone from `time_series`, `samples_v3`, `time_series_gin`, `metrics_15s`, `patterns`, `tempo_traces`, `tempo_traces_attrs_gin`, `tempo_traces_kv`" — eight tables. 06 §11.6: "**`tempo_traces_kv` is not deleted from.** It holds only `(date, key, val_id, val)` with no trace reference, so a per-profile delete is impossible there." 08 §14: "**`tempo_traces_kv` is not deleted, deliberately.** It is a shared value dictionary … a project's rows are bucket entries other projects still use." Separately the table lists disagree in size and membership across 02 §17 (seven, no `patterns`), 05 §7.4 (five, logs only), 08 §14 (seven, with `patterns`, no `_kv`) and 11 (eight).

**Fix.** Drop `tempo_traces_kv` from 11 I13. Then publish the authoritative table list as a single exported constant — 11 I14 already asks for exactly that ("the telemetry table list is one exported constant, imported by the delete path, the retention sweep and the teardown") — owned by 08, and make 02 §17, 05 §7.4 and 11 I13 all reference it rather than each enumerating their own.

### C52. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Two mutually exclusive log-ingest designs, and 05's breaks an invariant 08 and 10 build retention and billing on. 02 §1 lists `/loki/api/v1/push` under "Explicitly not exposed in P1" with the reason: "The Loki JSON decoder is the one path in gigapipe that emits `type = 0` samples … Restricting ingest to OTLP + remote-write is what makes the two-valued `type` model in the schema work-stream safe. That is a constraint this gateway must *enforce*, not assume." 02 also rejects OTLP/JSON with 415 (D12). 05 D1/§4.1 exposes `POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` as a first-class customer route **accepting `application/json`**, and D1 has apps/api decode OTLP itself and push Loki JSON onward to gigapipe. 08 §13 option (c) and 10 D10 both rest on "type 0 should never occur … an **enforced gateway invariant plus a monitored assertion**". 05's design makes type-0 rows reachable from customer traffic on day one.

**Fix.** Decide the log ingest shape once. If 05's Loki-JSON gateway wins (its cardinality argument against forwarding raw OTLP to gigapipe is strong and well-evidenced), then 08 §13 and 10 D10 must stop claiming type 0 never occurs and must instead state the retention residual honestly — and 02's route table must gain the Loki route. If 02 wins, 05 §4.1's route, its content-type parser and its P3.4b snappy sub-step all disappear. Either way, 02's `Interfaces → schema` row ("`type ∈ {1,2}` is safe **only because** this gateway does not expose `/loki/api/v1/push`") is currently false against 05 and must be corrected.

### C53. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** 02 and 05 specify mutually exclusive log ingest architectures, and the choice is an input to a P0 one-way door. 02 §1's P1 route table forwards `POST /telemetry/v1/logs` (protobuf only, JSON rejected 415 per D12) to gigapipe's `/v1/logs`, and lists `/loki/api/v1/push` under 'Explicitly not exposed in P1' on the grounds that it is 'the one path in gigapipe that emits type = 0 samples… Restricting ingest to OTLP + remote-write is what makes the two-valued `type` model in the schema work-stream safe. That is a constraint this gateway must *enforce*, not assume.' 05 does the exact opposite on all three counts: D1 — 'apps/api decodes OTLP itself and pushes Loki JSON. It never forwards OTLP to gigapipe'; 05:3 — 'It then pushes Loki JSON to gigapipe's `/loki/api/v1/push`'; 05:462-463 — it exposes a customer-facing `POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` **and** accepts `application/json` on `/telemetry/v1/logs`. 05's reasoning is strong (gigapipe's OTLP log decoder promotes every attribute including `trace_id`/`span_id` to a stream label with no config to disable it, ~10k new series/s at 10k lines/s, and the OTLP path never calls `sanitizeLabels`). But the protocol choice determines the `type`-column distribution in `samples_v3`, which is the premise of the conditional TTL written against the P0 partition key.

**Fix.** Settle the log ingest protocol as a P0 decision, before migration 22's DDL is written. If 05 wins — and its evidence says it should — delete `/telemetry/v1/logs` from 02's P1 route table entirely, move the OTLP-logs decode into 05's P3.4, and rewrite 02's 'Explicitly not exposed' rationale so it bans forwarding to gigapipe's `/loki/api/v1/push` rather than banning a Loki-shaped OpenPanel route. Reconcile the JSON/415 rule at the same time.

### C54. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** The three metric-browse procedures are specified twice, by two owners, on contradictory backends, both in P2. Doc 03 §Interfaces claims "the browse procedures (this spec owns the server half)" and defines `metricNames`/`metricLabels`/`labelValues` in `packages/trpc/src/routers/metrics.ts`, backed by `catalog.ts` which calls gigapipe's `GET /api/v1/label/__name__/values` with a constructed `match[]`. Doc 04 D4 gives six verified reasons never to call those endpoints — `GenericLabelReq` `break`s out of its scan loop on error and still writes `{"status":"success"}` (`reader/service/query_abels.go:62,67`), they hardcode `Limit: 10000` with no paging (`:220,292`), and `Prom2LogqlMatch` `panic`s on a malformed `match[]` (`:165`) — and therefore defines `metrics.names`/`.labelKeys`/`.labelValues` in `packages/trpc/src/routers/observability.ts` backed by a new `packages/db/src/services/telemetry-metadata.service.ts` reading ClickHouse directly. Doc 09 P2b's MetricPicker consumes "the browse procedures" without saying whose. Both are budgeted (doc 03 slice 5: 1.5 d; doc 04: inside its 4.0-week P2).

**Fix.** Assign the browse procedures to one stream in the blueprint. Doc 04's evidence is decisive on the backend — a label endpoint that silently drops rows and still returns HTTP 200 is not a picker source — so the metadata service wins, and doc 03 §7's `resolveMetricCatalog` should be rewritten to consume it rather than gigapipe. Settle the router namespace at the same time (`metrics.*` vs `observability.*`), since doc 09's UI imports one of them.

### C55. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** `Report.dataSource` is simultaneously rejected and mandated. 03 §15.1: "That last row is the point: **no migration**. Dispatch is on `series.some(s => s.type === 'metric')` behind one helper (`packages/db/src/engine/data-source.ts`, `getReportDataSource(series)`), not on a `Report.dataSource` column — that identifier exists nowhere in either repo today." 08 S1: "`Report.dataSource` is a new Postgres enum `DataSource { events metrics }` plus a column", with a migration, an inventory entry P2/P3, and nine whitelist sites. 09 D8 builds its entire eight-write-path table on the column ("MCP fails **open** the moment `dataSource` lands on `zReportInput`"), and 04 D4 prices it. Whoever implements P2 will either ship a migration nobody consumes or a dispatch helper that reads a column that does not exist.

**Fix.** Adopt 03's derivation (`getReportDataSource(series)`) and delete `DataSource` + `Report.dataSource` from 08's inventory, its migration list, its sequencing table and its rollback table. 03 is right on the merits: the discriminator already arrives on `zReportInput.series` as the `type:'metric'` union member, so 08's own argument ("the discriminator has to arrive from the browser regardless") is satisfied without a column, and the column adds an irreversible enum plus three write literals for nothing. Retarget 08's nine-site whitelist inventory and 09 D8's eight-site table at the *series union member* instead — that inventory is correct and valuable, only its subject is wrong. Rewrite 09 D8 and 04 D4 to match.

### C56. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Three documents specify three incompatible persistence models for a metric report, and the schema owner is in the minority. 03-metrics D2 and 09-ui D2 both make a metric query a third member of `zChartEventItem`, stored in the existing `Report.events` JSON column, and both add a `metric` branch to `transformReportEventItem` (09 calls this "the highest-severity finding in the review"). 08-schema D1 explicitly *rejects* that — "`transformReport` reads `(report.events as IChartEventItem[])` … a metric payload there throws or produces garbage" — and ships `Report.metricQuery Json?` as a separate column instead. 08 owns the migration. If 08 ships, 03's engine and 09's editor read from a column that holds nothing and 09's `transformReportEventItem` branch is dead. If 03/09 ship, 08's column is unused and the migration is wrong. Compounding this, the metric query *schema itself* differs in every field that matters: 03's `zMetricQuery` has `metric`/`matchers`/`metricType` (required, no default, and D6's entire rejection of `/api/v1/metadata` rests on it) with `fn ∈ {rate, increase, value, avg_over_time, histogram_quantile}` and `aggregation ∈ {sum, avg, min, max}`; 09's `zChartMetricItem` has `name`/`filters`, **no `metricType` at all**, `fn ∈ {none, rate, increase, delta}` and `aggregation ∈ {sum, avg, min, max, count, p50, p90, p95, p99}`. Neither compiles against the other's compiler. The enum name also differs (`DataSource` in 03, `ReportDataSource` in 08/09), and per-series `seriesLimit` (03) competes with the report-level `limit` column (09 D1a).

**Fix.** Blueprint decision, then one PR. My recommendation is 03/09's shape (union member in `Report.events`) because it inherits `alphabetIds`, reorder, `visibleSeries`, `hideSeries` and formulas for free, and because 08's objection is answered by the `transformReportEventItem` branch that both 03 and 09 already specify. Delete `Report.metricQuery` from 08. Publish exactly one `zMetricQuery` in `packages/validation` — 03's, which is the only one with `metricType`, and therefore the only one whose compiler can avoid the unscopable `/api/v1/metadata` endpoint. Reconcile `seriesLimit` vs `limit` (keep both: per-series is the topk cap, report-level is the render cap) and fix the enum name.

### C57. `08-schema-changes.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Two mutually exclusive persistence models for a metric report. 08 D1: `Report.dataSource ReportDataSource @default(events)` **plus** `Report.metricQuery Json?`, and §3 states "A metric report stores `events = []` and `breakdowns = []`, and its input carries `series: []`. `transformReport` must **skip** `transformReportEventItem` when `dataSource === 'metrics'`" — because "a metric payload there throws or produces garbage". 03 D2 and 09 D2 do the opposite: the metric spec is a third member of `zChartEventItem` stored in `Report.events`, and both make `transformReportEventItem` gaining a `metric` branch the single highest-risk item in their stream (03 D2: "the single highest-risk item in the work-stream"; 09 D2a: "the highest-severity finding in the review"). Verified against the tree: `zChartEventItem` is `z.discriminatedUnion('type',[zChartEventWithType, zChartFormula])` (packages/validation/src/index.ts:143), `transformReportEventItem` has exactly the two branches 03/09 describe (reports.service.ts:56-79), and `Report` has neither `dataSource`, `metricQuery` nor `limit` (schema.prisma:425-459). The two designs also disagree on the enum name (`DataSource` in 03 §3 item 2 vs `ReportDataSource` in 08/09) and on whether `limit` gets a column at all (09 D1a says yes and calls it load-bearing; 03 and 08 never mention it).

**Fix.** Adopt the union-member model (03 + 09) and delete `Report.metricQuery` from 08. It is what 03's engine actually consumes (`normalized.series`), what `format()`'s `definitions` parameter needs, and what buys the `alphabetIds`/`hideSeries`/reorder/`visibleSeries` reuse that decision #4 depends on; 08's own objection is answered by 09 D2a's `transformReportEventItem` metric branch with a `const _exhaustive: never` default. 08 must then rewrite D1, §3's "events = [] / series: []" paragraph, whitelist row 7, the migration SQL and the rollback section. Standardise on `ReportDataSource` (2 of 3 docs) and add `limit Int?` to the same migration per 09 D1a.

### C58. `08-schema-changes.md`, `05-logs.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** Whether OpenPanel Cloud's ClickHouse is clustered is answered three different ways, and one answer makes the whole plan unshippable on Cloud. Doc 08 D8 declares "Clustered telemetry is explicitly not supported in P0-P6" and leaves `CLUSTER_NAME` unset; doc 10 D20 agrees. Doc 05 L13 says the opposite and is correct on the mechanism — I verified `isClickhouseClustered()` at `packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts:83-93`: it returns **true by default** and only returns false when `SELF_HOSTED` is set, so Cloud runs clustered with Replicated locals and Distributed companions. Doc 05 spells out the failure: a plain `MergeTree` pre-create on a multi-node cluster means gigapipe writes non-replicated local tables on whichever node its connection lands on, pushes scatter and reads return a random subset with no error anywhere. Doc 04 Q1 states that if Cloud is clustered, `_dist` resolution and `GLOBAL IN` become "a P0 blocker rather than a P2 detail". Doc 06 T11 invents a third signal (`GIGAPIPE_CLUSTERED`) independent of both. So the plan simultaneously defers clustering past P6, treats it as a P0 blocker, and routes around it with a fourth mechanism.

**Fix.** Answer this in the P0 spike week — it is one `SELECT` against the Cloud deployment plus reading its `CLICKHOUSE_CLUSTER`/`SELF_HOSTED`. If Cloud is clustered, doc 08 D8 must be reversed and clustered telemetry becomes P0 scope (doc 04 sizes it at +1 week for `_dist` + `GLOBAL IN`, plus a boot-time table-existence assertion, a CI matrix and a seeded clustered fixture); doc 05's `isClickhouseClustered()`-branched pre-create becomes mandatory. If it is not, doc 05 L13 and doc 06 T11 must be amended and the reason recorded. Do not ship a self-hosted-only observability feature by accident.

### C59. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Four incompatible helpers for naming tables in the gigapipe database, in four files, reading four different env vars, and two of them export the *same symbol name* with different value shapes. 04 D12: `TELEMETRY_DB` + `TELEMETRY_TABLES` (unqualified values, e.g. `samples: 'samples_v3'`) + `G(t)` in `packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts`, database from `TELEMETRY_CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE`, cluster from `GIGAPIPE_CLUSTER_NAME`. 06 §5: `TELEMETRY_DB` + `TELEMETRY_TABLES` (**pre-qualified** values via `g()`, e.g. `traces: 'gigapipe.tempo_traces_dist'`) + `getTelemetryMutationTable()` + `TELEMETRY_IN`, in the same file, database from `GIGAPIPE_DB`, cluster from `GIGAPIPE_CLUSTER`. 05 §7.2: `gigapipeTable(name, 'read'|'mutate')` in `packages/db/src/gigapipe/table-name.ts`, database from `GIGAPIPE_DB`, cluster from OpenPanel's own `isClickhouseClustered()` — which 04 D12 explicitly forbids ("Do NOT reuse isClickhouseClustered() (client.ts:83), which defaults to TRUE unless SELF_HOSTED is set … nor getIsCluster()"). 08 §11: a third `TELEMETRY_TABLES` (unqualified) plus `telemetryDatabase()` in `packages/db/src/clickhouse/telemetry-client.ts`, database from `CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_URL`/`CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_DB`, with no cluster support at all. Two of these export `TELEMETRY_TABLES` from different modules with different member names *and* different value semantics.

**Fix.** One owner, one file, one env var. Recommend 08's `packages/db/src/clickhouse/telemetry-client.ts` as the home (it is the only one that is lazy, memoised and pins a single node, which the DDL and mutation paths require), extended with 06's `getTelemetryMutationTable()`/`TELEMETRY_IN` and 04's read-side `_dist` suffix. Pick one database var — `GIGAPIPE_DB` has the most citations and 10 §3.1 already declares it settled — and delete `TELEMETRY_CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE`, `CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_DB` and `CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_URL` or make them explicit aliases. Delete `packages/db/src/gigapipe/table-name.ts` from 05 and `G()` from 04. `TELEMETRY_TABLES` values must be unqualified; qualification is the helper's job, so a caller cannot accidentally double-qualify.

### C60. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Five different retention numbers, and two of them are load-bearing for query clamps. 04 §3: `GIGAPIPE_RETENTION_DAYS` default `7`, a single scalar. 05 §5.1: `PLAN_DEFAULT_RETENTION_DAYS = 30` for logs. 06 §6.0: "default 7 days", and `TRACE_SEARCH_MAX_WINDOW_H = 24 * 7; // == the telemetry retention ceiling`. 08 §13: worked example 90 metrics / 30 logs, citing tiers "trial 7/3, starter 15/7, growth 30/14, scale 90/30" attributed to `10-ops-retention-billing.md:925-932`. 10 §6.1 ships metrics 30 / logs 14 / traces 7 / labels 30 on cloud and one `TELEMETRY_RETENTION_DAYS` default 14 self-hosted, and §6.3 **defers** the tier table 08 cites as current. On top of that, 10's Interfaces exports `TELEMETRY_RETENTION_DAYS` as an object `{metrics:30, logs:14, traces:7, labels:30}` while 10 §2/§3.1 uses the same name as a scalar compose env var.

**Fix.** 10 owns retention. Publish one per-signal constant with one name (not `TELEMETRY_RETENTION_DAYS` for both a scalar env var and an object export — rename the object, e.g. `TELEMETRY_RETENTION`), have 04 replace `GIGAPIPE_RETENTION_DAYS` and its single `oldestQueryableAt` with per-signal values (10's Interfaces already asks for this), fix 06's `TRACE_SEARCH_MAX_WINDOW_H` to read the traces window rather than hardcoding 7 days, and correct 08 §13's worked example and its `TELEMETRY_RETENTION` citation to the deferred-tier reality.

### C61. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Three deletion designs with three function names, three ledger models, three call sites and opposite failure semantics. 05 §7.4: `purgeTelemetry({projectId, reason, olderThanNs, signals, resumeJobId})` + `model TelemetryPurgeJob` (states resolved/submitted/done/failed), called from `jobDelete` in a per-project try/catch where **only successfully-purged projects proceed to `deleteProjects`** ("Only projects whose journal row is committed may have their Postgres rows removed"). 08 §14: `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse(projectIds)` + `model TelemetryErasure`, called **inside** `deleteFromClickhouse`, and it "must never throw … NEVER propagate" so `deleteProjects` always runs. 06 §11.6: `deleteTelemetryForProjects(projectIds)` + `deleteTelemetryForProfile()` in `delete.service.ts`, called **between** `deleteFromClickhouse` and `deleteProjects`. 09 §14 cites 05's `purgeTelemetry`; 11 I13/gate 1.9 cites 08's `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse`. 05 and 08 are semantically opposite on whether a failed purge blocks the Postgres delete.

**Fix.** 08 owns `delete.service.ts` and its inside-`deleteFromClickhouse` call site is right — it is the only one that covers both `cron.delete.ts:46` and `admin/src/commands/delete-organization.ts:191`, and 08's argument that an unguarded throw stops every project and organization deletion on the deployment is verified (`jobDelete()` has no try/catch). Adopt 08's function, its non-throwing contract and its `TelemetryErasure` ledger; fold 05's resumability (`resumeJobId`, durable fingerprint set) into it because that part is genuinely better; delete 05's `TelemetryPurgeJob` and 06's two function names, and have 06's per-profile erasure become a `signals`/`subject` argument on the one function. Update 09 §14 and 11 I13/I14 to the single name.

### C62. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** 11's must-gate 1.9 mandates the wrong table-naming helper for gigapipe. I14: "`deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse` routes every target through `getReplicatedTableName` (`packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts:101-106`)". Verified in the repo: that function returns `` `${tableName}_replicated ON CLUSTER '{cluster}'` ``. gigapipe has no `_replicated` tables — its clustered layout is a plain-named local `ReplicatedMergeTree` plus a `_dist` Distributed companion (`ctrl/qryn/sql/log_dist.sql`), which is exactly what 05 §7.2 says ("Following the draft literally emits `ALTER TABLE gigapipe.samples_v3_replicated …`, which does not exist — on Cloud, where the paying customers are") and 06 §5 repeats ("gigapipe names its local tables without a suffix, so this is the un-suffixed name, not `_replicated`"). As written, 11 turns a known-broken construction into a required CI assertion, and on a clustered install the GDPR erasure path would fail with UNKNOWN_TABLE.

**Fix.** Delete the `getReplicatedTableName` clause from 11 I14 and replace it with an assertion that every gigapipe mutation target goes through the single shared telemetry table helper (per the previous finding) and that no emitted statement contains the substring `_replicated`. 05 and 06 are right; 11 is repeating a stale draft.

### C63. `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** Three incompatible telemetry-metering schemas with contradictory semantics for the same column. 08 D4: `Organization.subscriptionPeriodTelemetryCount BigInt @default(0)` + `subscriptionPeriodTelemetryLimit BigInt?` where "`limit === null` ⇒ telemetry not provisioned ⇒ no telemetry tokens, no metric reports" — nullability is the entitlement signal — with usage in a ClickHouse `openpanel.telemetry_usage` SummingMergeTree. 10 §10.4: `subscriptionPeriodTelemetryUnits Int @default(0)` + `subscriptionPeriodTelemetryLimit Int @default(0)` where "0 or negative means UNLIMITED, never 'shed everything'" (D12), with usage in a **Postgres** `telemetry_usage_daily` table and an explicit rejection of reading ClickHouse. 05 §8.8 adds a third: a Prisma `TelemetryUsage` model keyed `(projectId, signal, hour)` with a `TelemetrySignal` enum, annotated "Shared with the metrics and traces work-streams — coordinate before landing the migration". So the same `subscriptionPeriodTelemetryLimit` column is `BigInt?` where null means unprovisioned in one doc and `Int` where 0 means unlimited in another, and the counter is named `…Count` in one and `…Units` in the other.

**Fix.** 10 owns billing and quota and its `0 = unlimited` argument is grounded in a verified in-repo trap (`sendUsageAlerts` opens `if (!limit || limit <= 0) return;`, and `onboarding.ts:22-24` documents the same), so adopt 10's semantics and column names. But take 08's `BigInt` argument for the counter — 10's own sizing puts a large project at 2.8e10 log bytes/day, and 10 concedes the `Int` ceiling is "a support conversation waiting anyway". Result: `subscriptionPeriodTelemetryUnits BigInt @default(0)`, `subscriptionPeriodTelemetryLimit BigInt @default(0)` with `<= 0` meaning unlimited, and one usage table. Choose between 10's daily Postgres `telemetry_usage_daily` and 05's hourly `TelemetryUsage` (10's is the billing record and its 25-month audit window is stated; 05's hourly grain buys nothing billing needs) — then delete the other and 08's ClickHouse `telemetry_usage` along with 08 §C2, §T5 and the `deleteFromClickhouse` entry it adds. 08 must also drop its `hasTelemetryClients`-by-nullable-limit entitlement mechanism or move it to a separate flag.

### C64. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Three incompatible telemetry-metering Prisma models, and 10's cron code will not compile against 08's schema. 05 §4.6 declares `model TelemetryUsage { projectId, signal (enum TelemetrySignal), granularity (enum TelemetryGranularity), hour, lines BigInt, bytes BigInt }` with `@@unique([projectId, signal, granularity, hour])` and says "Shared with the metrics and traces work-streams — coordinate before landing the migration." 08 §4 declares `model TelemetryUsageDaily { projectId, organizationId, day @db.Date, metricDataPoints, logBytes, spans, units, finalizedAt }` with `@@unique([projectId, day])`. 10 §8.3's rollup writes fields `requests, bytesIngress, metricSamples, metricDatapoints, metricRejected, logsRecords, logsBytes, tracesSpans, billingUnits` and upserts on `organizationId_projectId_day`; 10's Interfaces then asks for `@@id([organizationId, projectId, day])`. Field names, grain, key and even the enum-vs-column modelling of the signal all differ.

**Fix.** 08 owns Prisma declarations, so 08's model is the target — but its field list must be replaced with 10's (10 owns metering semantics and needs both `metricSamples` and `metricDatapoints`, per its own D11 fan-out argument), and the unique key must be settled at `(projectId, day)` with `organizationId` denormalised, or at `(organizationId, projectId, day)` — one of them, written once. Delete 05's `TelemetryUsage` model entirely and have the logs stream write into the shared daily table; 05's hourly grain is a third design that neither of the other two consumes. This is one migration and it must be landed by one person.

### C65. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** The conditional TTL assigns `type = 0` rows to opposite retention windows in two specs. 05 §7.3: `toDateTime(...) + INTERVAL 30 DAY DELETE WHERE type IN (1, 0), toDateTime(...) + INTERVAL 395 DAY DELETE WHERE type = 2` — type-0 gets the **short (log)** window. 08 S13 and 10 D10: `... DELETE WHERE type != 1, ... DELETE WHERE type = 1` — type-0 gets the **long (metric)** window, with 08 adding a precondition "`metricDays >= logDays`, asserted before the DDL is emitted". 05's form is also non-total in a way 08 warns about if a fourth value ever appears. Additionally the day counts (30/395) appear in no other document, and 05 uses `INTERVAL n DAY` while 08 uses `toIntervalDay(n)`.

**Fix.** 08/10's `type != 1` / `type = 1` form is right and should replace 05 §7.3 verbatim, including the `metricDays >= logDays` assertion. 08's reasoning is the stronger one: a type-0 row is visible to *both* engines (`GetTypes` emits `type IN (n,0)`), so expiring it on the log window silently truncates metric query results, whereas expiring it on the metric window is only a retention-promise disclosure. Delete 05's 30/395 numbers and have it consume the windows from 10's constant.

### C66. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Two different pre-create DDLs for the one irreversible table, and they disagree on which tables are pre-created. 05 §7.1 pre-creates the **local `samples_v3` only** — "We pre-create the LOCAL `samples_v3` only — never `samples_v3_dist`" — with `PARTITION BY (toStartOfDay(…), type)`, and its test (§Test requirements, "schema shape at boot") asserts only `samples_v3`. 08 S6/C2/C3 pre-creates **both** `samples_v3` and `metrics_15s`, with `PARTITION BY (type, toStartOfDay(…))`, `type` **last** in column order, and `metrics_15s` created with gigapipe's post-ALTER sort key `(fingerprint, timestamp_ns, type)` — 08 §9 shows that getting `metrics_15s` wrong makes `ctrl.Init` panic and `op-gigapipe-init` crash-loop on every install. 10 D4 and its P0 gate 1 follow 08. If 05's migration is the one that ships, `metrics_15s` gets gigapipe's day-only partition key and per-signal retention on the rollup table — which 08 §13 and 10 §6.1 both require, because `metrics_15s_mv` rolls up log rows too — becomes permanently impossible.

**Fix.** 08 owns migration 22 and its DDL wins; delete 05 §7.1's DDL and its `gigapipe-bootstrap.test.ts` assertions, replacing them with a pointer. Note the partition-key column order also differs (`(type, day)` vs `(day, type)`); pick 08's and state it once, because `PARTITION BY` cannot be `ALTER`ed and two documents currently hand an implementer two strings for the same first-boot decision.

### C67. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Three documents give three different Cloud stories for the plan's single irreversible decision. Verified in the repo: `getIsCluster()` (`packages/db/code-migrations/helpers.ts:17-25`) is `--cluster || CLICKHOUSE_CLUSTER in {true,1}` — **false by default**; `isClickhouseClustered()` (`packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts:83-95`) is `!(SELF_HOSTED)` — **true by default**. On Cloud with neither variable set they disagree. (1) 05 §7.1 reasons from `isClickhouseClustered()` being true that Cloud is clustered, so a plain-`MergeTree` pre-create means 'gigapipe writes non-replicated local tables on whichever node its connection lands on: pushes scatter, reads return a random subset, and the explorer silently loses lines with no error anywhere.' (2) 08 §10 uses `getIsCluster()` and concludes 'What OpenPanel Cloud does in P0: `CLICKHOUSE_CLUSTER=true` and `GIGAPIPE_URL` unset. Migration 22 skips… Telemetry ships on single-node self-hosting first' — i.e. the whole programme delivers nothing to Cloud. (3) 10 §5.2 says Cloud runs one **non-clustered** gigapipe against a dedicated node named by `CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_URL`, with migration 22 re-run under `--force-telemetry`. Since `PARTITION BY` cannot be `ALTER`ed and every gigapipe statement is `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS`, guessing wrong costs the full-table rebuild in 08 §10.

**Fix.** Settle the Cloud topology in P0, as a decision recorded in `00-blueprint.md` and adopted verbatim by all three documents. 10 §5.2's dedicated-non-clustered-node answer is the one that lets Cloud ship; if it is chosen, 08 S9's `getIsCluster()` skip must be re-derived (a dedicated node is not the analytics cluster) and 05 §7.1's Replicated branch becomes dead. Also state explicitly which phase unblocks Cloud, because as written no phase does.

### C68. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** The conditional-TTL statement is written two incompatible ways, on a predicate whose correctness is security-adjacent. 08 S13 and 10 §6.1 write two **total** clauses — `type != 1` at 30 d (metrics) and `type = 1` at 14 d (logs) — deliberately assigning the three-valued column's `type = 0` (UNDEF/both, written by live ingest) to the **longer** window, with an asserted invariant `metricDays >= logDays`, because gigapipe's reader emits `type IN (n, 0)` so a type-0 row is visible to both signals and putting it on the short window 'would silently delete rows metric queries still return'. 05 §7.3 writes the opposite: `DELETE WHERE type IN (1, 0)` at **30 DAY** and `DELETE WHERE type = 2` at **395 DAY** — equality on the metric side, which 08 S13 explicitly rejects as non-total, and type-0 on the short window, which 10 D10 explicitly names as the silent-data-loss case. The day counts also disagree entirely (30/395 vs 30/14).

**Fix.** Delete the DDL from 05 §7.3 and replace it with a one-line reference to 08 S12/S13, which owns the statement. If 05 needs to restate it for readability, restate it byte-identically. Note that under the log-protocol resolution, whether type-0 rows occur at all is itself a P0 decision.

### C69. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Three specs give three different owners and mechanisms for the same irreversible DDL, and one of them explicitly rejects what the other two require. 05 L13/§8.2: OpenPanel pre-creates `samples_v3` in `packages/db/code-migrations/22-gigapipe-bootstrap.ts` with `PARTITION BY (toStartOfDay(...), type)` and `ORDER BY (fingerprint, timestamp_ns)`, with `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES: "false"` in its env manifest (§8.1). 10 D4/§5: a compose one-shot `op-gigapipe-provision` runs `self-hosting/clickhouse/gigapipe-provision.sql`, pre-creating **both** `samples_v3` and `metrics_15s` with `type` in the partition key, with `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES: "true"` on the long-running node (D3). 08 D7 says "OpenPanel owns no table inside `openpanel_telemetry`" and D11 explicitly rejects the pre-create: "**Rejected: pre-creating `samples_v3` with `type` in `PARTITION BY`.** … it forks from upstream DDL invisibly … **This is a one-way door**" — while also rejecting `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES=true` as a default. 11 §11.2 assertion 2 and §8.1 encode 10's position as a test (`samples_v3.partition_key` **contains `type`**, `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES: "true"`), so 11 fails against both 05 and 08. Separately, 05 and 08 both claim code-migration ordinal 22 (`22-gigapipe-bootstrap.ts` vs `22-telemetry-database.ts`); verified the directory already has colliding `20-`/`21-` pairs, so a third collision would be silently order-dependent.

**Fix.** Take one decision at P0 and write it into all four docs. 10's mechanism is the right one — a compose one-shot runs before gigapipe's first boot on every surface, whereas a code migration runs from `op-api`'s start command and would need a `depends_on` edge that 10 D7 forbids, and 05's version omits `metrics_15s`, which 10 correctly identifies as "the primary metric read path". So: adopt 10 §5's `gigapipe-provision.sql` + `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES=true`; delete 05's `22-gigapipe-bootstrap.ts` and change 05 §8.1's `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES` to `"true"`; rewrite 08 D7/D11's rejection into an acknowledgement that the DDL is owned by ops, leaving 08 with only `22-telemetry-database.ts` (the `openpanel`-side meter table). Whoever loses the ordinal takes 23.

### C70. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** 11 makes a must-gate out of using the wrong table-naming helper on the GDPR erasure path. 11's failure-mode 10 mitigation reads: 'the must-have is I14's source-level assertion that `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse` routes through `getReplicatedTableName`.' Verified in the repo: `getReplicatedTableName` (`packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts:100-107`) returns `` `${tableName}_replicated ON CLUSTER '{cluster}'` `` — an OpenPanel naming convention. 05 §7.2 establishes from gigapipe's own DDL that its clustered layout is the inverse: the plain-named local table becomes `ReplicatedMergeTree` and Distributed companions carry `_dist`; there is **no** `_replicated` variant, and 05 states plainly that following the OpenPanel convention emits `ALTER TABLE gigapipe.samples_v3_replicated …`, 'which does not exist — on Cloud, where the paying customers are.' So I14 as written would make a required check enforce a broken delete in the erasure path.

**Fix.** Rewrite I14 to assert that every gigapipe-database statement routes through the gigapipe-specific naming helper (05 §7.2's `gigapipeTable(name, 'read'|'mutate')`), and to assert that `getReplicatedTableName` is **never** applied to a gigapipe table name. Fold this into the single-home decision for that helper.

### C71. `07-alerting.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** The alerting schema and the schema-owning document specify different tables. Doc 07 §4 defines three Prisma models — `MetricAlertRuleRuntime` (scheduling, consecutive-error counter, config fingerprint, notification budget), `MetricAlertState` (per-series state with `incidentId`, `pendingSince`, `activeEvaluations`, `suppressedUntil`) and `MetricAlertEvent` — plus a `MetricAlertStateKind` enum. Doc 08, which owns Prisma, ships one model: D5 "Ship `TelemetryAlert` only. `TelemetryAlertEvent`, `threadId` and `version` are cut", with nine columns and no per-rule runtime row at all. Doc 07 exposes `MetricAlertEvent` in its Interfaces as a public contract ("the incident history any future surface — MCP tool, digest email, status page — reads from") and budgets item 12 (2.0 days) for an incidents route and event history that read it. Doc 08 sizes the whole thing at 1.5 days; doc 07 sizes the migration at 1.0 day. Neither number covers the other's schema.

**Fix.** Reconcile before P5 is scheduled — this is cheap now and a migration-on-a-migration later. Doc 07's `MetricAlertRuleRuntime` is not optional: doc 07's own D3 (level-triggered scheduling by stored due-time) and D9 (config reconciliation by fingerprint) have no home without it, and doc 08's D5 rejection reasoning addresses only the event table and `threadId`/`version`. Recommended: adopt doc 07's `MetricAlertRuleRuntime` + `MetricAlertState`, accept doc 08's cut of the event table, and delete doc 07's `MetricAlertEvent` from its Interfaces and from item 12's scope — or restore it with a named consumer. Re-size both docs afterwards.

### C72. `07-alerting.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The alerting spec and the schema spec ship mutually unimplementable Postgres schemas. 07-alerting §4 defines three models — `MetricAlertRuleRuntime` (`metric_alert_rule_runtime`), `MetricAlertState` (`metric_alert_states`), `MetricAlertEvent` (`metric_alert_events`) — with `enum MetricAlertStateKind { pending firing resolved suppressed }`. 08-schema D5 defines exactly one model, `TelemetryAlert` (`telemetry_alerts`), with `enum TelemetryAlertState { ok pending firing }`, and explicitly cuts the event log on the grounds that the draft "gave `TelemetryAlertEvent` no reader at all" — which 07 refutes, since its append-only log *is* the notification outbox (§6, §12) and its health badge and F10 debugging surface read it. Worse, 07's central scheduling decision D3 (level-triggered dispatch on stored `nextEvaluationAt`, chosen specifically because BullMQ does not backfill missed repeats) has no column to live in under 08's schema, so the scheduler silently reverts to the modular-arithmetic design 07 rejected. 11-testing D4 sides with 07 and says so by name. 08 claims to own "every persistence change the observability plan needs".

**Fix.** 07 owns the alert data model; 08 owns the migration mechanics. Delete `TelemetryAlert` and `TelemetryAlertState` from 08-schema and replace §5 with 07's three models verbatim plus the enum-ordering and `@db.Uuid`-vs-TEXT notes that 08 got right (`Project.id` has no `@db.Uuid`, `NotificationRule.id` does — that correction is real and must survive). Keep 08's `seriesKey`-is-a-hash rationale, which 07 also has.

### C73. `07-alerting.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Two complete, incompatible alert-state schemas, each presented as final. 07 §4: three Postgres models — `MetricAlertRuleRuntime` (dueness, `configFingerprint`, notification budget), `MetricAlertState` (enum `MetricAlertStateKind { pending firing resolved suppressed }`, `incidentId`, `activeEvaluations`, `lastEvaluatedTick`) and an append-only `MetricAlertEvent` that doubles as the notification outbox. 08 D5: "Ship `TelemetryAlert` only. `TelemetryAlertEvent`, `threadId` and `version` are cut", with `enum TelemetryAlertState { ok pending firing }` and no runtime or event table — and 08 §Interfaces lists `TelemetryAlert` as this stream's exposed contract. The notification payload discriminator also differs: 07 §3 `{ type: 'metric', alert: IMetricAlertNotificationPayload }` vs 08 §T3 `{ type: 'telemetry_alert', alert: {...} }`. Both docs define an export named `zNotificationRuleMetricConfig` with different fields (07 has `enabled`, `mutedUntil`, `everySeconds`, `evaluationDelaySeconds`, `cooldownSeconds`, `repeatSeconds`, `notifyOnResolve`, `maxSeries`; 08 has only `threshold`, `comparator`, `forSeconds`, `intervalSeconds`). 11 D4 says it will not design the schema and then tests a third variant, referencing `fireCount` and `missingSeries: 'keep'` (§10 rows 14, and P1 property list) — both of which 07 D6 explicitly cut.

**Fix.** 07 owns alerting and 08 owns schema mechanics, so 07's model is authoritative on shape and 08 owns its migration. Replace 08 D5 and §5 wholesale with 07 §4's three models and migration SQL; 08 should keep only what is genuinely its own — the enum-ordering rule, the `pnpm codegen` requirement for the `/// [IPrisma…]` annotation, the cascade behaviour and the reaper's placement in `cron.insight-cleanup.ts` (which 07 §F7 also specifies, in a different job — reconcile). Standardise the payload discriminator on `'metric'` (07) and delete 08's `'telemetry_alert'`. Delete `zNotificationRuleMetricConfig` from 08 §T2 and point at 07 §2. Update 11 §10 to drop `fireCount` and `missingSeries: keep` (rows 14 and P-list) since 07 D6 cut them.

### C74. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The single irreversible P0 choice in the plan is decided in opposite directions by the two docs that own it. Doc 10 D3/D4 pre-creates `samples_v3` **and** `metrics_15s` with `type` in the `PARTITION BY` before gigapipe's first boot, and sets `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES=true` on every long-running node; doc 11 P0 MUST 3 pins exactly that posture as a gate. Doc 08 D7 explicitly **rejects** `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES=true` as the default (because `cmd/gigapipe/main.go:66-72` skips `ctrl.Init` and `ctrl.Rotate` together, so it also disables schema *upgrades*), and doc 08 Q6 records the pre-create as "Time-boxed to P0 … Default answer is no (D11); it needs a positive result to change". `PARTITION BY` cannot be `ALTER`ed, so this is available only before the first boot on every surface, and the two docs would produce different physical tables from the same P0 checklist.

**Fix.** Make this the first blueprint decision, gated on one spike (doc 10 U1 / doc 05 OQ2 / doc 11 OQ5 — all the same experiment: multi-clause conditional `TTL … DELETE WHERE` under `ttl_only_drop_parts = 1` over type-homogeneous partitions, at 24.3.2 and 26.1.3.52). If the spike passes, adopt doc 10 D4 and amend doc 08 D7/Q6; if it fails, adopt doc 08's position and accept doc 05's `+1 w` fallback. Whichever wins, doc 08 D7's real objection — that `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES` also disables schema upgrades — must be answered with a stated upgrade procedure, not left as a rejected alternative in a doc the ops stream is not reading.

### C75. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** The P0 test harness — 11's largest 'must' row and the thing it says must land before anything else — depends on an artifact another document explicitly rejected, and it does not exercise the production bootstrap. 11 D10 and §2.3 require `self-hosting/clickhouse/gigapipe-provision.sql`, mounted into a `tg-provision` container, and W7 asserts the test copy is byte-identical to the shipped one. 10-ops D3 explicitly **rejects** 'a third `op-gigapipe-provision` container mounting `gigapipe-provision.sql`', and 08 S6 puts the pre-create in `packages/db/code-migrations/22-telemetry-database.ts` running inside `op-api`'s startup command. So the shipped file will never exist and W7 can never pass. Worse: the test stack orders `tg-provision → tg-init`, while production orders `migration 22 inside op-api → op-gigapipe-init`. Gate 0.2 (both tables carry `type` in `PARTITION BY`) is therefore proved by a mechanism that does not ship, and the race that both 08 §10 and 05 §7.1 identify as the one-way door — migration 22 losing to gigapipe's `CREATE TABLE IF NOT EXISTS` — is untested.

**Fix.** Replace `tg-provision` in `test/telemetry/docker-compose.yml` with a container that runs migration 22 itself (a `jiti` invocation of the real code-migration against `tg-ch`), and rewrite W7 to assert the migration ran rather than asserting file identity with a file that will not exist. Delete `gigapipe-provision.sql` from 11's Interfaces 'needs from others' table.

## Major (118)

### M1. `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Nobody has added up the cost. Summing each document's own estimate: 01 ≈ 44 days, 02 ≈ 18.5 + 3–5 blocking, 03 ≈ 17–20, 04 ≈ 9.7 weeks (≈48), 05 ≈ 13 weeks (≈65), 06 ≈ 55–59, 07 ≈ 19, 08 ≈ 14–18, 09 ≈ 13.5 weeks (≈67), 10 ≈ 6–8 weeks (≈35), 11 ≈ 43. That is roughly **435 engineer-days ≈ 20 engineer-months**, before the blueprint reconciliation work above, before the overlap-vs-double-count correction, and before every document's own "what could make it bigger" section (which collectively add another 20–30 days of named risk: OTLP proto vendoring, the `op_org_id` decision, non-conforming project ids, a mid-stream gigapipe upgrade, clustered telemetry). For a company whose entire existing product is web + product analytics, this is a second product, and no single document says so. Each spec's estimate is individually honest and reads as tractable; the set is not.

**Fix.** Put the aggregate in the blueprint, then cut. My recommendation: ship **metrics only** for the first release — docs 01, 02 (metrics path only), 03, 04, 08, 09 (P2 slice), 10, 11 — and defer 05 (logs), 06 (traces + correlation) and 07 (alerts) entirely to a second release gated on adoption. That is roughly 55% of the cost, and it removes, as a side effect, the four largest cross-document conflicts in this review: the OTLP-logs-vs-Loki-JSON architecture split, the traces direct-SQL-vs-gigapipe split, the alert schema conflict, and most of the router-namespace conflict. It also puts the buy-vs-build question in a much better place, because a metrics-only product is exactly the surface where gigapipe's embedded Prometheus engine earns its keep and nothing else about gigapipe is load-bearing.

### M2. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The one decision both docs mark as unrepairable-after-the-fact has no owner and appears in no phase's deliverables. Doc 01 Q7 (org-scoping — does the label become a pair, `op_org_id` + `op_project_id`?) is marked "**DECISION REQUIRED BEFORE THE FIRST BYTE IS WRITTEN IN P1** … Adding `op_org_id` later changes the label set, therefore every fingerprint (D11), therefore splits every series in the database at the cutover", with owner given only as "product + tenancy work-stream lead". Doc 02 Q10 asks the same question with "before P1 ships" and defers to "read-path + billing work-streams". Doc 01's effort section prices reversal at "~4 days *if decided before P1*; effectively a data migration nobody can do if decided after". Neither doc's deliverable list, slice table, or test gate contains an item that forces the decision.

**Fix.** Move it into the P0 spike-week decision table with a named human and a date, alongside the database name, the constant name and the log storage shape. It costs nothing to decide and is unfixable to defer. The same treatment applies to doc 08 Q7 (`BigInt` end-to-end for metering) and doc 05 OQ7 (resource attributes dropped from `attr`), both of which are cheap now and structural later.

### M3. `00-blueprint.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** Effort is multiply counted and never rolled up, so the plan's true cost is unknown. Concrete overlaps: the OTLP protobuf codec is budgeted four times — doc 01 ("Vendoring OTLP `.proto` + protobufjs codecs for metrics/logs/traces — 8 days, the largest single item"), doc 02 (2 d vendoring + 3 d rewrite), doc 05 P3.4 (1.0 w, re-doing "vendored protos, protobufjs"), and doc 06 item 2 (`stampTraces`, 4 d). The gigapipe HTTP client is budgeted twice — doc 03 slice 3 (7 d, including limiter, breaker and negative cache) and doc 04's MVS (0.8 w for transport/errors/types/config/gate). The telemetry deletion path is budgeted five times — doc 01 (4 d), doc 02 (2-3 d, "owned elsewhere"), doc 06 item 10 (3 d), doc 08 §12 (2.5 d), doc 10 (inside its 10-14 d P2 block). Traces are budgeted twice on incompatible designs (doc 04 P4 2.0 w; doc 06 items 3-4, 11 d). Naive sum of the eleven totals is roughly 430 engineer-days (~86 engineer-weeks); no document states a total and the units are inconsistent (days in 01/02/03/06/07/08/11, weeks in 04/05/09/10).

**Fix.** Build one roll-up table in `00-blueprint.md` with a single owner per line item, in engineer-days, after the package-home and design conflicts above are settled — most of the double-counting is a symptom of those. Publish the de-duplicated total and the parallelism assumption before committing to a delivery date; the current per-stream estimates cannot be added.

### M4. `00-blueprint.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The single most load-bearing constant in the plan has three names across the specs. `TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL` appears in docs 01 and 04; `OP_PROJECT_LABEL` in docs 02, 03, 06 and 11; `PROJECT_LABEL` in doc 07 ("this spec's `PROJECT_LABEL` constant must be updated to agree"). Doc 06 T4 registers the correlation constants "beside `OP_PROJECT_LABEL`" citing `_drafts/ingest.md §4.5` — a superseded file. Doc 01 §1.1 gives the canonical definition with a docstring explaining that the value can never change because it participates in gigapipe's fingerprint. Because the constant is imported by the ingest stamper, all three query compilers, the response verifier, the alert evaluator and the test matrix, three names means either three constants or a merge conflict discovered at integration.

**Fix.** Fix the name in `00-blueprint.md`'s name registry — `TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL` in `@openpanel/constants` per doc 01 §1.1 is the one with the reasoning attached — and amend the other five docs. Do the same for the sibling constants doc 06 T4 introduces (`op_session_id` / `op_profile_id`) and re-point its `_drafts/ingest.md` citation at `02-ingest-gateway.md`.

### M5. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Environment-variable and kill-switch naming has fragmented across the documents, and it lands squarely on a hazard 10 D2 already identifies. There are three names for gigapipe's base URL (GIGAPIPE_URL in 01/03/04/06/07/08/09/10; GIGAPIPE_READ_URL in 05; GIGAPIPE_INTERNAL_URL in 02/11), three for its credentials (GIGAPIPE_LOGIN in 02; QRYN_LOGIN in 02/04/05/11; CLOKI_LOGIN in 01/10), two for the database (GIGAPIPE_DB in 04/05/06/10; TELEMETRY_CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE in 04), and four kill switches in three Redis namespaces (02: TELEMETRY_ENABLED plus telemetry:disabled:{projectId}; 04 D15: op:gp:off / op:gp:off:<projectId>; 01 §11: telemetry:ingest:enabled / telemetry:read:enabled; 05 D12: a telemetryEnabled boolean derived from GIGAPIPE_READ_URL). I verified in cmd/gigapipe/main.go:172-183 that gigapipe reads both QRYN_* and CLOKI_* with CLOKI winning, and that basic auth installs only when both username and password are non-empty — and 10 D2 already notes that Compose substitutes a missing .env key with the empty string plus a warning. So a mismatch between the compose service's variable name and the boot assertion's variable name yields a silently unauthenticated gigapipe, which is exactly the failure 10 D2 says network isolation exists to backstop.

**Fix.** Fix the names in the blueprint and have every document cite rather than restate. One URL (GIGAPIPE_URL), one credential pair (GIGAPIPE_LOGIN/GIGAPIPE_PASSWORD on the OpenPanel side, mapped to CLOKI_LOGIN/CLOKI_PASSWORD on the gigapipe service and nowhere else), one database name (GIGAPIPE_DB), and one kill-switch namespace with two independent keys for ingest and read (01 §11's split is the right shape — a read-enforcement incident must not stop correctly-stamped ingest). Keep 02's boot assertion that refuses to start when TELEMETRY_ENABLED is true and either credential is empty, and make 11 add a gate that the compose-set name and the asserted name are the same string.

### M6. `00-blueprint.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Roughly eight sub-day experiments gate five work-streams, are asked independently in up to four documents each, and no document owns the list. Duplicated questions: the `fastify-metrics` registry question ('does a bare `new client.Counter()` land in a register the plugin serves?') is 01 Q4, 02 Q5, 04 Q7 and 10 Q9 — four separate open questions for one 15-minute check (verified premise: `apps/api/package.json:49` declares `fastify-metrics` and no `prom-client`; `apps/worker/package.json:35` declares `prom-client`). Whether `TTL <expr> DELETE WHERE <cond>` parses on `clickhouse/clickhouse-server:25.10.2.65` in the two-comma-separated-rules form is 10 Q1 ('blocking §6 entirely. Do it first'), 11 Q3 and Q7, 08 U3 and 05 OQ2. The ClickHouse grants / `CREATE DATABASE` question is 04 Q6, 06 Q1 and 10 Q3. `LookbackDelta: 0` is 03 Q1 and 07 Q3. snappy prebuilds and framing are 02 Q2/Q3 and 05's P3.4b risk, and 02 says each 'can independently cost a week if discovered mid-implementation'. And 11 Q11 — Prisma's behaviour when an older client reads a `Client` row with the new `telemetry` enum value during a rolling deploy — is asked in exactly one document while gating P1's very first migration.

**Fix.** Put a single spike sheet at the top of `00-blueprint.md`, with one owner, a target of the first three days of P0, and one result row per experiment. Every other document replaces its duplicate open question with a citation of that row. Sequence the spikes ahead of migration 22, since three of them (TTL syntax, `MODIFY ORDER BY` no-op, grants) determine whether the one-way door can be taken at all.

### M7. `00-blueprint.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** P6 is a dumping ground larger than P3, and the plan's headline decision #3 has no estimate anywhere in it. Deferred to P6 across the specs: raw PromQL (doc 01 §8 — outline only, and its Effort section says "Raw PromQL (P6) is **not** included"); cardinality enforcement + volume-quota gate + OTLP/JSON (doc 02, 7 d); live tail over WebSocket, pattern grouping, saved log queries, cardinality/volume tiers, MCP `query_logs` (doc 05, 5.25 w ≈ 26 d); Services grid + `SparklineBars` + recipe registry + raw-PromQL affordances + MCP metric-report support + tail socket (doc 09, 3.0 w ≈ 15 d); the `fetch`/XHR patch and the replay lane (doc 06, 9 d); log-based alerts, project-wide silences, per-rule recipients (doc 07); Polar product metadata and per-signal tooltips (doc 10, 2-3 d); the 13-row R-table and MCP tests (doc 11, 4.5 d). That is ~65 engineer-days of enumerated work plus an unestimated raw-PromQL implementation — comparable in size to P3 — with no internal ordering and no user-facing narrative.

**Fix.** Split P6 into P6a (quotas, cardinality enforcement, metering completion — has a customer-visible consequence and a billing dependency), P6b (raw PromQL — must be estimated before it is a phase; doc 01 §8 and doc 11's R-table are the input) and P6c (polish: Services, live tail, patterns, MCP). Estimate P6b explicitly; it is one of the plan's five load-bearing decisions and currently the only one with no number attached.

### M8. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The structured spec cannot express PromQL vector matching, in v1 or in any planned phase, and no document says so. Verified in packages/db/src/engine/compute.ts:42-53: compute() groups series by a positional breakdown signature, `serie.name.slice(1).join(':::')` — an ordered tuple of label *values*, not label names. There is no on(), no ignoring(), no group_left/group_right. So the single most common non-trivial observability query — an error ratio joining two series with different label sets, e.g. sum by (route)(rate(errors)) / sum by (route,method)(rate(total)) — is inexpressible. 03 §14.4 shows a formula working only because both operands were built from the identical groupBy in the identical order, and 03 acknowledges the sibling hazard (independent phase-A ranking producing a formula with a missing denominator) and fixes it by intersecting pin sets, which is not the same thing as a join. Layered on top, 03 §0 excludes irate, deriv, quantile_over_time, stddev_over_time, topk/bottomk/quantile as aggregations, subqueries, offset, @, absent, label_replace and native histograms. The result is a query surface materially narrower than 'PromQL' offered to an audience defined by already running Prometheus — and 01 §10's evasion table names group_left as 'the one that bites' even in the P6 raw-PromQL phase.

**Fix.** State the ceiling explicitly in the blueprint's product section and in 03 §0's exclusion table: 'formulas are post-hoc arithmetic over aligned buckets with identical groupBy, not PromQL vector matching; on()/ignoring()/group_left are not expressible in any planned phase'. Then decide whether that is acceptable for the target user, and say so. If it is not, the honest options are to bring P6 raw PromQL forward (which 01 prices at 2.5–3.5 w plus Tier-2, and which is the phase where group_left becomes the hardest enforcement case) or to add an explicit two-series ratio primitive to zMetricQuery with a declared join-label list — a much smaller thing than raw PromQL and probably the right v1 answer, since error ratio and mean latency are the two queries every observability user writes first.

### M9. `00-blueprint.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The gigapipe ClickHouse database has two names in circulation.
