Docs 01, 04 and 08 use `openpanel_telemetry` (38 occurrences in doc 08 alone, including the migration file name `22-telemetry-database.ts` and `CREATE DATABASE IF NOT EXISTS openpanel_telemetry`); docs 05, 06, 10 and 11 use `gigapipe` (doc 10's provisioning file is `self-hosting/clickhouse/gigapipe-provision.sql`, its grants are `GRANT SELECT ON gigapipe.*`, and its runbook queries filter `WHERE database='gigapipe'`; doc 04 mixes both, using `openpanel_telemetry` in prose and `gigapipe.tempo_traces_attrs_gin` in SQL). The name reaches the compose env (`CLICKHOUSE_DB`), the provisioning SQL, the grants, every direct-SQL query in docs 04 and 06, the retention cron's `MODIFY TTL` statements, the deletion sweep, and the backup/DR runbook.

**Fix.** Pick one in the name registry and sweep all eight docs. `gigapipe` has more surfaces already written against it and is what the provisioning SQL and grants use; `openpanel_telemetry` is more self-documenting in a shared ClickHouse. Either is fine — silently shipping both is not, because the mismatch surfaces as gigapipe panicking into a `restart: always` loop on first boot (doc 10 notes `ctrl/ctrl.go:30-33` panics on DB-init failure).

### M10. `00-blueprint.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Self-instrumentation is routed to by name from two documents and specified by none. 06 Q7 asks whether OpenPanel's own api/worker traces should go into gigapipe and answers "Belongs to the self-instrumentation work-stream; flagged here because P4 benefits first, and because it is the only way T-15's fixtures get exercised by real traffic before a customer sends any." 01's Interfaces table (line 1865) already assumes one: "OpenPanel's own telemetry is a tenant like any other. Give it a real `Project` row on an internal organisation." 05 §12 half-implements it (a third `LOG_EXPORTER=loki` value on `packages/logger` pushing with an internal token, `source="op_self"`). 10 §10 *rejects* pointing self-telemetry at gigapipe for monitoring gigapipe (correctly — circular) and quotes `apps/api/src/utils/observability.ts:9-10` ("Metrics stay on prom-client/Grafana") as an architectural position it is not changing. So dogfooding — the cheapest possible way to exercise P1–P4 before a customer sends a byte — exists as three fragments with no owner, no phase and no budget. Two pieces of work inside it are also unowned: the OTel SDK flush that `apps/api/src/utils/graceful-shutdown.ts` lacks (it closes Fastify, MCP, Prisma, ClickHouse, BullMQ, Kafka and Redis then calls `process.exit()`, with no `forceFlush`, so the last span batch is lost on every deploy), and the internal Organization/Project row 01 requires.

**Fix.** Either write a short twelfth document or give 10-ops an explicit self-instrumentation section. It owns: the internal org/project row and its telemetry token; which signals go where (logs → gigapipe per 05 §12; traces → gigapipe as a P4 dogfood; metrics stay on prom-client, and say so as a deliberate exception to the plan's premise); the graceful-shutdown flush; and the rule that the monitoring of gigapipe itself never travels through gigapipe. Sequence it before P2 so P1–P4 have a real ingest client. ~1–1.5 weeks.

### M11. `00-blueprint.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`

**Problem.** There is no story for moving a customer off Datadog, Grafana Cloud, or a self-run Prometheus — no historical import, no dual-run guidance, no cutover plan — and two design decisions actively block the obvious paths. Datadog appears in the eleven docs only as a gigapipe endpoint OpenPanel refuses to expose (01:255, 02:74); Grafana appears only as a thing not to install. Yet the repo has a house pattern for exactly this: `packages/importer/src/providers/` contains amplitude, mixpanel and umami providers with fixtures, and `apps/public/content/guides/` ships `migrate-from-mixpanel.mdx` and `migrate-from-google-analytics.mdx`. Two blockers are already written into the plan. First, historical backfill is refused: 02 §7.1 (line 910) sets `TELEMETRY_MAX_BACKFILL` = 7 d and line 919 states "Backfill is **not** a supported use case in P1; if it becomes one it needs an explicit opt-in" — so a customer cannot bring a year of Prometheus data across, and the OpenPanel chart shows a cliff at cutover. Second, a customer's existing Grafana dashboards are unusable: raw PromQL is deferred to P6 behind a parser (parent decision 3, 03 D8), so every panel must be hand-rebuilt as a `zMetricQuery`, which is the single largest switching cost and nobody has costed it. The dual-run half is nearly free and merely undocumented — an OTel Collector fans out to two exporters with one added block in the YAML 02 §13.1 already prints.

**Fix.** Own this in 00-blueprint as a go-to-market requirement with three concrete deliverables, most of which are documentation rather than code. (a) A dual-run page: the two-exporter Collector config, plus the caution that both backends bill the same volume. (b) A cutover checklist: alert-rule parity, dashboard rebuild, retention overlap. (c) A decision on historical import — either state plainly that OpenPanel does not import history and dual-run for the retention window instead (cheap, honest, and consistent with 02's 7-day window), or add an opt-in backfill path, which is materially expensive because 02 §7.1's window exists to protect `samples_v3` part counts. A Grafana-dashboard-JSON to `zMetricQuery` converter is the highest-leverage optional item and is the strongest argument for pulling raw PromQL forward from P6. Documentation ~3 days; a converter is 1–2 weeks and should be scoped separately.

### M12. `00-blueprint.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `07-alerting.md`

**Problem.** Nothing in the set addresses moving a customer off an incumbent. "dual-run", "cutover" and "Grafana Cloud" have zero occurrences across all eleven documents; "Datadog" appears five times, always as a gigapipe ingest protocol being excluded or as a prior-art citation. Four specific things are missing. (1) **There is no historical import path, by design, and nobody has said so.** 02 D14 clamps every element's timestamp to `[now − TELEMETRY_MAX_BACKFILL_HOURS (default 24), now + skew]` and drops what falls outside — so a prospect with a year of Datadog metrics starts from an empty chart, and the only lever is a support decision to raise an env var (02:1215). That is a defensible design consequence; it is not stated anywhere as a migration constraint. (2) No dual-shipping guidance — the obvious evaluation path is an OTel Collector fanning out to both vendors, and 02 §16.1's snippet has a single exporter. (3) No Grafana dashboard or Prometheus alert-rule import: the plan's query surfaces are structured specs (`zMetricQuery`, `zNotificationRuleMetricConfig`), not PromQL, so there is nothing to import into until raw PromQL lands in P6. (4) No parity checklist telling a prospect what they give up — cumulative-only temporality, no native histograms, no exemplars, no raw PromQL until P6, no per-subject erasure, no backup (10 D22), 24 h backfill. In a category where every prospect already has a vendor, "start from zero and find out what's missing as you go" is the largest adoption risk in the plan and no document owns it.

**Fix.** Add a migration section owned jointly by product and 02/03: a dual-run collector recipe (fan-out exporter, both vendors, a comparison window), an explicit statement that historical backfill is capped and why, a documented process for temporarily raising `TELEMETRY_MAX_BACKFILL_HOURS` during a cutover, a written parity/limitations table, and a decision on whether Grafana-dashboard import is ever a goal. Most of it is docs and one collector config; the import question is a genuine feature decision. ~1–2 weeks.

### M13. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** 10 §3 declares the config naming "settled" and names 04 as the authority, but it is not settled and 10's own reconciliation is incomplete. Four incompatible spellings of the gigapipe base URL are in live use across the set: `GIGAPIPE_INTERNAL_URL` (02:1859, 02:61, 11), `GIGAPIPE_URL` (04, 10), and the pair `GIGAPIPE_READ_URL`/`GIGAPIPE_WRITE_URL` (05:1014-1015, where the read URL is also the feature's kill switch). The credential is `GIGAPIPE_LOGIN` (02:1860, 02:1901), `GIGAPIPE_USER` (05:650, 10:159) and `GIGAPIPE_USERNAME` (04:703, 04:751 — inside the config table of the document 10 calls the authority). The database is `GIGAPIPE_DB` (04/05/06/10), `TELEMETRY_CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE` (04:1899, 04:758) and `CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_DB` (08/10). Cluster: `GIGAPIPE_CLUSTER` (06/10) vs `GIGAPIPE_CLUSTER_NAME` (04:757). Retention: `GIGAPIPE_RETENTION_DAYS` (04:718) vs `TELEMETRY_RETENTION_DAYS` (10). On the container side 10:159 sets `CLOKI_LOGIN` while 02:1860/2131 sets `QRYN_LOGIN`. 10 §3's reconciliation paragraph names only 03 and 06 as needing edits — it misses 02, 05, and 04's own internal divergence.

**Fix.** Make 04 actually be the authority: one table in 04 listing every `GIGAPIPE_*` / `TELEMETRY_*` / `CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_*` name with its owner and default, then edit 02, 03, 05, 06, 08 and 10 to match, and land the CI grep 04 already proposes so a new spelling fails the build. Decide the read/write split explicitly — 05 needs two URLs only if the gateway and reader can point at different gigapipe nodes; if they cannot, collapse to `GIGAPIPE_URL`. Pick `CLOKI_*` or `QRYN_*` on the container once (both work; `CLOKI_*` wins at runtime) and state it. ~1 day plus the grep.

### M14. `00-blueprint.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** P1 ships nothing a user can see, and nothing that proves the plan's largest technical risk. P0 is stack; P1 is ingest. At the end of P1 a customer can point a collector at OpenPanel and observe absolutely nothing in the product — the first user-visible surface is P2's metrics explorer. Doc 10 states this plainly ("about two and a half of those before a single metric is queryable") and P0+P1 together are roughly 6-9 engineer-weeks before that. Worse, the risk every doc names as the biggest — whether `op_project_id` reaches a queryable series label on metrics (doc 09 §0 dep 4: "the single largest schedule risk and it is not in this stream's control"; doc 07: "every metric alert silently never fires"; doc 04: "+unbounded") — is only exercised by a read path that P1 does not build.

**Fix.** Fold doc 04's "minimum shippable slice — one metric chart renders" (its own estimate: ≈2.5 weeks, covering the client skeleton, `queryRange`/`queryInstant`, one procedure, the step ladder and the lease) into P1. P1 then exits with a customer's collector producing a rendered chart, which is a demoable increment, an end-to-end proof of the data-point stamping decision, and a working target for the P2 engine to be built against instead of a stub.

### M15. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** P1 as scoped cannot ship anything a user would notice, and 02's own rollout keeps it switched off. P1 delivers ingest routes registered only when `TELEMETRY_ENABLED` is true; 02 §17 says with the flag off 'no such row exists and a rollback is clean', and separately gates enablement on a deletion sweep no phase owns. The read path (04's 2.8 w minimum slice), the engine (03's 2 w minimum slice), the explorer (09 P2b) and `/settings/telemetry` with the collector snippet a customer needs in order to point an exporter at anything (09 P2c) are all P2. So a phase costing 5–7.5 w (02) plus ~4.9 w (01 Tier 1) plus ~1 w (10-ops P1) ends with a green CI job and nothing demonstrable — and no internal dogfooding either, since 06 Q7 records that OpenPanel's own OTLP tracing is gated on `HYPERDX_API_KEY` and has no SDK flush in `graceful-shutdown.ts`.

**Fix.** Redefine the first shippable increment as 'mint a telemetry token, point an OTel collector at it, see a gauge on a chart': 02's P1a restricted to metrics + remote-write, 01's Tier-1 ingest primitives, 04's 2.8 w minimum slice, 03's 2 w minimum slice, 09's P2b explorer and P2c settings tab. Ship it as one increment rather than two phases. The rest of 02 (P1b), 03 (full engine) and 04 (full stream) become follow-ons behind a working surface.

### M16. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** There is no volume or cardinality ceiling on telemetry ingest for four phases after the flag can be turned on. 02 D8 ships cardinality **observation only** in P1 and states the exposure plainly: a customer who puts a request id in a label creates 40 million series 'which lands in `time_series`, `time_series_gin` and `metrics_15s`, and which nobody can undo'. Cardinality enforcement, `budget(project)` (Q9) and the Postgres usage rollup are all in 02's explicit 'Not in P1' list. 10-ops D13 schedules the shed hook in its own P5, 'after both email templates (P4)', and 10 D15 notes telemetry is '~10³× the bytes of a comparable analytics workload'. The only ceilings present from P1 are 02 §7's per-request structural limits (20 000 series and 100 000 elements *per request*, which a stream of distinct trace ids defeats trivially) and a 1-day `@fastify/rate-limit` in P1b, which bounds request rate, not series count or bytes.

**Fix.** Either make 'the shed hook exists' an explicit gate on enabling telemetry for any tenant — alongside the deletion sweep — or add a crude per-project bytes-per-day and series-per-day ceiling to 02's P1b, enforced from the metering counters that P1b already writes. Do not let the first paying tenant arrive before one of the two exists.

### M17. `05-logs.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** P2 (metrics) and P3 (logs) are serialised for no stated reason, and P3 is by far the longest phase in the plan. 05 costs P3 at **13.5 engineer-weeks** single-threaded ('roughly three months for one engineer'), against 03's 5–6 w, 04's 6.4 w, 06's 5.6–6.8 w and 07's 3.6–4.2 w. But 05's own dependency table shows the coupling is thin: P3.1 (the LogQL compiler, envelope, severity, retention — 1.75 w) is blocked by nothing; P3.5 (the logs read path — 1.5 w) is blocked only by P3.1; only P3.2 is blocked by P1. The sole genuine overlap with metrics is the shared gigapipe transport in 04, which both consume. 05 already names an MVP slice (P3.1 + P3.2 + P3.3 + P3.5 + P3.6, ≈ 8.25 w) and says two engineers gets it to 8–10 weeks.

**Fix.** After 01's primitives and 04's transport merge, run logs and metrics as two concurrent lanes rather than consecutive phases. Recommend logs starting one sprint behind metrics — long enough for the transport contract to stabilise, early enough that the longest stream is not the schedule. State the concurrency explicitly in the blueprint, since 05's 13.5 w figure is what a naive reader will add to the serial total.

### M18. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The FinalChart convergence is real but materially smaller than the plan's headline, and the residual leaks are all in the worst class — confidently displayed wrong numbers. Verified: zero test files in the repo reference FinalChart, ChartEngine or executeChart (60 test files total), and apps/start is excluded from vitest.workspace.ts, so none of the equivalence is inherited. What it genuinely buys: the inner line/area/histogram/bar/pie/metric components, ReportTable, the tooltip, SerieName, GrafanaGrid layout and MCP's shapeChart. What it costs, documented across two specs but never totalled anywhere: a `data` prop across seven renderer index.tsx files (09 D2), a rewrite of useRechartDataModel from O(points² × series) (09 D21), SerieIcon suppression to avoid a favicon request storm on http_* metric names (09 D20), View Users suppression in three files (09 D17), an allowDecimals escape hatch or every sub-unit gauge renders as a flat zero line (09 D23), ReportTable moved off Redux (09 D18), and two out-of-band channels bolted on anyway because FinalChart cannot carry them (03 D2's notices supertype and 04 D5's IObservabilityChartResult.resolution). The three genuine leaks: IChartSerie.data[].count is `number`, not `number | null`, so 03 D10 fills gaps with 0 and a measured zero becomes indistinguishable from a missing sample — which is the single most important distinction in observability and the plan defers fixing it; metrics.sum is dimensionally meaningless for any rate or gauge series yet drives both format()'s sort order and bar/pie percent-of-total (03 §10.1); and metrics.count is always undefined so chartType 'metric' renders N/A (03 §10.3).

**Fix.** Restate decision 4 in the blueprint as the narrower claim the documents already support — 'the metrics engine returns FinalChart, so the chart components, the report table, the tooltip, the dashboard grid and the MCP shaper work unchanged; the fetch layer, the chartType gate, the persistence whitelists and the share plumbing are new code either way' — and put 09's six shared-component changes in one place so their cost is visible as a single number rather than six decisions. Pull the `count: number | null` widening into v1 for the metrics path specifically (03 D10 prices it at seven renderers, report-table-utils, the tooltip and the MCP shaper): a filled zero that looks like a measured zero is not a rendering nicety in an observability product, it is a wrong answer during an incident. Steer report.metric to 'average' for avg/last-reduced series and gate bar/pie on all-series-reducer-is-sum, per 03 Q5.

Each of these touches only existing code: (a) `pnpm typecheck` is commented out in the repo's only workflow — verified at `.github/workflows/docker-build.yml:124-128`, and `.github/workflows/` contains exactly one file — yet 11 D16, gate 2.0, gate 5.7 and 09 §7.2's eight-file union audit all rely on it as the mechanical enforcement, and 11 calls it 'promoted from an open question to a blocking P2 prerequisite'; its true cost is unknown until someone runs it. (b) 11 gate 2.1, `final-chart.contract.test.ts` green **against the event engine**, is called 'a prerequisite for *starting* P2' but tests only existing code. (c) 03 §15.2's release-N soak — the `transformReportEventItem` and `listReportsCore` metric arms — are pure pass-throughs with no behaviour change, and a full release boundary must elapse before the union member ships. (d) 09's P2a″ shared-component fixes: verified `apps/start/src/hooks/use-rechart-data-model.ts:19-52` is O(dates × series × points) with `...acc` spread-in-accumulator (which also violates this repo's own CLAUDE.md performance rule) and takes the x-axis from `series[0]` alone; plus `useYAxisProps.allowDecimals`, `Combobox` controlled search, `report-table` off Redux. (e) 09's P2a′ `data`/`isLoading`/`error` props — verified `ReportChartContextType` at `context.tsx:30-40` has no `data`, and `line/index.tsx:11-26` is exactly the five-line fetch wrapper 09 describes; 09 itself says 'Land it first, on its own, behind its own review.' (f) 04 D7's `packages/redis` refcount PR, 0.2 w, independently revertible.

**Fix.** Create a P0 'repo readiness' lane run concurrently by a second engineer: re-enable typecheck in CI and fix the fallout; land the event-engine FinalChart contract test; ship 03 §15.2's release-N pass-through arms immediately so the soak clock starts on day one; land the `data` prop and each shared-component fix as its own PR; land the redis refcount PR. None of these blocks on gigapipe, and (c) removes a full release boundary from P2's critical path.

### M20. `00-blueprint.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** At least nine spikes are scattered across the specs as "blocks X" prose with no owner, no slot and no shared fixture, and several of them gate irreversible P0 choices. The set: gigapipe boot against ClickHouse 24.3.2 / 25.10.2.65 / 26.1.3.52 (doc 10 U2/U3, doc 08 Q4 "highest-risk P0 unknown", doc 05 OQ4, doc 11 P0 MUST 2); conditional TTL under `ttl_only_drop_parts=1` (doc 10 U1, doc 05 OQ2, doc 11 OQ5); `samples_v3` sort key (doc 05 OQ1, doc 10 U6 — "must be answered before P3.0, since the key is unchangeable afterwards", but `ADVANCED_SAMPLES_ORDERING` is set at gigapipe boot, i.e. P0); `op_project_id` as a data-point attribute end-to-end (doc 07, doc 09 dep 4); PromQL `=~` anchoring (doc 03 OQ1); offset-window acceleration (doc 03 OQ4); exemplar attributes becoming labels (doc 11 OQ2); busybox `base64`/`wget` in the image (doc 10 U4); Compose `service_completed_successfully` availability (doc 10 U12). Each is half a day to two days; all but two run against the same scratch stack.

**Fix.** Create an explicit spike week at the head of P0 with one owner and one deliverable — a written answer table committed into `00-blueprint.md`. Run them against a single scratch gigapipe + ClickHouse fixture (doc 11 §8 already specifies `.github/gigapipe/{docker-compose.yml,up.sh}`, so build that first). Nothing that depends on an irreversible answer — the pre-create, the sort key, the log storage shape, clustering — starts until the table is filled in.

### M21. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `05-logs.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** No document specifies the tenancy contract for telemetry through MCP, and MCP is where the ClientType widening becomes a read path. Every mention of MCP in the set is a schema concern: doc 03 §3 (`reportSchema` already accepts `dataSource`), doc 08 (the `.strict()` schema), doc 11 (rows 640, 1496, 1658). But doc 03 §13.3 records that `runReportFromConfig` takes an LLM-supplied config with **no DB row**, which is why the discriminator must live on the input schema — meaning a model-authored config decides which engine runs and against which report shape. Doc 05 §Interfaces exposes `compileLogQuery` to "the P6 MCP `query_logs` tool", and doc 04 §Interfaces exposes the whole `@openpanel/gigapipe` read surface to "P6 MCP". Meanwhile `packages/mcp/src/auth.ts:99` denies only `write` and then returns `clientType: isRoot ? 'root' : 'read'`, coercing any unrecognised type to a full analytics reader — the same coercion doc 02 §2.2 flags at `insights.controller.ts:85-93`. So a telemetry credential (or, after doc 08's rollback, a demoted one) reaching MCP becomes a telemetry reader, and no document states that MCP telemetry tools must derive `projectId` server-side from the authenticated client rather than from a tool argument.

**Fix.** Write the MCP contract once in the blueprint and reference it: telemetry MCP tools derive `projectId` from the authenticated MCP client's scope, never from a tool argument; a `telemetry`-type credential is rejected by MCP auth (the allow-list conversion, tested); and `runReportFromConfig` re-validates `dataSource` and the series union server-side because it has no DB row to trust (doc 03 already requires this for `executeReport` — say it for MCP too). Add a row to doc 11's P6 gate asserting a telemetry-derived credential cannot read metrics, logs or traces through any MCP tool.

### M22. `00-blueprint.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `07-alerting.md`

**Problem.** P6's MCP and agent telemetry tools are referenced by four documents as somebody else's job and specified by none. The brief lists "P6 polish (MCP tools, quotas, raw PromQL)": quotas are owned by 10 §9, raw PromQL by 01 §10, MCP tools by nobody. The references are concrete enough to show real work exists — 04:2486 imposes a hard constraint ("`getMetricCatalog`, `getMetricLabelKeys`, … perform no authorization. P6's MCP tools must either call the router or run their own `requireProjectAccess` first — 7.3's deletion-containment argument depends on it") and 04:1707 repeats it as a prohibition; 05:1862 and 05:2007 name a `query_logs` tool and cost it at 0.5 w inside a deferred list; 06:29 says traces are "consumed by P6 (MCP trace tools)"; 07:2576 wants "a future MCP 'what is firing right now' tool". Nobody owns the tool surface, the schemas, or the response-size bounds — which matter, because commit `0c7a087a` ("fix(mcp): bound tool response sizes with columnar tables and rollups") shows the team has already been burned by unbounded MCP payloads, and logs are the largest payload in the product. The in-app agent (`apps/api/src/agents/`) is likewise unaddressed for telemetry.

**Fix.** Give P6's MCP surface an owner and a short spec covering the tool list (metric query, log query, trace lookup, what-is-firing), the mandatory `requireProjectAccess` boundary 04 names, response-size bounding in the style of `0c7a087a`, and whether the in-app agent gets the same tools. ~1.5–2 weeks, and it should be scoped now because 04's authorization constraint is a design input to the metadata service, not a P6 afterthought.

### M23. `00-blueprint.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** gigapipe's residual role is far smaller than the plan's framing, and the adopt-versus-build decision was never re-run against it. After the individual work-streams finished their analyses, the plan uses: gigapipe's OTLP-metrics decoder, its Loki-JSON writer, its trace writer, its PromQL→ClickHouse accelerated planner and its LogQL→ClickHouse transpiler. Everything else is bypassed as unsafe or useless — every metadata endpoint (04 D3, seven findings), the entire Tempo reader (06 T1/T2), the OTLP log ingest path (05 D1), detected_fields (05 D7, a stub), the tail (05 D5, three disqualifying properties), the ruler (10 D23), the UI, profiles, and roughly twenty routes blocked outright (01 D9). Meanwhile OpenPanel takes on: an AGPL-3.0 Go dependency, a protobuf decode/mutate/re-encode gateway, pre-creation of gigapipe's own tables (08 S6), its retention (10 D9), its deletion (08 S15), its cardinality problems (05 D3, D8's ungateable per-line pattern clusterer), and a per-bump contract-test harness (02 §5.6) because every rule in the ingest spec is read off gigapipe internals.

**Fix.** Write the residual-value paragraph into the blueprint and revisit it at each phase boundary. The three writers are perhaps 2–3 weeks of TypeScript against tables the plan is already pre-creating, already applying TTL to, and already deleting from — so if a phase boundary ever finds the two transpilers are also being worked around, the dependency has stopped paying for itself. Concretely: define a kill criterion now ('if P2 finds we are compiling around the PromQL planner rather than through it, stop'), and record 11 Q14's decommissioning path as a blueprint item rather than a testing-strategy open question. This is not an argument to abandon gigapipe — the accelerated PromQL planner over metrics_15s is genuinely hard to reproduce and is worth the dependency — it is an argument to say out loud that it is the *only* thing worth the dependency.

Do not ship both.' 05 has not been updated and still writes packages/db/src/gigapipe/logql.test.ts (§2), packages/db/src/gigapipe/envelope.ts (§3.2), severity.ts (§3.1) and cardinality.ts (§3.5) throughout. 11 Q1 records it as blocking 'before anyone writes a test file'. 05's own open question 8 lists the shared gigapipe client as 'still unowned'.

Then do the mechanical rename pass across 05 and 11 in one commit, and add the answer to 11 Q1 so the test files can be written.

### M25. `00-blueprint.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** A substantial amount of work with zero dependency on gigapipe is trapped inside later phases, artificially lengthening the critical path. Verified examples: (a) the `FinalChart` conformance suite — doc 11 P2 MUST 1 says it "ships *before* the metrics engine, not with it" and doc 04 confirms nothing pins that shape today (I checked: `packages/db/src/engine/` contains only `formula.test.ts`), yet it sits inside P2; (b) doc 09's P2a prerequisites (`FormulaSeriesItem` extraction, `PureFilterItem` `values` prop, `ReportChartType` `allow`, `SerieIcon` `resolve`) are refactors of existing event-report UI, 1.0 week, no telemetry dependency; (c) the four `ClientType` allow-list conversions are a live pre-existing security defect — I verified `apps/api/src/controllers/export.controller.ts:26-30` guards with `request.client?.type === ClientType.read`, so any new enum value skips the cross-project check entirely — and they can ship as a standalone fix today; (d) the `publisher.ts` refcount, which doc 04 D8 correctly calls a prerequisite: I verified `subscribeToPublishedEvent` (`packages/redis/publisher.ts:45-70`) unsubscribes unconditionally and has four call sites (`live.controller.ts:41,88,130,172`), so two tabs on the visitors page already break each other — it is scheduled in P3; (e) doc 06 items 7, 8 and 12 (SDK propagation, span processor, customer docs — 12 of its 55 days) depend on nothing in this plan and would de-risk its O3 dependency question early; (f) Caddy `/api/metrics` auth — I verified `self-hosting/caddy/Caddyfile.template:4-6` strips the prefix, so `https://$DOMAIN/api/metrics` serves prom-client unauthenticated today.

**Fix.** Move all six into P0 as a parallel track. None needs gigapipe, a spike answer, or a phase decision; three are pre-existing bugs worth fixing regardless; and the FinalChart suite must exist before the metrics engine is written or decision #4's "same type" guarantee is unenforced. This shortens P2 and P3 without adding headcount.

### M26. `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** PII and secret redaction on log bodies and span attributes is flagged as a known gap and assigned to nobody. 05's Open questions item 12 (line 2230) reads: "**PII scrubbing.** `Project.filters` (ip / profile_id) is honoured by `validateSdkRequest` for events; nothing applies it to log bodies, and no redaction exists. | Product decision. Out of scope for P3 as specified — stated here so it is a known gap, not an oversight." I verified both halves: `packages/db/prisma/schema.prisma:269` has `filters Json @default("[]")` on Project, and `apps/api/src/utils/auth.ts:99,107` applies an `ip` filter and a `profile_id` filter — for the analytics track path only. No document picks the question up. The consequence compounds with two other decisions in the set. 08 §12 establishes that per-subject erasure is not expressible, so a secret or an email address in a log line cannot be surgically removed once stored — the only remedies are the retention window or deleting the whole project. And 08 §12's `tempo_traces_kv` analysis shows that span attribute *values* survive project deletion in a cross-tenant dictionary for up to `TELEMETRY_TRACES_RETENTION_DAYS`. So "we will scrub later" is not a neutral deferral: everything ingested before the scrubber exists is unremediable at row level. This is also the single most common real-world observability incident — a customer logs an Authorization header — and it will happen in month one, not month three.

**Fix.** Assign it to 05 (it owns the log envelope and is where `Project.filters` would be honoured) with 02 contributing the attribute-level pass for traces, and ship the minimum version in P3 rather than deferring: (a) honour the existing `Project.filters` `ip` rule against the log envelope and against span attributes at the gateway, reusing the `auth.ts:99` logic so there is one definition of the rule; (b) a fixed denylist of attribute keys dropped unconditionally (`authorization`, `cookie`, `set-cookie`, `x-api-key`, `password`), which is what every collector distribution ships by default; (c) documentation pointing customers at the Collector's own `redaction` and `attributes` processors as the real answer, since scrubbing at the source is both cheaper and more complete than scrubbing at our edge. About 2–3 days for (a) and (b). The operator-facing remedy when it happens anyway — shorten the project's retention, or delete and re-ingest — belongs in the runbook alongside the tenancy remediation in 01.

### M27. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `05-logs.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The operator's monitoring story does not exist for a self-hosted install. 02, 03, 05, 07 and 10 between them define a substantial operator alerting surface — `op_telemetry_rejected_total{signal,reason}`, the twelve stack-health gauges at 10 §10.1 (`gigapipe_up`, `gigapipe_ttl_applied_age_seconds`, `op_ch_disk_free_ratio`, `gigapipe_metric_fanout_ratio`, `telemetry_ingest_disabled`, …), 07's `metric_alert_stale_rules`, 05 §8.2's pipeline metrics — all exported through `apps/worker`'s prom-client registry. Verified: there is no Prometheus or Grafana anywhere in `self-hosting/` (a case-insensitive grep for prometheus/grafana/`/metrics` over that whole tree returns nothing), the compose template's seven services are proxy/db/kv/ch/api/dashboard/worker, and `/metrics` is not routed through `Caddyfile.template`. 05's Effort table assigns "the operator-alert destination and the Prometheus scrape of gigapipe's own `/metrics` (behind Basic auth)" to **P0**; 10's P0 line item is the two CH experiments, the `get_latest_images` fix, two compose services, `.env`/`quiz.ts`, dev compose, smoke stack and the docs page — the destination is not there, and neither P6 ("stack-health cron, disk guard, kill-switch writer") nor P7 picks it up. So every gauge and every alert threshold in the set has no consumer on a self-hosted install, and the product whose pitch is "you don't need Grafana" cannot observe itself without one.

**Fix.** 10-ops takes the handoff 05 already made. Either (a) ship an optional `op-prometheus` profile in the compose template with the alert rules the five documents imply, plus routing `/metrics` behind auth, or (b) state explicitly in `self-hosting/observability.mdx` that self-hosters get gauges on `apps/worker:9999/metrics` and must bring their own scraper, and give them the rule file. Then say which one Cloud uses. ~0.5–1 week.

### M28. `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The plan's stated differentiator — correlating telemetry back to OpenPanel sessions and profiles — is reachable only from JavaScript, while OpenPanel documents SDKs for six backend languages it cannot serve. `apps/public/content/docs/(tracking)/sdks/` contains `python.mdx`, `ruby.mdx`, `rust.mdx`, `java.mdx`, `kotlin.mdx` and `swift.mdx`. Both halves of the correlation mechanism in 06 §8 are JS-only: `packages/sdks/sdk/src/propagation.ts` (06:889) emits the `baggage` header from the browser, and `packages/logger/src/otel.ts` (06:994) is a TypeScript `SpanProcessor` implementing `@opentelemetry/sdk-trace-base`'s interface that copies `op_session_id`/`op_profile_id` onto the local root span. A Django or Rails or Spring backend receiving that `baggage` header gets nothing: no equivalent processor is specified, and a grep of all eleven documents for python, ruby, java, kotlin, swift or rust in an instrumentation context returns zero hits. This is not a small omission — 06 T6's own rationale for cutting the automatic `fetch` patch is that a customer "must edit their init code regardless", which is exactly as true for a Python customer, and the receiving side is genuinely simple in every OTel SDK because `baggage` is in every default composite propagator. The gap is a missing recipe, not a missing capability.

**Fix.** 06 should own a section "receiving the correlation ids in a non-JS backend" with a ten-line recipe per language, written against the standard OTel API rather than an OpenPanel package: read `op_session_id`/`op_profile_id` from baggage in a `SpanProcessor`/`on_start` hook (Python `opentelemetry.baggage.get_baggage`, Java `Baggage.current()`, Ruby `OpenTelemetry::Baggage.value`) and `set_attribute` them on the local root span. Ship Python and Java first — they are the two languages a Datadog-refugee backend is most likely to be. This is roughly 2–3 days of writing plus a test app per language, and it should be in P4 alongside the JS processor rather than deferred, because shipping the differentiator for one language and calling it a platform feature is the kind of thing that surfaces as a support ticket in month three.

### M29. `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The stated differentiator — session correlation — is not reachable in the form the plan advertises, and P4 as specified ships documentation rather than a mechanism. `grep -rn "propagateSession|getBaggage" packages/ apps/` returns nothing: neither exists. 06 T6 refers to `propagateSession` as though it were an existing option ("`propagateSession` defaults to `false`, so a customer must edit their init code regardless") and then, on that basis, demotes the automatic `fetch`/`XHR` patch — the thing that would actually make correlation happen without customer work — to "a stretch goal and the first thing cut". What ships is `op.getBaggage()` plus documentation. The customer must then thread a `baggage` header through their own request wrapper *and* run a span processor in their backend to lift it onto the root span (T5). Two further constraints compound it: T10 documents that `fetchDeviceId` returns `sessionId: ''` for a brand-new device (verified at apps/api/src/controllers/track.controller.ts) — i.e. no correlation on the first page view of every new visitor, which is a large fraction of the sessions anyone wants to debug — and the server mints a *deterministic bucketed* session id (apps/api/src/utils/ids.ts:127-134), so an SDK-held id can disagree with the id the server assigns to concurrent events at a bucket boundary.

**Fix.** Either fund the fetch/XHR patch as core P4 work with its hazard list treated as engineering rather than as a reason to cut (double-wrapping, a pre-existing `baggage` from the customer's own OTel SDK, already-patched `fetch` from Sentry/Datadog/Next.js, `Request`-body handling, late script load), or stop describing session correlation as the differentiator and describe it as a documented integration. A differentiator that requires the customer to write both halves of the propagation is a tutorial, not a moat. Also fix T6's phrasing so `propagateSession` is presented as a new option, and resolve the cold-start and bucket-boundary cases before P4 rather than deferring both to P6.

### M30. `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** 06's design forces a coordinated npm release across every published OpenPanel SDK, and nobody has costed the release mechanics. 06 §11.2 modifies `packages/sdks/sdk/src/index.ts` — the base package every other SDK extends — and §11.3 creates a brand-new published package `@openpanel/otel` at `packages/sdks/otel/`. Verified against `tooling/publish/`: `loadPackages` (publish.ts:82-105) reads *every* directory under `packages/sdks/` and parses its `package.json`, so a half-finished `otel/` directory is picked up by the next release of any SDK — and a malformed or version-less package.json breaks the release entirely. `findDependents` (:107-) walks reverse dependencies, and `@openpanel/sdk` is the root of the tree (`web` → `sdk`; `nextjs`/`astro`/`nuxt` → `web`; `express` and `react-native` → `sdk`), so touching the base bumps and republishes **seven** packages. `generate-readme.ts:217-228` skips README generation unless `package.json.config.docPath` names an existing mdx file (the convention is `apps/public/content/docs/(tracking)/sdks/express.mdx`, `packages/sdks/express/package.json:7`), so `@openpanel/otel` cannot ship a README without a docs page first. And every dependent pins the base with `"@openpanel/sdk": "workspace:1.3.1-local"`, which the release script rewrites — those pins must move in lockstep. None of this appears in 06's effort table, which prices the SDK piece at "2–3 d".

**Fix.** 06 (or the SDK owner) adds a release section: register `@openpanel/otel` with a `config.docPath`, write the docs page it points at, decide the peer-dependency support matrix for `@opentelemetry/api` / `@opentelemetry/sdk-trace-base` (`^1.30.1 || ^2.0.0`) and test against both majors, and plan the seven-package coordinated bump — including what happens to a customer on an old `@openpanel/web` who upgrades only one. ~3–5 days on top of the current estimate.

### M31. `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The session-correlation differentiator — the thing the whole build is justified by — is behind four opt-ins and a retention wall, and nothing totals the funnel. From 06 §11: propagateSession.enabled defaults to false (§11.6, deliberately, and correctly, because it sends a user identifier into a backend telemetry store); patchFetch defaults to false and 06's own effort table lists it as 'cut first'; the customer must install a new published @openpanel/otel package and register a span processor (§11.3, and 06 correctly notes packages/logger cannot ship it because it has no build and no publishConfig); the `baggage` header is not CORS-safelisted so any cross-origin call needs the customer's own Access-Control-Allow-Headers (§11.4); the first request of every new session carries no session id because this.sessionId is populated from the /track response (§11.1, 'cold start is accepted, not closed'); and traces inherit gigapipe's SAMPLES_DAYS-derived TTL — 7 days on 10 D9's cloud defaults — while sessions are retained far longer, so 'traces for this session' is empty for the majority of sessions a user would click (§11.6, 'the session page renders traces are retained for N days, not no traces'). Each of these is individually honest and correctly argued. The plan's framing — 'the differentiator no Grafana stack can offer' — never multiplies them.

**Fix.** Before committing to P4, prototype the funnel end to end against one real design partner: their SDK version, their collector, their actual cross-origin topology, and measure how many of the five steps they complete unassisted. Record the result in the blueprint next to decision 5. Two cheap changes would move the numbers materially and should be evaluated in that prototype: raise the trace retention default above 7 days for correlated traces specifically (or state the asymmetry on the session page, which 06 already requires), and reconsider whether patchFetch being off by default is the right trade given it is the step most likely to be skipped — 06's hazard list for it is real but every item on it is a known, solved problem in the RUM industry. If the prototype shows the funnel does not convert, decision 5 stands as a design but stops being the justification for the schedule, and the metrics-only cut becomes clearly correct.

### M32. `08-schema-changes.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Doc 08 uses `P1`–`P4` as its own internal migration numbering, and those labels collide with the parent phase names in a way that will misroute work. Its §2 is "P1 — `ClientType += telemetry`" (parent P1, correct), §3 "P2 — `Report.dataSource`" (parent P2, correct), §4 "**P3** — telemetry metering on `Organization`" (parent P3 is *logs*; metering is doc 10's P1/P2 and doc 02's P6), and §5 "**P4** — `TelemetryAlert`" (parent P4 is *traces*; alerts are parent P5). Its Inventory table repeats the same labels, and its Effort table is broken down by them. A reader planning the logs phase who opens doc 08 §4 finds metering; a reader planning traces finds the alert schema.

**Fix.** Renumber doc 08's sections to `M1`–`M4` (migrations) or relabel them with the correct parent phases, and align the migration directory timestamps accordingly. Also reserve migration numbers centrally: `packages/db/code-migrations/` already contains duplicate prefixes today (two `20-` and two `21-` files), and doc 08 claims `22-telemetry-database.ts` with no reservation mechanism against doc 10's ClickHouse work.

### M33. `00-blueprint.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Only self-hosting operator documentation is owned; product documentation and the public site are unowned. The docs set collectively names exactly three mdx files, all under `apps/public/content/docs/self-hosting/`: `environment-variables.mdx`, `high-volume.mdx`, `deploy-kubernetes.mdx` (10 §4.5 table, lines 480–482). A grep for `content/docs` across all eleven docs, excluding self-hosting, returns nothing. What that leaves unowned, all of which exists today for analytics: `apps/public/content/docs/meta.json`, which is a hand-maintained nav array and would need an `---Observability---` section and a `...observability` entry; a getting-started page and per-language instrumentation pages (the analytics equivalent is fourteen files under `docs/(tracking)/sdks/` plus seventeen under `content/guides/`); a query-reference page for `zMetricQuery`/`zLogQuery`, which matters more than usual precisely because raw PromQL/LogQL/TraceQL are all refused (parent decision 3, 05 L4, 06 T7) so users have no standard language to fall back on; an alerting guide; `docs/self-hosting/changelog.mdx`; and the marketing surfaces `apps/public/src/app/(content)/features`, `(content)/pricing` and `src/lib/features.ts`. The pricing page is a single-dimension "events per month" slider (`apps/public/src/components/pricing-slider.tsx` over `PRICING` from `@openpanel/payments/prices`, with `eventsLimit` metadata written into Polar products by `packages/payments/scripts/create-products.ts:98`), so the commercial packaging of telemetry units — bundled, second slider, or add-on — is an unmade decision that 10 defers to a P6 line (`telemetryUnitsLimit` on Polar products). 09 §10 does specify the in-app onboarding page, which is the only user-facing writing anyone owns.

**Fix.** Add a documentation-and-GTM work item, owned by whoever owns 09 (it has the copy already) with 10 contributing the operator half. Scope it honestly by adopting 09's own instinct at §10 — it cut six instrumentation recipes down to one Collector snippet because five would go stale — so: one getting-started page, one Collector page, one query-reference page, one alerting page, the `meta.json` nav entry, a changelog entry, and a pricing/packaging decision recorded in 00-blueprint. Roughly 5–8 days of writing spread across P2–P5, plus a separate product decision on packaging that should be made before P2 rather than at P6, because the metering weights in 10 §10.1 are already marked UNMEASURED and the price depends on them.

### M34. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Nothing owns proving that this plan does not break the analytics product that pays for it. Two verified facts frame the risk: `vitest.workspace.ts` is `export default ['packages/*', 'apps/*', '!apps/start']` — the dashboard is excluded from the test run entirely — and `pnpm typecheck` is commented out in CI (`.github/workflows/docker-build.yml:124-128`, alongside a commented-out Biome step). 11 D16 identifies both, correctly promotes re-enabling typecheck to a blocking P2 prerequisite, and then declines to stand up a front-end suite ("a half-day-plus of config nobody budgeted, and the first thing it would test is not where the risk is"). But the plan modifies a great deal of shared, currently-untested analytics code: `ReportChartProps` gains `data`/`isLoading`/`error` across eight `<ReportChart>` render sites (09 D2); `useRechartDataModel` is rewritten to a single `Map` pass "before any metric surface ships" (09 D21); `Combobox` and `ComboboxAdvanced` gain controlled search and "move out of reused unchanged" (09 D22); `useYAxisProps` gains an `allowDecimals` escape hatch (09 D23); `PureFilterItem` is extended (09 D9); plus nine report-persistence whitelist sites (08 §3), `zReportInput`, `validateExportRequest` (which also gates `/insights`), `cacheMiddleware`, and two notification unions. Any of these regressing silently degrades the existing product, and the only safety net proposed is a typecheck gate plus some tRPC tests.

**Fix.** 11-testing takes the decision back: either stand up the `apps/start` Vitest project D16 declined and cover the five shared components the plan mutates, or specify an equivalent `createCaller`-style regression pack over the existing *event* report paths (chart.chart, chart.aggregate, report create/update/duplicate round-trip, share, MCP `create_report`/`update_report`/`duplicate_report`, `/export/charts`, `/insights`) that fails if a metric-shaped change alters an event-shaped result. Either way it is a named deliverable with a phase, not a decision to skip. ~1 week.

### M35. `00-blueprint.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Scope versus capacity is not addressed anywhere. Summing the documents' own estimates: 01 ≈4.9 w (+2.5–3.5 P6), 02 ≈5–7.5 w, 03 ≈5–6 w (+2–3 P2.5), 04 ≈6.4 w, 05 ≈13.5 w (+5.5 P6), 06 ≈5.6–6.8 w (+2.4 P6), 07 ≈3.6–4.2 w, 08 ≈2.4 w, 09 ≈8–9 w, 10 ≈7–8.5 w, 11 ≈5.7 w. That is roughly 70 engineer-weeks of core work and 85–95 including the named P6 items — and it excludes the load test (10), the P6 differential corpus (11), and every 'what could make this bigger' contingency, several of which are individually multi-week. Meanwhile `git log --since='12 months ago'` shows 588 commits of which 548 are from one author. P1 (ingest) hard-blocks P2, P3, P4 and P5, so the phases do not parallelise for a solo maintainer even in principle. At one engineer this is 18–24 months during which the analytics product that pays for it receives no attention.

**Fix.** State the number in the blueprint and make the cut an explicit decision rather than a discovery. My recommendation is a metrics-only v1: P0 + the metrics half of P1 + P2 + a metrics-only P2.5 + the metrics half of 09 + 10's P0/P1 + 11's irreducible core, which is roughly 28–30 weeks and ships a real product. Logs (13.5 w) and traces (6–7 w) and their correlation surfaces become a second release with their own go/no-go. Metrics is the right first cut for two reasons that are not preference: it is the only signal where gigapipe's residual value (the accelerated PromQL planner) is actually load-bearing, and it is the only one that tests the FinalChart bet, which is the plan's largest leverage claim. If demand forces logs first instead, say so and accept that the FinalChart bet goes untested for a year.

### M36. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** The telemetry auth design lets an unauthenticated caller write unbounded keys into a `noeviction` Redis shared with BullMQ, which kills event ingestion. Both docs cache the secret-verification result under a key containing an attacker-chosen value: doc 01 §3.2 `telemetry:auth:${clientId}:${sha256(secret)}`, doc 02 §2.1 the same with a 16-hex truncation. I verified `getCache` (packages/redis/cachable.ts:16-58) has no `shouldCache` guard and unconditionally `setex`es the result — doc 02 correctly notes the negative result is cached, but neither doc notices that this makes the *key space* attacker-controlled. Doc 02's pre-auth limiter is `TELEMETRY_IP_RPM = 1200`, so one IP writes 1200 new 300-second keys per minute; a distributed source is unbounded. self-hosting/docker-compose.template.yml:52 runs `redis-server --maxmemory-policy noeviction`, under which memory pressure makes *writes* fail — and doc 02 §7.4's own "Redis blast radius" paragraph explains that this kills BullMQ event ingestion. That paragraph mitigates only the cardinality keys (`TELEMETRY_REDIS_URL`, per-project caps, fail-open); the auth keys are on the same instance and are the ones reachable before authentication. Doc 01 §3.2 spots the adjacent problem — the shared 5000-entry process LRU being evicted by a guessing loop — and names the rate limiter as the mitigation, which does not bound the Redis side.

**Fix.** Do not key the cache on caller-supplied secret material. Cache the negative decision per `clientId` with a small counter (`telemetry:authfail:{clientId}`, capped, short TTL) and cache only the positive verification, keyed on `clientId` plus a hash of the *stored* secret. Route telemetry auth caching through `TELEMETRY_REDIS_URL` (doc 02 already introduces the var) so the blast radius genuinely excludes the queue instance, and state in both docs that every telemetry Redis write fails open. Add a test that N failed authentications with N distinct secrets create O(1), not O(N), Redis keys.

### M37. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** The two ingest specs define the reserved namespace with different semantics, different constant names, and different module homes — and each has tests asserting behaviour the other forbids. 01 reserves the whole `op_` **prefix** via `TELEMETRY_RESERVED_LABEL_PREFIX`, so `op.latency` is stripped; its worked alias table and test T1.4 assert exactly that, and §2 explains it as deliberate namespace ownership. 02 reserves an explicit **set** of three names (`op_project_id`, `op_organization_id`, `__ttl_days__`) via `OP_TELEMETRY_RESERVED_LABELS`, under which `op_latency` passes through untouched. The constants also differ (`TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL` vs `OP_PROJECT_LABEL`) and the code lives in different places: 01 puts every primitive in a new `packages/gigapipe` workspace package, 02 D9 puts the gateway in `apps/api/src/telemetry/` with the sanitizers in `packages/constants` and the vendored protos in `apps/api/src/telemetry/proto/`, while 01 puts them in `packages/gigapipe/vendor/opentelemetry-proto/`. 04 D1 then arbitrates a *third* layout (`packages/gigapipe` split by layer between two owners) that neither 01 nor 02 reflects. Smaller divergences on the same path: the auth cache key is a full SHA-256 (01) vs a 16-hex truncation (02); OTLP body limits are 16 MiB (01) vs 4 MiB (02); the wind-down block answers 429 + `Retry-After: 3600` (01) vs 403 (02).

**Fix.** One owner for the primitives. 04 D1's layer split is the most thought-through arbitration — adopt it, publish the constants once, and pick the prefix semantics (01's is safer and its `op.latency` strip is already justified as a dashboard signal rather than a bug). Reconcile the body limit against 02's memory arithmetic in §6, which is the only place the number is actually derived. Reconcile the wind-down status against 02's argument that OTLP clients do not retry 403 and 01's that 429 makes senders back off — these are opposite intents (permanent refusal vs backpressure) and the product must choose.

### M38. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** Two label-shape decisions that change stored data are explicitly deferred past the point where they become expensive, and one is deferred by design. 01 Q5 asks whether telemetry tokens need to be org-scoped as well as project-scoped, and answers its own urgency: 'far cheaper now than after data exists: if yes the label becomes a **pair** (`op_org_id` + `op_project_id`) and every compiler emits both.' 02 Q10 gives a firm recommendation ('no; mint one token per project') but 01 still carries Q5 as open, so no decision exists. 01 Q6 asks whether the reserved namespace should narrow from the `op_` prefix to an exact set, and schedules the evidence gathering as 'Measure `openpanel_telemetry_ingest_stripped_total` by sanitized key over the first 60 days of real traffic. If benign `op_*` keys dominate, narrow **before GA** — after GA it is a breaking change in the wrong direction.' Both alter the strip set or the fingerprint, i.e. stored rows, and 01's own Effort calls Q5 'the cheapest question on the list to answer early and the most expensive to answer late'.

**Fix.** Close both in P0 as decisions rather than measurements. Adopt 02 Q10's recommendation for Q5 and mark 01 Q5 closed. For Q6, either commit to the `op_` prefix permanently and say so, or narrow now — the 60-day measurement window as written guarantees the answer arrives after the data it would have protected.

### M39. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

The gigapipe base URL appears as `GIGAPIPE_URL` (01, 03, 04, 06, 10, 11), `GIGAPIPE_INTERNAL_URL` (02, 07), `GIGAPIPE_URL_INTERNAL` (10) and `GIGAPIPE_READ_URL` + `GIGAPIPE_WRITE_URL` (05). The basic-auth username appears as `GIGAPIPE_LOGIN` (02, 10), `GIGAPIPE_USER` (04, 05), `GIGAPIPE_USERNAME` (03) and `GIGAPIPE_BASIC_AUTH_USER` (07). A CI grep enforces it" — a good rule scoped to one document, which guarantees eleven internally-consistent and mutually-inconsistent surfaces. This is not cosmetic: 10 owns the compose template, `quiz.ts` substitution, `.env.template` and `environment-variables.mdx`, and 02's §12 and 04's §2.3 each specify a *complete* set that 10 does not emit.

### M40. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

### M41. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** The gigapipe base URL and credentials have five naming schemes, and the flag that means "telemetry is on" is a different variable in three specs. Credentials: 02 `GIGAPIPE_LOGIN`/`GIGAPIPE_PASSWORD`; 04 §3 `GIGAPIPE_USERNAME`/`GIGAPIPE_PASSWORD`; 05/10 `GIGAPIPE_USER`/`GIGAPIPE_PASSWORD`. Update 11 gate 1.7.

### M42. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Five kill-switch mechanisms for one operator lever, two of them with opposite polarity. 01 §11: Redis `telemetry:ingest:enabled` / `telemetry:read:enabled`, "defaulting to *enabled* only when `GIGAPIPE_URL` is set" — a value means enabled. 04 D15: Redis `op:gp:off` / `op:gp:off:<projectId>`, "presence of either key" means disabled, no TTL ("a brake that un-pulls itself at 3 a.m. is not a brake"). 02 §4: Redis `telemetry:disabled:{projectId}` / `telemetry:disabled:*` with a **mandatory** TTL ("so an emergency block expires rather than being forgotten") — directly opposite to 04 on TTL. 10 §10.3 writes `telemetry:disabled:*`. 06 §15: env vars `GIGAPIPE_TRACES_READ_ENABLED` / `GIGAPIPE_TRACES_INGEST_ENABLED`. 05 D12: unset `GIGAPIPE_READ_URL`. An on-call engineer reading any one of these will pull a lever the other four do not observe.

**Fix.** One key namespace, one polarity, one TTL policy, documented once in 10 (which owns the runbook). Recommend 02's `telemetry:disabled:*` / `telemetry:disabled:{projectId}` namespace since 10 already writes it, with 04's split between read and ingest (which is genuinely useful — 04's reasoning that a read-path enforcement bug must not stop correctly-stamped ingest is right) and 02's mandatory TTL for the per-project key plus no TTL for the global one. Delete 01 §11's and 06 §15's variants.

### M43. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** 02's route table omits a route that two other specs require. 02 §1 lists four POST routes — `/telemetry/v1/{metrics,logs,traces}` and `/telemetry/api/v1/write` — plus a GET probe, and its "Routes deliberately not exposed" list does not mention Loki push at all, so its absence reads as an oversight rather than a decision. 01 §3.1's table includes `POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` → `POST {GIGAPIPE_URL}/loki/api/v1/push` at an 8 MiB limit, with a dedicated enforcement module `packages/gigapipe/src/ingest/loki-push.ts` and tests T1.24–T1.26. 05 §4.1 registers `POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` as one of its two ingest routes and §4.5's `pushLogs` posts to it as the *only* way log data reaches gigapipe. 05 §10 also makes `packages/logger`'s new `LOG_EXPORTER=loki` mode post to it. Since 02 owns the router, the route does not exist.

**Fix.** Add `POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` to 02 §1's route table with its content types (JSON, and protobuf+snappy), its body limit and its rate-limit budget. If the intent was to defer it, say so explicitly in 02's "deliberately not exposed" list and have 01 §3.1 and 05 §4.1/§4.5/§10 fall back to `/telemetry/v1/logs` — but note that under the resolution of the log-ingest-path conflict above, this route is the primary log path and cannot be deferred.

### M44. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** There are three incompatible definitions of the reserved-key predicate, and each fails differently. Doc 01 §2 `isReservedAttributeKey` returns true for any name whose image under identity, `SanitizeKey` or `sanitizeLabels` starts with `op_` — a prefix rule. Doc 02 §4.0 `isReservedOtlpKey`/`isReservedPromLabel` test membership in a three-element `Set` (`op_project_id`, `op_organization_id`, `__ttl_days__`) — an exact-match rule, and it applies only the sanitizer "that matches the destination path". Doc 06 §2.1 uses a raw prefix check with an explicit `CLIENT_ALLOWED_SPAN_ONLY` carve-out for `op_session_id`/`op_profile_id`. Under doc 01's predicate, ingest strips every propagated `op_session_id` and the session-correlation differentiator — the entire premise of doc 06 — silently returns nothing, with no error anywhere. Under doc 02's, the reserved namespace is three names, so the "we own `op_*` and can add enforcement labels later without a migration" promise in doc 01 §1.1 is false. Doc 06 §2.1 already documents that it found this contradiction in its own draft ("a security-critical function in two mutually contradictory forms, and the copy-pasteable one silently deletes every `op_session_id`") but resolves it only within doc 06.

**Fix.** One exported predicate, one location, with the carve-out as a parameter rather than a second function: `isReservedAttributeKey(name, { level: 'resource' | 'scope' | 'record' | 'datapoint' | 'span' })`, prefix-based over the union of all three sanitizers (doc 01's rule, which is the safe one), returning false for `op_session_id`/`op_profile_id` at span level only. Docs 02 and 06 import it; neither re-declares the constants. Doc 01's alias test table (T1.4) and doc 02's test 1 become one table-driven suite that includes a row asserting `op_session_id` survives on a span and is stripped on a resource — doc 06 §2.1's stated reason for the resource-level strip (it would be appended onto every span by otlp.go:81 and win the merge) is the row that matters.

### M45. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** Four different per-route body limits for the same routes, with no shared source. 01 §3.1: OTLP 16 MiB ("comfortably under gigapipe's own 64 MiB `OTLPMaxMessageSize` … so a batch we accept is never rejected downstream"), remote-write 4 MiB compressed, Loki push 8 MiB. 02 §3.1: `TELEMETRY_MAX_OTLP_BYTES` = 4 MiB ("Ours is 16× lower because gigapipe streams into ClickHouse column buffers while we materialise a protobufjs object graph at roughly 5–10× wire size"), `TELEMETRY_MAX_PRW_BYTES` = 4 MiB. 05 §4.1: `OTLP_MAX_BODY = 4 * 1024 * 1024`. 06 §2.4: "Per-route `bodyLimit` on `POST /telemetry/v1/traces` — 8 MiB → 413". 02's memory-bound arithmetic in §6 (`8 × (4 MiB inbound + ~20–40 MB object graph) ≈ 200–350 MB`) is computed from its own 4 MiB figure and does not hold at 16 MiB.

**Fix.** 02 owns the gateway and is the only doc that derives the number from a memory bound; adopt 4 MiB as `TELEMETRY_MAX_OTLP_BYTES` and make it one env-configured constant every route reads. Rewrite 01 §3.1's table, 06 §2.4's requirement row, and confirm 05 §4.1 imports rather than redeclares it. If traces genuinely need 8 MiB, 06 should argue it against 02 §6's arithmetic and 02 should restate the semaphore sizing.

### M46. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The response an ingest client gets when its organisation is blocked is specified four different ways, and two of them are stated as direct opposites. 01 §3.5: "the block answers **429 with `Retry-After: 3600`**: OTLP and Prometheus clients back off on 429 rather than hot-looping". 02 §7.3: "`telemetryWindDownHook` — the same lookup, the same fail-open, with **403** … 403 rather than 402 or 202 because it is permanent until the customer acts, and OTLP clients do not retry 403". 10 §11.3, in a code comment: "**Never 402/403**: these clients treat a non-429 4xx as permanent and drop." 05 §4.3: `200 {"partialSuccess":{"rejectedLogRecords":N,"errorMessage":"ingestion paused: <reason>"}}` for OTLP and `204` + `X-OP-Blocked` for Loki. 06 §2.4: `HTTP 200` + `ExportTraceServiceResponse{partial_success}`. 11 §7.2 only requires "a signal-appropriate body". The 429-vs-403 pair is a straight contradiction about whether the customer's buffered data survives an upgrade.

**Fix.** 10 owns quota/billing and its reasoning is the one tied to a customer outcome ("a mid-period upgrade recovers the buffered data"), so standardise on 429 + jittered `Retry-After` for both wind-down and quota, with `RESOURCE_EXHAUSTED` on gRPC. Rewrite 02 §7.3 and its §9.1 status table, and 05 §4.3's and 06 §2.4's OTLP rows. Keep 01's `Retry-After: 3600` value or 10's 900 s — pick one. Note 05/06's 200+`partial_success` shape is separately wrong here for the reason 02 D6 gives (an exporter reports zero drops), and 10 U9 already flags that the correct non-200 body is `google.rpc.Status`; settle U9 once and apply it to all four docs.

### M47. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** Four different HTTP statuses for a wind-down-blocked telemetry ingest, one of which is a must-gate. 02 §4 and 01's "What the user sees" table: **403** with a `google.rpc.Status` ("OTel exporters treat 403 as permanent and stop retrying"). 10 D15: **403**, same reasoning. 05 §4.3: **200 with `partialSuccess`** for OTLP and **204 + `X-OP-Blocked`** for Loki, plus **404** for `telemetry_disabled`. 06 T20/§4.1 step 0: **202-and-drop**, "the same 202-and-drop semantics as `track.router.ts:14`". 11 A15: **429 + `Retry-After`**, described as "a **deliberate divergence**" and listed as a must-row in gate 1.2.

**Fix.** 02 owns the gateway's error semantics. Pick one — 403 and 429 are both defensible (403 stops retries, 429 backs off and preserves the data if the block lifts) but 05's 200/204 is not, because it makes a blocked org look healthy to its collector, which is the failure 02 D7 and 05 D13 both otherwise argue against. Recommend 429 + `Retry-After` (11's reasoning) or 403 (02/10's), decided in 02, then propagate to 01's table, 05 §4.3, 06 §4.1 and 11 A15 in one edit.

### M48. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Adding `ClientType.telemetry` widens existing deny-lists, and four of the five docs enumerate a shorter list than the one that matters. 02 §2.2 lists seven sites and leads with the one the others omit: "`apps/api/src/utils/auth.ts:42-176` `validateSdkRequest` — **no `client.type` check anywhere in the function**" behind `POST /track`, `/event`, `/profile`, calling it "the widening that matters most: without it every telemetry credential — which lives in a collector config on every host a customer runs — gets full analytics-event write access". It also adds `insights.controller.ts:85-93`'s `clientType: client.type === 'root' ? 'root' : 'read'` coercion. 01 §3.2 lists four sites and mentions `validateSdkRequest` only to say it must not be *reused*. 05 §4.2 lists the same four. 08 §2 lists five edits and mentions `validateSdkRequest` only in prose after the table. 11's P1 MUST 6 asks for "a `write` token is rejected" but its enumerated conversions cite only auth.ts:202/:237 and mcp:99. Verified: `enum ClientType { read write root }` at schema.prisma:353, so all five docs are describing the same live grant.

**Fix.** Promote 02 §2.2's seven-row table to the canonical checklist and have 01 §3.2, 05 §4.2, 08 §2 and 11's P1 MUST 6 cite it rather than restate a subset. Add a row for `validateSdkRequest` to each doc's test list explicitly — 11's P1 MUST 6 should read "a `telemetry` client is rejected by `POST /track`, `/event` and `/profile`", which is the only assertion that catches the highest-severity case. Since none of these fail a compile or an existing test (verified: `apps/api/src/utils/` has only `ids.test.ts` and `image-proxy.test.ts`), the checklist must be a merge gate on the P1-a PR, as 08 D3 and 02 D2 both say.

### M49. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Four designs for the telemetry secret-verification cache, and 01 argues two of the others are unrotatable. 01 §6.1 keys `telemetry:auth:${clientId}` with `{hash, digest}` in the value at 60 s, and explains why: "Keying on the presented secret (as `packages/mcp/src/auth.ts:106-108` does, and as the draft of this document did) makes the entry unaddressable at rotation time: no code path can reconstruct the key without the old plaintext, so nothing can clear it." 02 §2.2 keys `telemetry:auth:${clientId}:${secretHash(clientSecret)}` at 300 s — exactly the shape 01 rejects. 05 §4.2 uses "SHA-256 prefix of the secret as the cache key" at 5 minutes — same shape. 11 A17 requires a different change again: a new `client:authv2:` prefix on the *shared* `validateSdkRequest` cache, and calls it "a prerequisite, not a test". The stated revocation SLA follows the same fragmentation: 60 s (01, 08), 360 s (02, which uniquely identifies that `client.remove` never calls `getClientByIdCached.clear()`), 5 min (05), two SLAs (11 A18).

**Fix.** 01's key design is correct and its rotation argument is decisive; 02 §2.2 and 05 §4.2 should adopt it. Fold 02's real finding — `packages/trpc/src/routers/client.ts` `client.remove` never clears the cache, making dashboard revocation 300 s + 60 s — into 01's revocation-SLA table and make the one-line fix a P1a deliverable with a named owner. Then state one number in one place and have 05, 08 and 11 A18 cite it.

### M50. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** Telemetry deletion — a GDPR launch gate — has five budgets, four phases and no owner. Doc 11 P1 MUST 9 requires `deletion-e2e.test.ts` rows green for metrics and logs at **P1**. Doc 10 schedules `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse` + erasure ordering + both call sites inside its **P2** block. Doc 09 D13 calls it a launch blocker "owned by P0/P1" and says this stream "must not ship the four nav items before that sweep exists" (i.e. gates **P2**). Doc 02 lists it under "Required before GA, owned elsewhere but blocking" with owner "schema/ops". Doc 08 §12 owns the function and spreads its 2.5 days "across P1-P5". Doc 01 D10 also specifies it, with a `TelemetryPurge` model doc 08 does not have. Doc 04 §12 says explicitly it is not this work-stream's job and raises it as Q5, "a launch blocker for GDPR-exposed deployments".

**Fix.** Name one owner (doc 08 is the natural home, since it owns `delete.service.ts` and `TABLE_NAMES`) and one phase. Given that it gates the first user-visible surface, it belongs in P1 alongside metrics ingest, not P2 — you cannot quarantine or erase what you cannot delete, and doc 01's remediation runbook is inert without it. Reconcile doc 01's `TelemetryPurge` model with doc 08's design in the same pass.

### M51. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** 02-ingest states a verifiable falsehood about gigapipe and builds a default on it. §3.1's cap table justifies `TELEMETRY_MAX_PRW_BYTES` with "gigapipe hard-rejects a *decompressed* remote-write body over `10*1024*1024` with a 400 `\"body is too long\"` (`writer/controller/middleware.go:122-124`). Staying under it means the caller gets our error with our message." I read the function: `withUnsnappyRequest` builds `errors.New400Error("body is too long")` inside an inner closure, and the `if err != nil` branch at middleware.go:133-137 **discards it**, stuffs the still-compressed body into the request context and returns `nil`. There is no 400 — gigapipe hands unparseable bytes to the protobuf decoder. 01-tenancy §3.4 gets this exactly right ("that error is **discarded**… gigapipe never emits a 400 for an oversize body") and so does 10-ops §2 ("a separate 10 MiB decompressed check that produces no error… do not write a conformance test asserting gigapipe rejects it"). Relatedly, the two docs specify opposite outbound encodings for the same leg: 02 D5 forwards snappy-compressed with no `Content-Encoding` header (a well-argued analysis of gigapipe's two-stage decompression), while 01 §3.4 sends uncompressed protobuf and concludes "the snappy path is never entered at all" — which is itself imprecise, since `withUnsnappyRequest` is route middleware that always runs and merely fails through.

**Fix.** Correct 02 §3.1's rationale to match 01 and 10. Keep the 4 MiB cap (the real reason — the gateway is the only enforcement point and gigapipe provides none — is stronger than the stated one). Adopt 02 D5's snappy-with-no-`Content-Encoding` as the single outbound encoding and delete 01's uncompressed variant, or vice versa; either works, but one of them must be deleted.

### M52. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The reserved-key predicate has two signatures and two behaviours, and 01's own test asserts the behaviour 02 argues is a bug. 01 §5: `isReservedKey(key, protocol)` — for `protocol === 'otlp-traces'` it compares the **raw** key, and 01 T1.2 asserts "`isReservedKey('op.project.id','otlp-traces')` is **false** (keys stored verbatim)". 02 §6.0: `isReserved(k)` tests the raw key and both sanitized forms unconditionally, and argues the per-protocol form is wrong precisely on traces — "under a literal reading a span attribute `op.project.id` would *not* be stripped … and would land in the trace tag index under the raw name and inside the `proto.Marshal`ed `payload`, which is exactly the lie the traces strip exists to prevent." 11 requires `isReservedKey(key, path)` "the **computed** strip predicate (D7) … with no alias table anywhere in the strip path" and its E5b asserts the source has no alias array. 01's T1.2 would fail against 02's implementation.

**Fix.** 02 is right; over-stripping is free and the traces path is where the per-protocol form leaks. Change 01 §5 to the unconditional three-form predicate, delete the `protocol` parameter, and fix 01 T1.2's traces row. Then settle the name (`isReservedKey`, per 11) and the home (`packages/gigapipe/src/labels.ts`, per 01 D11 and 11) — 02 currently declares it in `apps/api/src/telemetry/labels.ts`, which is a fourth location.

### M53. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** Metric/label/tag metadata — the surface doc 01 §4.5 calls "the most dangerous defaults" — has two contradictory designs, and one of them takes the whole surface outside every enforcement chokepoint the plan builds. Doc 03 D6/§7 resolves the catalogue through gigapipe's `/api/v1/label/__name__/values` with a server-constructed single `match[]`, which passes through doc 01's route-key client and doc 04's `transport.ts` gate. Doc 04 D4 abandons gigapipe for direct ClickHouse SQL in `packages/db/src/services/telemetry-metadata.service.ts` (five statements), and doc 06 §6 adds a sixth for trace tag values. Under doc 04's design the metadata path is not in `GIGAPIPE_ROUTES`, does not traverse the enforcement gate, has no response-side verification, and its tenancy rests on a hand-written `key = 'op_project_id' AND val = <pid>` predicate repeated in six separate SQL strings with no single function that can be audited or tested as a totality — which is precisely the property doc 04 D1 argues is load-bearing for the query compilers ("only holds if there is exactly one function that emits a `{`"). Doc 04 also introduces a second interpolation surface: `GIGAPIPE_DB` from the environment plus `sqlstring.escape` values, with the identifier validated in one file (§2.4) and re-validated in another (doc 06 §3).

**Fix.** Choose one metadata path in the blueprint. If doc 04's direct-SQL path wins (its six reasons are good — the label endpoints silently drop rows on error at query_abels.go:62,67, hardcode `Limit: 10000`, and `Prom2LogqlMatch` panics into a 500), then it needs the same chokepoint discipline the gigapipe path gets: a single `scopedFingerprints(projectId, window)` / `scopedTraceSpans(projectId, window)` CTE builder that every statement must compose, a test asserting no generated SQL contains `op_project_id` outside that builder, and one owner for `TELEMETRY_DB`/`TELEMETRY_TABLES` (doc 06 §Interfaces already asks for doc 04's local `T` map and its own to collapse — do that). If doc 03's gigapipe path wins, delete doc 04 §9 and doc 06 §6.

### M54. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** `compileMetricQuery` has three signatures with different argument orders and one required capability nobody owns. 04 §5.1: `compileMetricQuery(q: IMetricQuery, opts: { projectId, step, timezone, maxSeries, pinnedSeries? }): { promql, legend }` — with `maxSeries` required and a mandatory `topk()` wrap. 07 §Interfaces: `compileMetricQuery(projectId: string, query: IMetricQuery, opts?: { mode?: 'chart' | 'alerting' }): { promql }` — projectId first, no step/timezone, and a `mode` 03 never mentions. 03 §4 exports something different again: `compile(query: IMetricQuery, grid: MetricGrid, ctx: { projectId }): CompiledMetricQuery` with `promql`, `selectionPromql`, `windowMs`, `groupBy`, `foldFn`, `valueScale`, `fragments`, `notices`. 07 lists the mismatch as an open question it cannot close ("Will P2 accept `mode: 'alerting'` on `compileMetricQuery`? … If P2 declines, the fallback is a thin alerting-only wrapper — worse, because it means two places know PromQL"), and 04's required `topk` wrap and `pinnedSeries` are handled by 03 in a different component (`select.ts`'s two-phase selection), not by the compiler.

**Fix.** 03 owns the compiler; publish one exported signature there and have 04 §5.1 and 07 §Interfaces consume it verbatim. `mode: 'alerting'` should be deleted in favour of making the `by (op_project_id)` behaviour unconditional (see the aggregation finding above), which is what 07 actually needs. 04's `maxSeries`/`pinnedSeries` requirements map onto 03 §8's `seriesLimit` and `pinnedSeries` — restate them as constraints on `select.ts`, not as compiler parameters. Whoever writes the merged signature should also confirm 01 §4.2's `compileSelector`/`compileAggregation` are the same functions under different names or genuinely a lower layer 03 calls.

### M55. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`
