**Problem.** Four positions on whether a metric report can be publicly shared. 03 §13.5 reuses `chartProcedure`, keeps the share path working, and adds rate limiting plus window clamping. 09 §5.7 requires it to work and specifies an 11,000-step clamp on caller-supplied windows. 01 D6's `resolveProjectScope` is share-aware by design. 11 §5 tests "share path takes `dataSource` from `ctx.report`, **never** from the client's input". 04 D6 forbids it: `observabilityProcedure` "**hard rejects** anything that is not provably a metric report" and every procedure "rejects `shareId` with `FORBIDDEN`" (test T-22), with §7.2 listing six preconditions before it could ever be enabled. 08 §3 takes a third position: "P2 blocks public sharing of metric reports" — the share *creation* mutation rejects `dataSource === 'metrics'` and the read path returns `TRPCNotFoundError`.

**Fix.** Decide once. 04 §7.2's preconditions are the real content — no metadata enumeration, no raw log lines, window overrides only from the four-field allow-list, visible retention truncation — and 03 §13.5 and 09 §5.7 independently supply the two missing controls (rate limiting on the share branch, the step clamp). So the shippable answer is: metric *charts* are shareable in P2 with 04 §7.2's six constraints enforced; logs, traces and all metadata procedures are never shareable. Rewrite 04 D6 from "reject" to "constrained", delete 08 §3's share-creation block, and have 09 §5.7 and 03 §13.5 cite 04 §7.2's list rather than restating fragments of it.

### M56. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Two incompatible `zMetricQuery` definitions are on disk, and the current phasing has P1 building the one P2 deletes. 07 Q1 documents both: 01:1027-1051 places it in `packages/validation/src/telemetry.validation.ts` with 11 `fn` values, `aggregation` including `topk`/`bottomk`/`quantile`, and a `k` field; 03:386-450 places it in `packages/validation/src/index.ts` with a required `metricType`, 5 `fn` values, `aggregation` narrowed to `sum|avg|min|max`, and no `k`. 03's Interfaces section is explicit that '`zMetricQuery` is rewritten, not extended' and that `compileAggregation`'s quantile/topk/bottomk branches 'become dead the moment `zMetricAggregation` narrows… Delete them in the same change or it will not compile.' Yet 01's Effort puts the three query compilers in Tier 1 and gates P2 on them, and 09 D3 consumes 03's shape verbatim. 07 states this 'blocks §1, §3, §9.2 and §12' and 'the schema question (Q1) blocks the very first file'.

**Fix.** Adopt 03's shape as canonical in P0, before 01 writes a line of compiler. Then 01's compiler is written once against the narrowed enum, 07 unblocks its first file, and 09's D3 is already correct. 07's own position ('P2's, because 03 claims ownership explicitly and it is the object the chart builder persists') is the right one; make it a decision, not a question.

### M57. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** Doc 09 §Design (the "show me the query" affordance) adds an optional `compiled?: string` to `chart.chart`'s output object, populated for `dataSource === 'metrics'`. That returns the compiled PromQL to the procedure's caller — which on the share path is anonymous (chart.ts:83, `publicProcedure`) — and the compiled string is by construction `metric{op_project_id="<project uuid>", …user matchers…}`. So an unauthenticated share viewer receives the project's UUID, the metric name, and every label matcher name and value the report author wrote. It is also written verbatim into Redis by `cacheMiddleware` (trpc.ts:222-230), which writes unconditionally in every environment and only gates the *read* on production. Doc 04 §11 states the opposing rule for its own logs: "**Never** the compiled query at `info` or below — it contains label values". Doc 09 chose this shape over a dedicated procedure for a good reason (a second round-trip could display a string that differs from the string that ran, making the boundary unauditable), but the fix is scoping, not the channel.

**Fix.** Populate `compiled` only when the request resolved through the member branch (`ctx.report === null`, i.e. an authenticated project member) and never on the share branch — assert it in doc 09's hostile-caller suite alongside the step clamp. If doc 08's block on metric shares wins, this is moot for P2, which is another reason to settle that decision first. Also state in doc 09 that `compiled` is part of the cached value, so the same rule has to hold at cache-write time, not only at render time.

### M58. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** `metricType` is required and persisted in 03 but absent from the UI schema that has to write it. 03 D6: "`zMetricQuery.metricType` is `z.enum(['counter','gauge','histogram'])` with **no default and no `auto`**", and 03's whole compilation table (§4.2) branches on it — `rate` on a gauge is a hard compile error, `histogram_quantile` on a counter is a hard compile error. 03 §Interfaces states the requirement to the UI stream: "The metric picker … Must write a concrete `metricType`." 09 D2's `zChartMetricItem` has no `metricType` field at all, and 09 §4.5's `MetricQueryCard` table lists Function, Window, Aggregation, Group by and Max series but no type control — its only nod to the concept is disabling `p50…p99` "unless `metaQuery.data?.type === 'histogram'`", i.e. reading it transiently from the picker rather than persisting it. 01 §4.2's `zMetricQuery` also omits it. A saved report would therefore fail 03's zod parse, or worse be materialised by 03's `transformReportMetricItem` with its `item.metricType ?? 'gauge'` fallback, silently turning every saved counter into a gauge and making `rate` a hard error on reload.

**Fix.** Add `metricType` to 09 D2's `zChartMetricItem` and to 09 §4.5's `MetricQueryCard` as a visible, user-overridable control seeded from the catalogue (03 D6 says "The picker lets the user override"). Delete 03's `metricType: item.metricType ?? 'gauge'` fallback in favour of a loud error, or state explicitly what a pre-migration row should do. Align 01 §4.2's schema to 03's per the `zMetricQuery` finding above.

### M59. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** The single tenancy-bearing middleware is specified twice with different bases. `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §7.1 builds `observabilityProcedure` on `publicProcedure` and hand-rolls the session check and `getProjectAccess`. `04-read-path.md` D13 rebuilds it as `protectedProcedure.use(rejectShareId)` and gives the reason: `enforceAccess` already does this. Verified `packages/trpc/src/trpc.ts:91-112` — `enforceAccess` additionally carries the demo-mode mutation guard (`if (type === 'mutation' && process.env.DEMO_USER_ID) throw`), the `organizationId` branch, and the fail-closed `needsWrite` rule whose own comment says it covers "any procedure that takes a top-level projectId … including ones added later". 01's version reproduces only the `projectId` read half. Since 01 is the tenancy document and calls this "the single tenancy-bearing middleware", its code sample is the one an implementer will copy — and it opts the one router the plan calls a security boundary out of the repo's central access middleware, permanently, including out of every future change to it. 04 D13 also corrects 01's stated justification (`requireProjectAccess` at `level: 'read'` *is* the truthiness test; the `canWriteProject` branch only exists at `level: 'write'`).

**Fix.** Delete `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §7.1's `publicProcedure` sample and replace it with a pointer to `04-read-path.md` §6.1, keeping only the two rules 01 owns (the share rule and "the project id is derived, never received"). Keep `04-read-path.md`'s `NO_PROJECT_ID` fail-closed allow-list and its T25a/T25b/T25c iterate-the-router tests as the enforcement.

### M60. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** LogQL compilation is owned twice, and 04 depends on two exports that neither owner defines. 01 §4.3 defines `compileStreamSelector(filters, projectId)` in `packages/gigapipe/src/query/logql.ts`, exported in §Interfaces as "P3's only route to a LogQL selector". 05 §5.2 defines a different, much larger `compileLogQuery(input, { timezone, retentionDays }) → ICompiledLogQL` in `packages/db/src/gigapipe/logql.ts`, covering the selector, line filters, the parser stage, field filters, the retention clamp and `countQuery` — and 05 §2's I1–I8 make that function the sole enforcement point. Separately, 04 §Interfaces makes two named requests of 01 that 01 does not fulfil: "`assertLogqlScoped(expr, projectId): void` … **new, requested by this spec**" and "`assertTraceqlScoped` … **new, requested by this spec**; required before P4 exposes `traces.search`", and 04 §4.6's enforcement gate does not compile without them.

**Fix.** 05 owns LogQL end-to-end — its compiler is the one with the invariant analysis and the `notMatches`/apostrophe/empty-value hazards worked out. Delete `compileStreamSelector` from 01 §4.3 and §Interfaces and have 01's LogQL section become an appendix on the gigapipe planner behaviours 05's compiler must respect. Then either 05 or 01 must actually define `assertLogqlScoped` as the byte-level prefix assertion 04 D3 specifies, and `assertTraceqlScoped` becomes moot if 06's direct-SQL trace design is adopted (see above) — 04 §4.6's verifier table drops to `promql` only.

### M61. `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** `LOG_DRILLDOWN` is required on and shipped off, and the routes it gates are simultaneously required and blocked. 05 D8: "**`LOG_DRILLDOWN=true` in every deployment** … The selector builder's cardinality hints and the settings card both need `/detected_labels` and `/index/volume`, so turning the flag off costs more than it saves", and 05 §5.3 maps `logs.labels` to `GET /loki/api/v1/detected_labels`. 10 §3 sets `LOG_DRILLDOWN=false` ("cost with no consumer"). Independently, 01 D9 blocks `/loki/api/v1/index/volume`, `/detected_labels`, `/detected_fields` and `/patterns` from the allowlist entirely, and 04 D3 removes them from `GIGAPIPE_ROUTES`. So 05's `logs.labels` procedure is unreachable under both the env manifest and the route allowlist.

**Fix.** Resolve in 01's favour on the routes (its argument that `QueryVolume` string-interpolates `targetLabels` into a query and re-parses it is a real injection surface, which 05 I6 independently confirms) and have 05 derive label cardinality from the direct-ClickHouse metadata service 04 §9 already builds, rather than from `/detected_labels`. Then 10's `LOG_DRILLDOWN=false` is correct and 05 D8's first paragraph should be rewritten to say so. If instead the drilldown routes are wanted, 01 D9 and 04 D3 must both be amended and 05 I6's validator becomes a hard requirement.

### M62. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** Three specs give three trace-read architectures, and doc 04's is the one doc 01 proves unsound. Doc 04 §4.3 requires `tempo.trace` requests to carry `provenance: { verifiedBy: 'tempo.search'; at: number }` — i.e. a scoped TraceQL search proves ownership, then the unscoped by-id fetch is proxied. Doc 01 D4 rejects exactly that design with a concrete attack: `GetQueryRequest` (reader/service/tempo.go:53-104) filters on `trace_id` and an optional time window and nothing else, and trace ids are entirely client-supplied on ingest, so an attacker who ingests one span carrying the victim's trace id under their own project makes the scoped search hit — after which the gateway hands back every span of that trace from every project. Doc 01 chooses response filtering (`filterTraceToProject`, fail-closed per span). Doc 06 T2 chooses direct ClickHouse SQL with the project predicate inside the statement and before the limit, and additionally shows why doc 01's response filter has a second failure mode: gigapipe's hardcoded `LIMIT 2000` (tempo.go:83) applies with no attribute predicate, so on a cross-project trace it can return 2,000 spans of which none belong to the caller, and the post-filter then 404s a trace the caller genuinely owns. Doc 06 §Interfaces posts an amendment asking doc 04's owner to accept or reject the replacement; that amendment is unresolved in the set.

**Fix.** Delete the `provenance` two-step from doc 04 §4.3 and record doc 01 D4's attack as the reason, so nobody re-derives it. Then resolve doc 06's amendment explicitly: either adopt doc 06 T2 (direct SQL, predicate before the limit, `foreign` and `not_found` indistinguishable to the caller — the strongest of the three, and it removes the reader dependency for traces entirely) or keep doc 01's response filter and add doc 06's truncation caveat to it. Whichever wins, `/api/traces/{traceId}` must be removed from the other two documents' route tables and from `GIGAPIPE_ROUTES` if unused.

### M63. `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Doc 06's operational counters use `{project}` as a Prometheus label — `op_telemetry_spans_ingested_total{project}`, `op_telemetry_span_attrs_stripped_total{project,level}`, `op_telemetry_spans_clamped_total{project}`, `op_telemetry_spans_rejected_total{project,reason}`, `op_telemetry_trace_decode_failures_total{project}`, `op_telemetry_gin_rows_estimate{project}` — on the existing prom-client registries. Doc 01 §Detection says flatly "Never put the project id in a metric label — that is unbounded cardinality on our own `/metrics`", and doc 04 §11 says "`projectId` is a **log field, never a metric label**". Two problems compound. First, cardinality: one time series per project per counter per label combination, unbounded and never garbage-collected within a process, on the registry that also serves the API's operational metrics. Second, disclosure: doc 10 §12 confirms `self-hosting/caddy/Caddyfile.template`'s `handle_path /api*` strips the prefix so `https://$DOMAIN/api/metrics` serves the full prom-client dump **unauthenticated today**, which means these counters publish an enumeration of every project id on the instance plus their relative span volumes to anyone on the internet. Doc 06's own text notes the exposure two paragraphs later and defers the fix to P0.

**Fix.** Drop `{project}` from every counter in doc 06 and move the per-project dimension to structured log fields, matching docs 01 and 04. Where a per-project number is genuinely needed for the settings card, read it from the meter (doc 05 §4.6 / doc 10 §7) rather than from `/metrics`. Separately, make doc 10 §12's Caddy `handle /api/metrics` + `basic_auth` block a P0 blocker rather than a P0 item, since three work-streams (01, 02 §10, 03 §16, 04 §11, 06) all add counters to that endpoint and each cites the others' need to fix it.

### M64. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** Five specs each add crons to the same three files with no shared inventory, and two of them use the same job name for different jobs. 05 §4.7 registers `telemetryUsage` (hourly `5 * * * *`), `telemetryRetention` (`15 3 * * *`) and `telemetryReconcile` (`45 3 * * *`). 10 §6.2 registers `telemetryRetention` (`10 */6 * * *`) and `telemetryUsageRollup` (`20 1 * * *`) — same name `telemetryRetention`, different schedule, different body. 07 D7 adds `metricAlerts` (60 s) plus a whole new `alerts` BullMQ queue. 01 §Detection(b) adds `cron.telemetry-tenancy-probe.ts` every 15 minutes. 06 §11.7 adds a weekly orphan sweep. `boot-cron.ts` removes every scheduler not in its `jobs` array, so a partial landing silently unschedules whichever one lost — which 07 A31 identifies as "the worst failure an alerting system has".

**Fix.** Publish one cron inventory in 00-blueprint.md (name, type, schedule, owning doc, file), and make 11 §7.2's exhaustive registration test assert against it. Resolve the `telemetryRetention` name collision — 10's is the TTL re-assert, 05's is the per-project purge; rename 05's to `telemetryPurge`. Check the schedule slots against 10 §6.2's existing-slot list, which is the only place that inventory exists today.

### M65. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** Prisma models and columns that other specs treat as load-bearing are absent from the schema work-stream's inventory. 01 D5 introduces `model ProjectIdTombstone` and a change to `getId()` and calls the tombstone "the boundary" for failure mode F8 ("a real cross-tenant read produced by the design, not by a bug"); 08's Postgres inventory (P1–P10, six migration files) contains neither. 05 §3.6 introduces `Project.telemetryLabelKeys`, `Project.telemetryLogRetention` and `Project.telemetryLogsBlockedAt` plus `model TelemetryPurgeJob` and two enums; 08's inventory contains none of them and 08 §Scope claims to own "Every Prisma enum, model and field addition".

**Fix.** 08 must absorb `ProjectIdTombstone` (with the `getId` guard and the backfill 01 §3.3 specifies) into its migration list and sequencing table, or 01 D5 must be re-owned. Likewise for 05's three `Project` columns — decide whether per-project label promotion and log retention survive 10 D9's "one window per signal for everyone" (they probably do not in v1, in which case `telemetryLogRetention` should be cut from 05 rather than silently omitted from 08).

### M66. `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `05-logs.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** Stream and series metadata outlives log bodies by the metrics window, and no user- or DPA-facing statement says so. `08-schema-changes.md` §13 and `10-ops-retention-billing.md` §6.1 apply the conditional per-signal TTL to `samples_v3` and `metrics_15s` only; `time_series`, `time_series_gin`, `patterns` and the `tempo_*` sidecars get a flat TTL at the longest tier. Under 10's default windows (metrics 30 d, logs 14 d) a log stream's entire label set survives 30 days after its bodies are gone — and under `05-logs.md` §3.1 that label set is `service_name`, `service_namespace`, `deployment_environment`, `host_name`, `k8s_namespace_name`, `k8s_deployment_name`, `k8s_container_name` plus up to five customer-promoted keys, i.e. a customer's infrastructure inventory. 05 flags it in one sentence ("label cardinality is retained at the metrics window even for logs") and routes it nowhere. Two adjacent gaps compound it: `06-traces-and-correlation.md` §11.6 states `tempo_traces_kv` is deliberately never deleted per-subject and expires only on its own TTL, and `10-ops-retention-billing.md` F20 records that gigapipe's `Rotate` never touches the `profiles_*` family so nothing expires it at all. Meanwhile `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §12 promises the data-handling documentation will state the erasure position "before telemetry is generally available, not discovered in a DPA review", and names no owner.

**Fix.** Add one table to `10-ops-retention-billing.md` §6 giving the effective retention of every gigapipe table by signal, including the metadata tables and the two never-expired families, and make it the source for the customer-facing retention statement. Give `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §12's data-handling deliverable a named owner and a phase gate. State explicitly, in the same place, that log-line retention and log-label retention differ.

### M67. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** `ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES` is pinned to opposite values, and the P0 env manifest that both cite does not contain it. 01 §3.1 decides `true` ("Two work-streams silently disagreeing about a security-relevant flag is precisely the failure this document exists to prevent, so it is decided here: **Set `ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES=true` in the P0 env manifest**") and its Interfaces records "`05-logs.md:323` and `:1619` need a one-line edit to match". 05's Interfaces still lists `ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES=false` as a consumed P0 contract and warns "If any value differs, the I1–I3 tests are void." 10 §3 — the document that actually owns the env table and the compose block — lists neither value.

**Fix.** Make the one-line edit in 05 (01's reasoning is correct: the flag removes the empty-value fail-open on both the LogQL and PromQL paths, and I2's rule survives because it is re-justified by I3's `analyzeStreamSelect` path). Then add the flag to 10 §3's env table and the compose block, which is the only place it becomes real.

### M68. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Three documents disagree about `ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES`, and the document that owns the compose file sets nothing, so the shipped value is the one that disables the defence. `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §3.1 makes a cross-stream decision — "Set `ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES=true` in the P0 env manifest" — and lists it in F7 as one of four controls against the empty-project-id fail-open, where an empty `op_project_id` compiles to `simpleJSONHas(labels,'op_project_id') = 0`, i.e. *select every series lacking the tenancy label*, on both the LogQL and PromQL paths. `05-logs.md` I2 and its Interfaces table pin it to `"false"` so that I2's *evidence* stays observable, and 01 asks 05 for a one-line edit that has not been made. `10-ops-retention-billing.md` §3, the exhaustive "Every environment variable, and why" table, does not mention it at all. Verified: `boolEnv` (`gigapipe/cmd/gigapipe/main.go:54-62`) maps unset to `false`, so the compose in 10 §2 ships 05's value and 01's defence-in-depth is absent. The same three-way pattern exists for `LOG_DRILLDOWN` (05 D8 requires `true` everywhere; 10 §2 sets `false`; 01's Interfaces says "only if 05 needs it") — and with `true`, gigapipe registers four untenanted routes including `/loki/api/v1/index/volume`, whose `targetLabels` is string-interpolated into a LogQL expression that is then re-parsed (05 I6 gives the working cross-tenant injection string).

**Fix.** `10-ops-retention-billing.md` §3 is the env manifest; add both variables to it with a decided value and a citation to whichever document justifies it, and treat the manifest as normative. Resolve `ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES` to `true` per 01 §3.1 and edit `05-logs.md` I2 to re-justify itself from I3's `analyzeStreamSelect` path as 01 proposes. Resolve `LOG_DRILLDOWN` once — if `true`, state in 01 §7.6 and 04 §4.1 that the four extra routes are excluded from `GIGAPIPE_ROUTES` and add a test.

### M69. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Four documents give three answers for erasing `tempo_traces_kv`, and it is the table that holds the highest-sensitivity identifier. I verified its shape at ctrl/qryn/sql/traces.sql:34-51: `(oid, date, key, val_id, val)`, `ReplacingMergeTree ORDER BY (oid, date, key, val_id)`, fed by an MV from `tempo_traces_attrs_gin` — so it retains a de-duplicated copy of every attribute value, including `op_profile_id` (often an email, per doc 06 §11) and every `op_session_id`. Doc 01 §6 deletes only `WHERE key = 'op_project_id' AND val IN (...)` and states the residual honestly ("tag values a deleted project contributed survive unless they were unique to it… Put that in the GDPR response"). Doc 08 D13 does the same. Doc 10 §deletion says the table is "**not deletable**". Doc 06 §11 requirement 4 disagrees with all three and is right: `val` is a real column, so `ALTER TABLE tempo_traces_kv DELETE WHERE key = 'op_profile_id' AND val = :id` is expressible and is exactly the shape per-profile erasure needs. Under the three documents that say otherwise, a project delete leaves customer email addresses in the autocomplete dictionary until the global TTL, and a per-profile DSAR has no mechanism at all.

**Fix.** Adopt doc 06's requirement 4 across docs 01 §6, 08 D13 and 10, with the two statements spelled out: `key = 'op_project_id' AND val = <pid>` for project deletion, and `key IN ('op_profile_id','op_session_id') AND val = <id>` for per-profile erasure. Keep doc 01's honesty about the remaining residual (other keys' values contributed by the project) but shrink it to the keys that are not identifiers. Doc 10's "not deletable" row must be corrected — it is the document an operator reads during an incident.

### M70. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** Doc 10 §3 publishes gigapipe's port 3100 on the dev compose surface with `QRYN_LOGIN`/`QRYN_PASSWORD` unset ("3100 **is** published so engineers can `curl` gigapipe while building the compiler and explorer; `QRYN_LOGIN`/`QRYN_PASSWORD` are unset"). Doc 01 D9 calls unreachability a hard P0 requirement rather than defence in depth, and gives the reason no allowlist can substitute for it: writer/grpc/mux.go:28-36 dispatches on transport shape before the path router runs, so the OTLP gRPC receivers sit outside every path-level control and cannot be switched off on a writer-mode node. On a published, unauthenticated port, `MODE=all` with `READONLY` unset also mounts the Elastic wildcard writes (`POST /_bulk`, `POST /{target}/_doc`) and the whole read surface with no tenant predicate — including `/api/v1/metadata` and the v1 tag routes that both doc 01 §4.5 and doc 04 §4.3 permanently un-allowlist. Docker publishes ports past most host firewall rules, and dev boxes routinely carry a copy of production-shaped seed data. Doc 10 §12 separately documents that `https://$DOMAIN/api/metrics` is already unauthenticated through Caddy's `handle_path /api*`, so the deployment stream is aware of this class of gap.

**Fix.** Bind the dev publish to loopback (`127.0.0.1:3100:3100`) and set `QRYN_LOGIN`/`QRYN_PASSWORD` in dev as well — a developer curling gigapipe can pass `-u`. State in doc 01 D9 that the only permitted exception is a loopback bind with auth on, so the requirement cannot be read as "production only". Add the `.github/smoke` assertion doc 11 already contemplates: `docker compose port op-gigapipe 3100` must fail on every non-dev surface.

### M71. `04-read-path.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `05-logs.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** `packages/gigapipe` versus `packages/db/src/gigapipe` is decided in one doc, unresolved in two, and 04's own layer table contradicts 02. 04 D1 decides `packages/gigapipe` and adds "**Action for the P0 owner:** rewrite `03-metrics-engine.md:2113` and `05-logs.md`'s file table to `packages/gigapipe/src/...` before P2.1, or reverse this decision. Do not ship both." 05 still places `logql.ts`, `client.ts`, `envelope.ts`, `severity.ts`, `logs.ingest.ts`, `logs.parse.ts`, `table-name.ts`, `retention.ts` and `cardinality.ts` under `packages/db/src/gigapipe/` throughout, and its open question 8 is still open. 11 Q1 is "blocking — before anyone writes a test file". Separately, 04 D1's layer table assigns `src/ingest/*.ts` and `vendor/opentelemetry-proto/**` inside `packages/gigapipe` to the ingest work-stream, while 02 D9 puts all of it in `apps/api/src/telemetry/` and justifies that at length.

**Fix.** Ratify 04 D1 in 00-blueprint.md and do the rewrite of 05's file table in the same PR. Then fix 04 D1's own table: remove the `src/ingest/*.ts` and `vendor/` rows, because 02 D9's placement (one directory in `apps/api`, one Prisma seam in `deps.ts`, so the later lift-out is a `git mv`) is the better-argued of the two and 04 does not defend its version.

### M72. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The gigapipe credential has three names on the OpenPanel side and two on the container side across the doc set, and the failure mode of any mismatch is a silently unauthenticated gigapipe. OpenPanel side: `02-ingest-gateway.md` §15 uses `GIGAPIPE_LOGIN`/`GIGAPIPE_PASSWORD`, `04-read-path.md` §3 uses `GIGAPIPE_USERNAME`/`GIGAPIPE_PASSWORD` (and enforces its list with a CI grep), `10-ops-retention-billing.md` §3.1 declares `GIGAPIPE_USER`/`GIGAPIPE_PASSWORD` canonical and lists cross-doc edits for 03 and 06 but not for 02 or 04. Container side: 10 §2 sets `CLOKI_LOGIN`/`CLOKI_PASSWORD`, 02 §15 says "→ `QRYN_LOGIN`", 05 and 11 say `QRYN_LOGIN`/`QRYN_PASSWORD`. Verified `cmd/gigapipe/main.go:321-324`: the auth middleware installs only when *both* values are non-empty, and Compose substitutes a missing `.env` key with the empty string plus a warning. The resulting state is invisible: apps/api keeps sending an `Authorization` header, every healthcheck stays green, and gigapipe serves `/loki/api/v1/push`, the Elastic `POST /_bulk` write routes and the always-on cleartext-HTTP/2 gRPC OTLP receiver to anything on the compose network. `10-ops-retention-billing.md` F2 mitigates with `${GIGAPIPE_USER:?…}` and a smoke assertion — but the `:?` guard only protects the name 10 happens to use, and 02's boot assertion checks a different one.

**Fix.** Freeze one name per side in `10-ops-retention-billing.md` §3.1 and edit `02-ingest-gateway.md` §15, `04-read-path.md` §3 and `05-logs.md`/`11-testing-strategy.md` to match. Promote 10's "unauthenticated `GET /ready` against `op-gigapipe` returns 401" from a smoke assertion to a blocking gate in `11-testing-strategy.md` §Test requirements P0, since it is the only detector for the empty-credential state.

### M73. `05-logs.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** Doc 05 §4.1 states a security property that is exactly backwards. It says `/telemetry` is "deliberately **not** added to `corsPaths` … so browser-based OTLP exporters cannot reach it". I read apps/api/src/app.ts:109-125: `corsPaths` is the list of *private* paths; membership gives `{ origin: isAllowed ? origin : false, credentials: true }`, and a path **not** in the list falls through to `return callback(null, { origin: '*', maxAge: 86_400 * 7 })`. So omitting `/telemetry` gives the ingest routes a wildcard CORS policy readable by every web origin — the opposite of the stated control. Any page can POST telemetry with a leaked credential and read the response, and a browser-side exporter is fully functional, which is the configuration doc 05 says it prevents. Doc 02 §12 gets this right and requires adding `/telemetry` to `corsPaths` as a one-line change in the same PR, with the correct reasoning. Since doc 05 is the logs work-stream's spec and doc 02 is the gateway's, whichever lands the router first decides.

**Fix.** Correct doc 05 §4.1 to require adding `/telemetry` to `corsPaths` (app.ts:109) and cite the private/permissive branch split at :116-125 so the reasoning is auditable. Add a route test asserting a cross-origin preflight from an arbitrary origin against `POST /telemetry/v1/logs` does not receive `Access-Control-Allow-Origin: *`. This belongs in doc 02's test list (which owns the router) with doc 05 referencing it.

### M74. `05-logs.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** `05-logs.md` §4.1 reintroduces the rate-limit key that `02-ingest-gateway.md` D11 explicitly rejects as a DoS vector. 05 passes `keyGenerator: (req) => resolveTelemetryClientId(req.headers)` — the client id from the `openpanel-client-id` header or from `Authorization: Basic` — with the comment "header OR Basic, no IP fallback here". Verified `apps/api/src/utils/rate-limiter.ts:31-42`: the wrapper's own fallback already prefers `openpanel-client-id` over the trusted IP, which is why 02 D11 refuses to reuse it — "Client ids are public. An attacker rotating that header value mints unlimited fresh 600/min buckets." The limiter runs before auth in both specs (it installs its hook at register time), so a fabricated id is attacker-chosen and pre-authentication: each rotation buys a fresh bucket, a `getClientByIdCached` lookup, and — for any id that exists — the argon2 `verifyPassword` that 02 §2.2 deliberately puts a Redis-backed lockout in front of. 05 has neither the composite key nor the lockout.

**Fix.** Whichever ingest spec wins, take `02-ingest-gateway.md` D11 §9.1 wholesale: a plugin-local `@fastify/rate-limit` with `keyGenerator: tel:${trustedIp}:${clientId ?? '-'}`, plus §2.2's per-client-id and per-trusted-IP failed-auth counters checked before `verifyPassword`. Delete `05-logs.md` §4.1's `activateRateLimiter` call and its `SELF_HOSTED`-branching `max`, and record in both documents that ingest routes have no inherited rate limiting today (`activateRateLimiter` appears only on mcp/manage/export/insights).

### M75. `05-logs.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** `05-logs.md`'s telemetry router has no scoped error handler, so a 4xx on the ingest routes writes the long-lived ingest credential to the log. Verified `apps/api/src/app.ts:392-427`: `SKIP_LOG_ERRORS` is exactly `['UNAUTHORIZED','FORBIDDEN','FST_ERR_CTP_INVALID_MEDIA_TYPE']`, and every other error logs `req: { …, headers: request.headers, body: rawBody ?? request.body }` — at `warn` for 4xx as well as `error` for 5xx. 05's own design generates precisely the statuses outside that list: 415 for an unknown `Content-Encoding` (§4.1), 415 for protobuf on the Loki route until P3.4b, 413 for an oversize body, and 400 for a malformed payload. `openpanel-client-secret` is in `request.headers` on every one of them. `02-ingest-gateway.md` D11 and §11.3 identify this and install a plugin-local `setErrorHandler` that logs an explicit field list with "deliberately: no headers object, no body"; 05 specifies no error handler at all and its Interfaces table does not consume 02's.

**Fix.** Add `02-ingest-gateway.md` §11.3's `setErrorHandler` to `05-logs.md` §4.1's router (or make 05 consume it explicitly), and add a test asserting no log line emitted from a `/telemetry/*` 4xx contains `openpanel-client-secret`. Separately, route the underlying repo-wide issue — the app-level handler logging all headers on 4xx for `/export`, `/insights` and every other public route — to an owner; `02-ingest-gateway.md` Findings-routed-out item 8 currently ends at "someone should fix it globally".

### M76. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Four different request body limits for the same routes, each claimed as owned. 02 §5.2/§15: `TELEMETRY_MAX_COMPRESSED_BYTES = 8 MiB` (OTLP), `2 MiB` (remote-write), `TELEMETRY_MAX_DECOMPRESSED_BYTES = 32 MiB`, with a justification for 32 over 64 ("for two of three signals **the gateway's cap is the only decompressed-size bound in the system**"). 05 §4.1: `OTLP_MAX_BODY = 4 * 1024 * 1024` as a route-level `bodyLimit` on both log routes. 06 §4.2: "set it at or below 16 MiB and reject with 413" for `/v1/traces`. 11 E29 asserts "payload over 64 MiB" against gigapipe's ceiling and requires "our limit must be at or below it".

**Fix.** 02 owns the gateway and its numbers are the best-reasoned; delete 05's `OTLP_MAX_BODY` and 06 §4.2's 16 MiB and have both cite 02 §15's env block. 11 E29 should assert against 02's configured value, not 64 MiB, or the test passes while the real cap is unset.

### M77. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Trace-ingest mutations are double-owned, single-costed and numerically inconsistent. 06 §4.1 specifies seven ingest-side mutations on POST /v1/traces plus two cross-signal strips, and says explicitly 'That is seven ingest-side mutations plus two cross-signal strips, not three op_* stamps'. 02 §6.3's traces walk contains none of the correlation-specific ones: no snapshot-then-restore for op_session_id/op_profile_id, no op_root marker, no TELEMETRY_MAX_CORRELATED_SPANS_PER_TRACE cap, no exception lifting into op_exception_type/op_exception_message. Worse, 02's scrubAttrs unconditionally drops anything matching isReserved('op_'), which would delete the correlation ids the entire P4 differentiator depends on. The two documents also disagree on numbers for the same limits: backfill window 24 h (02 §15 TELEMETRY_MAX_BACKFILL_HOURS) versus 48 h (06 §4.1 step 3); attribute value cap 1024 bytes (02) versus 2048 (06 F4); attributes per span 64 (02 TELEMETRY_MAX_ATTRS) versus 128 (06 TELEMETRY_MAX_ATTRS_PER_SPAN). And 06 §4.1 step 0 specifies 'the same 202-and-drop semantics as track.router.ts:14' while 02 §4 and 10 D15 both argue at length that 202 is wrong for telemetry and must be 403, because an OTLP exporter reads any 2xx as delivered.

**Fix.** Fold 06 §4.1's seven steps into 02 §6.3 as the authoritative traces rewrite, keep 06 §4.1 as a pointer, and reconcile the five numbers in the blueprint's limits table. Add an explicit exception list to 02's isReserved/scrubAttrs contract for the correlation keys, with the snapshot-then-restore ordering stated as a contract (06 correctly notes 'the ordering is part of the contract, because step 2 restores what step 1 removed') and a test that a span arriving with op_project_id, op_session_id and op_root comes out with exactly one op_project_id from the token, the session id preserved only on a local root, and op_root gone. Change 06 §4.1 step 0 to 403 and cite 02 §4. Re-cost: 02's '4 rewrites, 4–6 d' does not include any of this and 06 budgets it separately at 2–3 d, so one of the two estimates is double-counting or the work is unowned.

### M78. `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** Metering and quota are specified twice, incompatibly, at both the column and the key level. 08 D4 ships `subscriptionPeriodTelemetryCount BigInt @default(0)` and `subscriptionPeriodTelemetryLimit BigInt?` with `limit === null` meaning not-provisioned and doubling as the entitlement signal, and argues at length that `Int @default(0)` "closed an open decision by accident and closed it wrong". 10 D12 assumes exactly `Int @default(0)` and defines `limit <= 0` as unlimited, citing the same trap from the opposite direction. The Redis schemes also differ: 02 §7.5 specifies a per-UTC-day, org-keyed hash `telemetry:usage:{YYYYMMDD}:{organizationId}` with nine per-project raw-unit fields and TTL 40 days, and hands draining to billing; 10 D10/D11 specifies a *billing-period*-keyed hash that rotates with the Polar cycle and a single composite billing unit (1 data point = 1, 1 KiB log = 8, 1 span = 30, weights explicitly unmeasured). 10 also puts the retention tier at `SAMPLES_DAYS: "90"` while 04 §2.3 sets `GIGAPIPE_RETENTION_DAYS="7"` and uses it to clamp read queries — so the read path would refuse to query 83 of the 90 days that are retained.

**Fix.** 10 owns billing and retention; 08 owns columns; 02 owns the counters the gateway can observe. Make 08's column shape authoritative (nullable is genuinely better — it distinguishes unprovisioned from zero-allowance and needs no feature-flag table) and rewrite 10 D12 against it. Make 10's period-keyed hash and composite unit authoritative and rewrite 02 §7.5's key scheme to match, keeping 02's raw per-signal fields as the underlying counters. Set `GIGAPIPE_RETENTION_DAYS` from the same constant as `SAMPLES_DAYS`.

### M79. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The telemetry deletion sweep is named as the gate on ever enabling the feature, yet no phase owns it and one document schedules it three phases late. 02 §17 states: 'the sweep must exist and pass a test before `TELEMETRY_ENABLED` is turned on for any tenant', and explains why — `deleteFromClickhouse` deletes by `WHERE project_id IN (…)` and no gigapipe table has a `project_id` column, so 'every project deletion (including the one the wind-down cron arms via `deleteAt`) silently leaves all telemetry behind forever, including OTLP log bodies, which will be the most PII-dense data the product has ever stored.' 11 makes it gate **1.9** (P1) and its Interfaces table flags 'a call from `deleteFromClickhouse` or `jobDelete`' as 'the call site is the part currently owned by nobody'. But 08's section heading files the erasure ledger under 'P4/P5/P7' and prices `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse` at 2 d with no phase assignment, and 10-ops has no sweep row in its P0 or P1.

**Fix.** Assign `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse`, its call inside `deleteFromClickhouse` (08 S15 already argues correctly for that call site, covering both `cron.delete.ts:46` and `admin/src/commands/delete-organization.ts:191`), the `TelemetryErasure` ledger and gate 1.9 explicitly to P1, owned by 08. Move 08's §4 heading accordingly. Make 'gate 1.9 green' the literal precondition on flipping the flag for any tenant.

### M80. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** The quota shed hook is scheduled in two different phases by the two docs that own it. Doc 10 D13 chooses P2 and explicitly rejects P1 ("A shed with no preceding warning is a support incident, and in P1 the column it compares against does not exist"), sizing the hook, the limit column, the daily rollup and both emails inside its 10-14 day P2 block. Doc 02 D7 says "P1 ships cardinality *observation* only … Enforcement (bounded exact set + `SMISMEMBER`) and the volume-quota gate land in **P6**, where the plan already puts quotas", and its effort table lists the quota gate under "Deferred to P6". The parent phase plan agrees with doc 02. So the same `telemetry-quota.hook.ts` is a P2 deliverable in one doc and a P6 deferral in the other, and doc 10's argument (telemetry volume is bounded by configuration, not traffic — "one `logger.debug` in a hot loop is 100× overnight") is the stronger one and is not addressed by doc 02.

**Fix.** Settle in the blueprint. Doc 10's risk argument should win — an unbounded shared `samples_v3` with no shed is the failure that takes out analytics — which means the limit column, the rollup and the emails move into P2 and doc 02 D7 must be amended. If P6 wins instead, doc 10 must state what protects the shared ClickHouse instance in the meantime beyond the §6 profile.

### M81. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** The tRPC router namespace is unresolved and every stream has already written call sites against its own answer. 04 mounts `observability: observabilityRouter` with `metrics.*`, `logs.*`, `traces.*`; 06 §6.6 lists seven procedures under `observability.traces.*`; 03 §Interfaces consumes `observability.metrics.chart`; but 05 §5.3 creates `packages/trpc/src/routers/logs.ts` exporting `logsRouter` and every 05 call site is `trpc.logs.*`. 09 Q1 flags it as blocking ("The UI cannot import three namespaces for one feature") and 11 Q1 flags the package-location half as "blocking — before anyone writes a test file". Beyond the name, the base procedures differ too: 04 D13 `protectedProcedure.use(rejectShareId)` plus a kill switch, 05 a plain `protectedProcedure.use(telemetryGate)` throwing `PRECONDITION_FAILED`, 01 §7.1 a hand-rolled `publicProcedure` with its own session and access checks.

**Fix.** Close it now, in 00-blueprint.md, with one line: `observability.{metrics,logs,traces,status}` on one router, base procedure per 04 D13. 04 D13 is right against 01 §7.1 — re-deriving `enforceAccess` opts the one router the plan calls a security boundary out of every future change to the repo's central access middleware — so 01 §7.1's code block should be replaced by a pointer, and 05 §5.3's `logsRouter` folded in.

### M82. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `05-logs.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Three tRPC router namespaces for one feature. 04-read-path D6 ships a sibling `observability.*` router and rejects branching inside `chart.chart`; 06-traces and 11-testing consume `observability.traces.*` and `observability.status`. 03-metrics D4 centralises on `executeReport()` called from `chart.chart`/`chart.aggregate` and all seven existing executor call sites. 09-ui, the consumer of all of it, calls `trpc.telemetry.capabilities`, `trpc.telemetry.logs.query`, `trpc.telemetry.chart`, `trpc.telemetry.labels.*` **and** `trpc.chart.chart` in the same document. 05-logs uses `logs.*`. Worse, 04 D6's stated *consequence* — "the share path is out of scope for this work-stream. There is no stored report that can hold a metric series, so 'a shared metric report' is not constructible" — is false the moment 08's migration and 03's/09's union member land, which are in the same release. 04 then specifies a share branch that "hard rejects anything that is not provably a metric report", i.e. it hard-rejects the exact case the other streams are building.

**Fix.** Decide in the blueprint. The cheapest coherent answer is: metric *reports* (saved, shared, on dashboards, via MCP/REST) go through `chart.chart` + `executeReport` per 03, because that is what makes decision #4 pay off; the *explorers'* ad-hoc surfaces (log query, trace search, label pickers, capabilities, status) go through `observability.*` per 04. Then rewrite 04 D6's consequence paragraph, delete `telemetry.*` from 09, and align 05's `logs.*` under `observability.logs.*`.

### M83. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Four homes are proposed for the gigapipe client and its table-name helper, three documents mark the question blocking, and nobody is named to decide. 04 D1: a workspace package `packages/gigapipe`. 04 D12: `TELEMETRY_DB` / `TELEMETRY_TABLES` / `G()` live in `packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts`. 05 §7.2: `gigapipeTable()` lives in `packages/db/src/gigapipe/table-name.ts`. 08 S10/S11: `getTelemetryClient()` and `TELEMETRY_TABLES` live in `packages/db/src/clickhouse/telemetry-client.ts`. 04 Q1 calls it blocking 'for the client'; 11 Q1 says it must be settled 'before anyone writes a test file' and every path in 11 containing `packages/gigapipe/` depends on it; 09 Q1 says the UI 'cannot import three namespaces for one feature'. 11 additionally needs a single exported telemetry-table constant shared by the retention sweep, the deletion function and the T2 teardown (I14).

**Fix.** Decide in P0, in one hour, and record it in `00-blueprint.md`. Recommend `packages/gigapipe` (04 D1) for transport, errors, lease, killswitch, labels and compilers, with the ClickHouse-side pieces — `getTelemetryClient`, `TELEMETRY_TABLES`, `gigapipeTable` — in `packages/db` since they need the existing client machinery. Then sweep the file paths in 03, 05, 08, 09 and 11 in a single mechanical PR.

### M84. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `07-alerting.md`

**Problem.** `topk` in the compiler is both required and forbidden. 04 §8.2: "`maxSeries` is passed to the tenancy compiler, which wraps any query carrying a `groupBy` in `topk(maxSeries, ...)` — **server-side, in PromQL**", and 04 §8.2 further makes a response with more than `maxSeries` series a `GigapipeUpstreamError`. 09 D4 repeats it ("`compileMetricQuery` takes a required `maxSeries` and wraps breakdowns in `topk`"). 03 D8 rejects it outright — "**It is not a cap.** `topk` is a Prometheus aggregation operator evaluated independently at every evaluation timestamp of a range query" — and 07 D4 rejects it again for a second reason ("for an `lt` rule it keeps the *healthiest* series and discards exactly the ones that should fire. The rule would never alert, forever, with no error anywhere"). 03 §Interfaces amendments 1, 4 and 5 already ask 04 to delete these lines; 04 still contains them.

**Fix.** 03 and 07 are right and their evidence is independent. Apply 03's six amendments to 04 (delete the `topk` wrap at §8.2 and the `maxSeries`-exceeded `GigapipeUpstreamError` at the same section; fix or retire `clampStep`; raise `cfg.maxPoints`; add `maxRankSeries`/`metricFanoutConcurrency`/`metricDeadlineMs`), and rewrite 09 D4's `limit` paragraph, which is currently justified by the `topk` wrap.

### M85. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** `FinalChart` — the single type that decision #4 stakes everything on — is extended by two documents and declared inviolate by a third. 03-metrics D7 adds `notices?: MetricNotice[]` and argues persuasively that without it seven separate degradations (DST bucket drift, rate-window widening, window clamping, rank-window truncation, interval widening, fill records, series limiting) are silently discarded. 09-ui D6 adds `compiled?: string` to the chart response so the explorer can show the emitted PromQL including the injected matcher. 04-read-path D5 states "`metrics.chart` returns `FinalChart` unchanged", refuses to add even a `clampedByRetention` field, and rejects a `{ chart, meta }` wrapper — surfacing the clamp out-of-band through `observability.status` instead. All three arguments are individually sound; they cannot all be implemented. Note also that no test anywhere in the repo currently asserts the `FinalChart` shape, so nothing will catch divergence between the two producers.

**Fix.** Allow optional additive fields (03's `notices` and 09's `compiled` are both optional and every verified consumer — `useRechartDataModel`, `createFlatRows`, `useVisibleSeries`, the MCP shaper — is inert to extra keys), and amend 04 D5 to say so while keeping its correct rule that no *required* field may be added and no wrapper introduced. Then write the `FinalChart` conformance suite against the *event* engine first, before the metrics engine exists, so the contract has an executable specification — this is a cheap de-risking step none of the eleven budgets.

### M86. `04-read-path.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Metric-name and label enumeration is specified two ways, and each doc calls the other's approach unsafe or unusable. 04 D4: "Metric names, label keys and label values come from ClickHouse directly, not from gigapipe", with six verified reasons (silent row loss in `GenericLabelReq`, a hardcoded `Limit: 10000`, a `panic(err)` on a malformed `match[]`, metadata living in a different table) and full SQL in §9.3 against `gigapipe.time_series`/`time_series_gin`. 03 §7's `resolveMetricCatalog` goes through gigapipe: `GET /api/v1/label/__name__/values` with `match[]={op_project_id="<pid>"}`. 09 §4.3 does the same and adds `GET /api/v1/series` for `metrics.meta`. Both routes are on 01 §4.6's `GIGAPIPE_ROUTES` allow-list, so nothing structurally prevents either — but two implementations of the same picker with different failure modes will drift, and 04's `telemetry-metadata.service.ts` and 03's `catalog.ts` would both exist.

**Fix.** 04's evidence is the strongest in the set and its queries are already written against the real sort keys; adopt direct ClickHouse. Rewrite 03 §7's `resolveMetricCatalog` to call 04 §9.3.1's `getMetricNames` (which already returns `{name, type, help, unit, seriesCount}` from `time_series.metadata`, exactly what 03 D6 needs for the required `metricType` — and it removes 03's dependency on the `_bucket`-sibling/`_total`-suffix heuristic entirely). Rewrite 09 §4.3's two endpoint notes to say the gateway reads ClickHouse. Then remove `promLabels`, `promSeries` and `promLabelValues` from 01 §4.6's allow-list, or mark them explicitly unused-but-allow-listed as 04 D4 does.

### M87. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Three specs disagree about whether `FinalChart` may gain fields — the type whose stability is the whole point of plan decision #4. 03 D7 adds one: `FinalChart = { series, metrics, notices?: MetricNotice[] }`, and makes it the delivery channel for seven degradations (DST drift, rate-window widening, interval widening, rank truncation, fill, series limiting). 04 D5 forbids it: "Adding a `clampedByRetention` field would break the property that makes decision 4 of the plan pay off" and routes its one out-of-band fact through `observability.status` instead. 09 §Interfaces adds two more: item 4 `chart.chart`/`chart.aggregate` output "gains optional `compiled?: string`" and item 6 needs "`truncated?: boolean` alongside `compiled`". 11 §4.1's `assertFinalChart` pins eight invariants and mentions none of the three fields, so the contract test would not cover them.

**Fix.** Settle the rule rather than the instances: optional, additive, never-set-by-`format()` fields on `FinalChart` are permitted, because every renderer types against `IChartData = RouterOutputs['chart']['chart']` structurally (09 §5) and an added optional key is assignable. Then take all three fields together — `notices`, `compiled`, `truncated` — in one edit to `packages/validation/src/types.validation.ts`, owned by 03. Rewrite 04 D5 to say retention is surfaced via `status` *by choice*, not because the type is closed. Add the three optional fields to 11 §4.1's `assertFinalChart` so the contract test covers them.

### M88. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Three different chart-response envelopes for one procedure. 03 D2: `MetricChartResult = FinalChart & { notices: MetricNotice[] }`, and D2 explicitly **rejects** adding a field to `FinalChart`. 04 D5: `IObservabilityChartResult { chart: FinalChart; resolution: {...} }` — no notices. 09 D14/D15: the response carries `series`, `metrics`, `notices` **and** `compiled`, and D15 asserts "`03-metrics-engine.md` D7 adds `notices?: MetricNotice[]` to `FinalChart`" — which is the opposite of what 03 D2 says. The notice code sets are also disjoint: 03 has `series_capped, interval_coarsened, rate_window_widened, increase_window_pinned, gaps_filled_with_zero, non_finite_samples_dropped, previous_period_unavailable`; 09 has `dst_bucket_drift, rate_window_widened, window_clamped, interval_widened, rank_window_truncated, series_filled, series_limited`. One code overlaps. 09's UI is specified against a response no server spec produces.

**Fix.** 03 owns the engine return type and D2's structural-supertype approach is right (it preserves 04 D5's stated property without editing `FinalChart`). Merge 04's `resolution` fields into `MetricChartResult` as additional properties rather than a wrapper, so there is one envelope. Rewrite 09 D15's notice list to 03's seven codes and its D14 `compiled` field as an explicit addition to `MetricChartResult` that 03 must accept or reject — today it appears in no engine spec.

### M89. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Three point budgets and two step algorithms for one grid. 03 §6.1/§6.3: `resolveStep` walks `MAX_SUB_BUCKET[interval]` (the **coarsest** sub-bucket) and coarsens the interval when the budget is exceeded, at `cfg.maxPoints = 3000`, and §Interfaces amendment 4 says 04's loop "returns the *smallest* fitting ladder value, which contradicts its own worked examples at `:1365-1370` and costs 2-4x". 04 §4.3 still ships `resolveStepMs` with `TARGET_MAX_POINTS = 1_500` and a find-first-fitting loop. 09 Interfaces item 5 cites "8 000-point engine ceiling under gigapipe's 11 000". The three numbers produce three different charts for the same `(range, interval)`.

**Fix.** 03 owns the grid; apply its amendment 4 to 04 (delete or rewrite `resolveStepMs`/`clampStep`, raise `maxPoints` to 3000) and correct 09's 8 000 to whatever the settled number is. Note this also decides 04 D9's `effectiveInterval` reporting versus 03's `interval_coarsened` notice — they are two mechanisms for the same fact and only one should ship.

### M90. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** 09's P2 ordering is inverted relative to both sibling specs, and it front-loads the riskiest untested change. 04 D4 is explicit: ship a sibling `observability.*` router and 'do not branch inside `chart.chart` yet', because `zReportInput` is a plain `z.object()` that strips an unknown `dataSource` (verified at `packages/validation/src/index.ts:233`) and `ctx.report` is null for every authenticated in-app query (verified at `chart.ts:598`). 03 files report adoption as 'P2.5 — specified, not shipped in P2' and 'separately schedulable'. But 09's Effort leads with **P2a** — `dataSource` plumbing across the eight write sites plus the §7.2 union audit — at 1.5–2 w, *ahead* of P2b (the explorer, 2–2.5 w). 09 itself ranks that union audit as 'the largest single risk' and 'eight files of casts the compiler will not help with, in the middle of the report editor, which has no tests'. Doing it before any metric chart exists means the riskiest shared-code change in the stream is validated by nothing.

**Fix.** Reorder 09's P2 to: P2a′ (the `data` prop) → P2a″ (shared-component fixes) → P2b (explorer, which proves the engine end-to-end through `observability.metrics.chart`) → P2c (settings/token/snippet) → P2a (persistence and the eight write sites), the last renamed P2.5 to match 03. This is also what 03 §15.2's staged release requires, since the release-N soak must have elapsed before the union member ships.

### M91. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** The UI offers two chart types for metric reports that the engine spec excludes for verified correctness reasons. 09 D7 allows `linear, area, bar, histogram, pie, metric`. 03's v1 cut allows `linear, area, bar, metric`, and D9 gives the reason: `sum()` rounds to two decimals (verified, packages/common/src/math.ts:20-21), so any metric whose bucket values sit below 0.005 — error ratios, per-second rates on low-traffic routes, latency quantiles in seconds — has `metrics.sum === 0` on every series, at which point `pie/chart.tsx:81`'s `serie.metrics.sum / sum` is `0/0 = NaN` (verified) and `format.ts:153`'s sort is a no-op so `limit`'s `slice(0, limit)` keeps an arbitrary subset. 03 excludes `pie` specifically to delete that case rather than define behaviour for it. 09 also never mentions `histogram`, which routes to `chart.chart` and whose bucketing semantics for a metric series are undefined in both documents.

**Fix.** 09 D7 adopts 03's list. Independently, land 03 D9's fix — `format()` sorts on a locally computed unrounded total while `metrics.sum` keeps its rounded display value — which 03 correctly shows is provably a no-op for the event engine, since event counts are non-negative integers for which `round(x, 2) === x`. Without it the wrong five routes are shown on every low-magnitude metric chart, silently.

### M92. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`

**Problem.** `09-ui-surfaces.md` D14 and D15 are built on a change to `FinalChart` that `03-metrics-engine.md` D2 explicitly forbids, and the leak mitigation exists only in 09. 03 D2 rejects adding any field to `FinalChart` — including an optional one — because every renderer types against `IChartData = RouterOutputs['chart']['chart']`, and puts notices on a structural supertype `MetricChartResult = FinalChart & { notices }` returned by one resolver. 09 D15 states "03-metrics-engine.md D7 adds `notices?: MetricNotice[]` to `FinalChart`" and D14 additionally puts `compiled?: string` — the full compiled PromQL, containing the internal project id and every label matcher — on the chart response. 09 D14 correctly identifies that `chartProcedure` serves anonymous share viewers and that today's `FinalChart` carries no project identifier at all, and mitigates with "`compiled` is populated only when `ctx.report === null`". But that rule is a single conditional in a resolver with no test coverage anywhere in the repo (verified: no test file references `FinalChart`, `ChartEngine` or `chartProcedure`), and 03 — which owns the type — does not know about it.

**Fix.** Reconcile 09 D14/D15 with 03 D2 in one direction. Preferred: keep `compiled` and `notices` off `FinalChart` entirely and put both on `03-metrics-engine.md`'s `MetricChartResult` / `04-read-path.md`'s `IObservabilityChartResult.resolution`, which are returned only by authenticated `observability.*` procedures — that removes the share-path leak by construction rather than by a conditional. If `compiled` must reach a saved-report surface, make its omission on the share path a `ctx.report !== null` assertion in the transport rather than in a resolver, and keep `11-testing-strategy.md` T9 as a required gate.

### M93. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** The plan does not have one answer to "is OpenPanel Cloud's ClickHouse clustered", and the answer changes P0. I verified `isClickhouseClustered()` (packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts:83-93): it returns **true** unless `SELF_HOSTED` is set — so Cloud is clustered by default. 05-logs L13 states this as fact and branches its whole schema on it, correctly noting that a plain `MergeTree` pre-create on a multi-node cluster makes gigapipe write non-replicated local tables and reads return a random subset with no error. 04-read-path §2.4 marks it **UNVERIFIED** and says "If it is, §2.4 is a P0 blocker, not a P2 detail" — and identifies a second landmine: with `distributed_product_mode: 'allow'` set globally (verified, client.ts:114), fingerprint subqueries silently drop rows rather than erroring, so its direct-SQL metadata path returns wrong answers on a cluster. 06-traces T11 adds a third position (a `GIGAPIPE_CLUSTERED` signal deliberately independent of `isClickhouseClustered()`). Meanwhile 08-schema D8/D9 declares "Clustered telemetry is out of scope for P0. Ship `chTelemetryDDL` only… Single-node telemetry topology", and 10-ops ships a single-node compose with `CLUSTER_NAME` deliberately unset. So the deployment that most needs the tenancy boundary is the one the schema and ops streams have declared out of scope.

**Fix.** Settle it before P0 by reading Cloud's `CLICKHOUSE_CLUSTER`/`SELF_HOSTED` values — it is a five-minute lookup and it gates whether 10's `gigapipe-provision.sql` must emit `ReplicatedMergeTree ON CLUSTER` (which it can only do *before* gigapipe's first boot, since `PARTITION BY` is not alterable), whether 04's and 06's direct SQL needs `GLOBAL IN`/`GLOBAL JOIN`, and whether 08 D8/D9 are tenable. If Cloud is clustered, 08 D8/D9 must be reversed and the clustered DDL is P0 work, not deferred.

### M94. `04-read-path.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** Four capability/status surfaces. 04 §6.5 defines `observability.status` returning `{enabled, reachable, schemaReady, hasData, signals:{metrics,logs,traces}, retentionDays, oldestQueryableAt, database, clustered, version, degraded}` and D10 guarantees it never throws for an authorized caller. 09 Interfaces item 9 and its sidebar/route/settings-tab gating are built on `telemetry.capabilities → {enabled, hasMetrics, hasLogs, hasTraces, patterns, blocked}` — a different procedure, a different shape, plus a `blocked` field and a `patterns` field 04 does not have. 05 D12 uses a server-side boolean `telemetryEnabled` on the app context plus `isTelemetryEnabled()`. 06 §15 flips `observability.status.signals.traces` from an env var.

**Fix.** One procedure. 04 owns it; extend `observability.status` with 09's `blocked` (which it genuinely needs, for the wind-down banner) and drop `patterns` unless `LOG_DRILLDOWN` is turned on. Rewrite 09's `telemetry.capabilities` references, its sidebar query and its Q-list to `observability.status`, and have 05's app-context flag derive from the same procedure rather than a second boolean.

### M95. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** Three incompatible retention regimes and three values for `SAMPLES_DAYS`, which every doc agrees is the one number that must not drift. 05 §8.1/§8.3: `SAMPLES_DAYS: "30"` "TREAT AS IMMUTABLE AFTER INSTALL", conditional TTL logs 30 d / metrics 395 d. 08 §11.1: `TELEMETRY_LOGS_RETENTION_DAYS` 14, `TELEMETRY_METRICS_RETENTION_DAYS` 90, `TELEMETRY_TRACES_RETENTION_DAYS` 7, `SAMPLES_DAYS = max(all) + 1` = 91. 10 §2/§8.2/§8.4: `SAMPLES_DAYS: "90"`, table TTL metrics 90 / logs 30 / traces 30, plus a four-tier per-plan matrix (trial 7/3/3 … scale 90/30/30) with a 7-day downgrade grace and a nightly partition sweep. 04 §2.3 has a fourth number: `GIGAPIPE_RETENTION_DAYS="7"`. The env var names also collide: `OP_TELEMETRY_RETENTION_{METRICS,LOGS,TRACES}_DAYS` (10 §4.3) vs `TELEMETRY_{LOGS,METRICS,TRACES}_RETENTION_DAYS` (08 §11.1). Since `SAMPLES_DAYS` is stamped by `rotateTables` onto eight tables and a change re-arms rotation over any hand-applied TTL, three values means the retention design silently loses to whichever compose surface ships.

**Fix.** 10 owns retention; adopt its numbers, its `TELEMETRY_RETENTION` tier matrix and its `TELEMETRY_RETENTION_MAX_DAYS = 90 = SAMPLES_DAYS` coupling. Rewrite 05 §8.1/§8.3 (its `SAMPLES_DAYS: "30"` and 395-day metric window both go) and 08 §11.1 (its three constants become references to 10's `TELEMETRY_RETENTION`). Change 04's `GIGAPIPE_RETENTION_DAYS` into a read of 10's `getTelemetryRetentionTier(org)` — 04 §8.5 already anticipates this ("Until it exists, this work-stream ships a constant"), so it only needs the name and default aligned. Standardise the env prefix on `OP_TELEMETRY_RETENTION_*_DAYS` (10's, which is the one that reaches `.env.template`).

### M96. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** The self-hosted gigapipe version-skew problem has no owner. 10 §11.2 is a sound upgrade runbook *for the OpenPanel team* — "read the schema diff first: `git diff v5.4.1..vNEXT -- ctrl/qryn/sql/`", "run the init container alone first, on a copy or in a maintenance window", "re-check the fan-out arithmetic" — none of which a self-hoster can perform. Meanwhile every mechanism points the other way: the generated `docker-compose.yml` is gitignored and hand-edited; `get_latest_images` deliberately does not manage the gigapipe tag (10 §4.2); `./update` pulls the `self-hosting` branch, not `main` (05 OQ9); and gigapipe exposes no usable version endpoint — 04:862-865 establishes that `/api/v1/status/buildinfo` writes `uc.Version` verbatim and is always the empty string, and 11 D14/K22 gets the version from `docker inspect` on the *container runtime*, which `apps/api` cannot do. So the fleet fragments across gigapipe versions, OpenPanel has no way to learn which version it is talking to, there is no supported-version range, no boot-time compatibility assertion, and no degradation path. Every line-number-pinned behaviour in 01/03/04/05/06 is valid "only for the pinned tag" (05 §14) against a tag the operator controls and OpenPanel cannot observe.

**Fix.** 10-ops and 04 jointly: publish a `GIGAPIPE_SUPPORTED_VERSIONS` range as a checked-in constant; at boot, probe schema shape rather than version (the `system.tables` read 08 §10 already builds will tell you whether `samples_v3` has `type` in the PARTITION BY and whether `type_v2` exists) and surface the result on `observability.status` as a degraded state with a named remedy; write an operator-facing upgrade page in `self-hosting/observability.mdx` covering the init-container-first sequence and the "there is no downgrade" fact 10 §11.2 already establishes. ~3–5 days.

### M97. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Live tail is built in P3 by one doc and deferred to P6 by two others, and doc 04's P3 estimate includes the work the other two say not to do. Doc 04 D7 and §10 specify a server-side 1 s poll exposed as a WebSocket at `GET /live/logs/:projectId`, with `wsProjectLogs` in `live.controller.ts`, a tail lease, filter-change-is-a-reconnect semantics, and per-user/per-project tail caps — and folds "live tail + the `publisher.ts` refcount" into its 3.0-week P3. Doc 05 L5 defers live tail to P6 with two reasons doc 04 does not answer (multi-replica `apps/api` means one poller per query *per replica*, and nothing bounds `TailKey`s per project) and ships "a Live toggle that sets `refetchInterval: 10_000`". Doc 09 D10 agrees with doc 05 and specifies the polling composition in detail — at 5 s, not 10 s.

**Fix.** Adopt doc 05 L5 / doc 09 D10 (polling in P3, socket in P6) — the multi-replica argument is decisive and neither doc 04 D7 nor §10 addresses it. Remove the tail work from doc 04's P3 scope and re-estimate that phase; keep the `publisher.ts` refcount, which is independently justified and should move to P0 per the parallelisable-work finding. Reconcile the interval (5 s vs 10 s) while you are there.

### M98. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Three incompatible contracts for the same log-query procedure. Response shape: 04 §6.2 returns `{ entries: [{ts, timestampNs, line, labels, level, traceId, spanId, sessionId, profileId}], nextCursor, truncated }`; 05 §6.5 returns `{ lines: IServiceLogLine[], hasMore, cursor, clampedToRetention }`; 09 §Interfaces returns `{ rows: [{timestampNs, line, labels}], meta: { next: {endNs} | null } }`. Cursor: 04 uses `"<timestampNs>:<first 8 hex of sha1(line)>"`; 05 §6.4 argues that is insufficient ("Neither `timestampNs` nor `${streamHash}:${timestampNs}` is unique") and specifies `zLogCursor = { endNs, skipBoundarySecond }` with a per-key occurrence multiset and a boundary-second force-advance; 09 uses a bare `endNs`. Histogram: 04 §6.2 decides "the histogram **never** includes the `search` term" to keep the query on the `metrics_15s` rollup; 05 §5.2 decides the opposite — "We accept the line filters (a histogram that ignores the user's search is a lie)". Only 05 has `clampedToRetention`, which 05 §5.1 makes the sole signal that the window was clamped.

**Fix.** 05 owns logs and its cursor analysis is the only one that survives the millisecond-clock case it documents; adopt 05 §6.4/§6.5 wholesale and rewrite 04 §6.2 and 09's `logs.query` signature to it. On the histogram, 05's user-facing argument beats 04's cost argument — but 04's rollup-shortcut analysis is correct and should be preserved as a documented cost note plus a UI affordance, not as a silent filter drop. Reconcile in one place and delete the losing paragraph rather than leaving both.

### M99. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Live tail is designed, deferred and re-designed across three docs. 04 D7 builds it in P3: a server-side 1 s poll behind `GET /live/logs/:projectId` (WebSocket), `wsProjectLogs` in `live.controller.ts`, per-user and per-project tail leases, a 30-minute hard session lifetime, a `telemetry:batch` Redis nudge channel, and a prerequisite refcount fix to `subscribeToPublishedEvent` in `packages/redis/publisher.ts` (D8) — all sized into 04's P3 estimate. 05 L5 cancels it: "live tail is **deferred to P6**; P3 ships a polled refresh" with `refetchInterval: 10_000`, on the grounds that "`apps/api` is multi-replica … 'one poller per distinct compiled query' is therefore one poller *per query per replica*, and nothing in the draft bounded the number of `TailKey`s". 09 D10 also says polling but with `refetchInterval: 5000`, and specifies the Live/paginated composition (buffer capped at 1000, evict oldest, "Load older" disabled) that neither other doc mentions.

**Fix.** Adopt 05 L5 (defer to P6) — its multi-replica objection is decisive and 04's own design concedes the fan-out. Move 04 §10 in full to a P6 section, keeping the analysis (it is better than anything in 05 or 09) but removing it from 04's P3 scope, effort table and test list (T-25…T-29). Reconcile the poll interval between 05 (10 s) and 09 (5 s) — 09 owns the UI so its number wins unless 05 objects on cost. Note that 04 D8's `subscribeToPublishedEvent` refcount fix is a genuine pre-existing bug (it describes two `wsVisitors`/`wsProjectEvents` tabs reproducing it today); keep it as a standalone fix, not a tail prerequisite.

### M100. `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** The trace read path is specified twice, incompatibly, and 06 flags this itself without it being resolved. 04 §6.3 routes `observability.traces.search` → `GET /api/search?q=<traceql>&limit&start&end` and `observability.traces.get` → a two-step scoped-search-then-`GET /api/traces/{traceId}`, with `assertTraceqlScoped` as a prerequisite; 01 §4.4 and §4.6 build the TraceQL compiler and allow-list entries for exactly that. 06 T1/T2 rejects both: "Trace search is direct ClickHouse SQL from `apps/api`" and "Trace-by-id is direct SQL over `tempo_traces.payload`", because gigapipe's TraceQL `&&` is trace-scoped rather than span-scoped (`index_groupby.go:15-33` + `complex_and.go:16-44`) and because no correlation surface is expressible in TraceQL at all. 06 §Interfaces states the collision as an open amendment: "**Amendment to `read-path.md` §6.3, which its owner must accept or reject.** … **Neither is used.**" It remains unresolved, and 01's `compileTraceQL`, `filterTraceToProject` and the four Tempo allow-list entries exist only to serve 04's rejected design.

**Fix.** Accept 06's amendment — its span-vs-trace scoping evidence is specific and checkable, and it eliminates a whole dialect (TraceQL) from the security boundary, which is a net reduction in risk. Rewrite 04 §6.3 to reference 06's direct-SQL procedures, delete `assertTraceqlScoped` from 04's Interfaces requests and from 01's exports, and remove `tempoSearch`/`tempoTagsV2`/`tempoValuesV2`/`tempoTraceById` from 01 §4.6's allow-list (P4 then depends on gigapipe's *writer* only, as 06 states). 01 §4.4's TraceQL analysis and D4's response-filtering design should be retained as an appendix documenting why the proxy route was rejected.

### M101. `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Two incompatible conventions for reading and mutating the gigapipe ClickHouse database from OpenPanel. 04 D12 and 06 §5 both put TELEMETRY_DB / TELEMETRY_TABLES / a G() or g() helper in packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts and route every statement through the existing chQuery / chQueryWithMeta (04 §9's three catalogue queries; 06's seven trace queries; 06 T14 depends on chQueryWithMeta's Int coercion behaviour). 08 S11 and 10 D19 mandate the opposite: a separate memoized getTelemetryClient() in packages/db/src/clickhouse/telemetry-client.ts, and 'never through ch, chQuery or chMigrationClient' — because chQuery takes ClickHouse settings, not query params, as its second argument and because ch/chQuery round-robin across every entry in a comma-separated CLICKHOUSE_URL with failover, so DDL that must be repeatable and readable-back needs one pinned node. 04 F21 documents that exact round-robin failure ('G() returns local shard tables and chQuery round-robins nodes, so autocomplete returns a different subset per call and hasData is a coin flip') and then uses chQuery anyway.

**Fix.** Decide in the blueprint. The distinction that actually matters is DDL/mutations versus reads: 08/10 are right that anything which must be written and then read back on the same node (migration 22, the TTL reconciler, the deletion sweep) needs a pinned client, and 04/06 are right that ordinary catalogue and trace reads want chQueryWithMeta's existing statistics logging and type coercion. Specify both: getTelemetryClient() for DDL and mutations, chQuery/chQueryWithMeta with TELEMETRY_TABLES for reads, and one shared TELEMETRY_DB / _dist-suffix decision that both consult. Reconcile 04's `G(key)` and 06's `g(base)` + getTelemetryMutationTable into one exported surface, and note that 04 F21's coin-flip is then a real read-path caveat rather than an unmitigated one.

### M102. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** The capability/status procedure is named and shaped differently by its provider and its consumer. 04 §6.4 defines `observability.status` returning `{ enabled, reachable, schemaReady, signals: {metrics, logs, traces}, retentionDays, oldestQueryableAt }` and calls it "the procedure that must never throw". 09 D15 builds its entire routing gate on `telemetry.capabilities` returning `{ enabled, hasMetrics, hasLogs, hasTraces, patterns, blocked: 'winddown'|'quota'|null }` — a different name, a flat rather than nested signal shape, no `schemaReady`, no `retentionDays`/`oldestQueryableAt`, plus two fields 04 does not provide (`patterns`, `blocked`). 06 §13 consumes "`observability.status` … extended with `signals.traces`". 09's `beforeLoad` redirect, sidebar gating and five-state taxonomy all key on its own shape, and 04 §8.5 makes `retentionDays`/`oldestQueryableAt` the *only* channel for the retention clamp ("if nobody compares, nobody ever sees it") — a field 09 never reads.

**Fix.** Merge into one procedure on the router chosen above: `telemetry.status` returning `{ enabled, reachable, schemaReady, signals: { metrics, logs, traces }, patterns, blocked, retentionDays, oldestQueryableAt }`. 04 owns the implementation and the never-throws contract; 09 must consume `signals.metrics` rather than `hasMetrics` and must render 04 §8.5's `<RetentionNotice />` from `oldestQueryableAt`, which is currently specified by 04 and implemented by nobody. Update 06 §13's reference.

### M103. `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Two trace read paths are user-triggerable scans over every tenant's index, with no rate limit and a lease that fails open. `06-traces-and-correlation.md` §6.4's "load all tag keys" query and §13.3's `span_profile` CTE are declared sanctioned exceptions to the F2 prefix invariant: the outer read has no `key` predicate, so it scans the window's `tempo_traces_attrs_gin` rows for *every* project before the `(trace_id, span_id)` intersection prunes it. 06 §12's own arithmetic puts that at ~1.5 M gin rows per minute across all tenants, i.e. ~22 M rows at the 15-minute clamp. Correctness depends entirely on the intersection being present, so the query is one careless edit from being a cross-tenant read rather than a cross-tenant scan — and 06's T-2 test is the only thing enforcing the allow-list of named exceptions. The cost side has no brake: `04-read-path.md` §8.4's per-project lease fails open on any Redis error (F17, "these are fairness controls, not security controls"), there is no `rateLimitMiddleware` on `traces.tagKeys` in 06's tRPC surface, and `09-ui-surfaces.md` §4.5 wires the label combobox to fire on a user action with only a 10-minute `staleTime`.

**Fix.** Either promote 06 T16's gateway-written tag dictionary (`telemetry_trace_tag_keys`) from P6 to a P4 prerequisite — 06 itself prices it as "a rounding error next to the 2.8 GB/day the gateway already writes" — or require a `key` prefix on the outer read and drop the unprefixed variant. In the interim, add `rateLimitMiddleware` to `traces.tagKeys` and `traces.tagValues` in `06-traces-and-correlation.md` §6.6, and state in `04-read-path.md` §8.4 that the lease's fail-open posture means it is not a control for the three F2 exceptions.

### M104. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** Three distinct client paths hit the shared ClickHouse for telemetry and only one of them is bounded, so the noisy-neighbour control is incomplete in the direction that is easiest to trip. 10 §6 adds a `<gigapipe>` profile (`max_memory_usage` 4 GB, `max_execution_time` 60, `max_concurrent_queries_for_user` 16, `max_threads` 4) selected by `CLICKHOUSE_AUTH=gigapipe:` — that bounds path (1), gigapipe's own reads and writes.
