It does not touch path (2): OpenPanel's own telemetry queries, which 04 §9 routes through `chQuery` — the shared analytics client built from `CLICKHOUSE_URL` (`packages/db/src/clickhouse/client.ts:198-210`), whose URL in `.env.example:5` carries no username and therefore authenticates as `default`. The `default` profile in `self-hosting/clickhouse/clickhouse-user-config.xml` sets only `log_queries` and `log_query_threads` — no memory, time, thread or concurrency limit at all. Through that same unbounded `default` connection the plan issues: 04 §9's label and metric-name resolution against `time_series_gin` (a `(key,val)` index range on a table sized by every project's cardinality), 10 §8's retention `ALTER TABLE … MODIFY TTL`, 10 §8.4's layer-2 delete sweeps, and 08 §12's `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse` chunked mutations. 04 §9.2 sets `max_execution_time: 10` per metadata query, which is good but is one of five needed limits and covers only that one call site; the mutations carry nothing. And 04 §8's cost controls (lease per chart, step ladder, series cap) govern gigapipe HTTP calls, not this path.

**Fix.** 10 §6 should own a total ClickHouse budget covering all three paths, not just gigapipe's. Concretely: give OpenPanel's own telemetry work a distinct ClickHouse user with its own profile (an `openpanel_telemetry` user, or at minimum `SETTINGS` applied on every `chQuery` in `telemetry-metadata.service.ts` and on every retention/delete statement), and set explicit `max_memory_usage`/`max_execution_time`/`max_threads` on the mutation path — an unbounded `ALTER … DELETE` over `samples_v3` from the analytics pool is the single most likely way this plan degrades the existing product. 04 should state that its metadata path runs on the analytics connection pool and inherits its concurrency, so the reader knows §8's leases do not cover it. 08 should carry the same settings on its deletion statements. Roughly 2 days, and it belongs in P0 with the profile, not later.

### M105. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** Doc 09's sidebar snippet renders all four Observability nav items behind a single `capabilities?.enabled` guard, but its own route table ships `/metrics` and `/settings/telemetry` in P2, `/logs` in P3, `/traces` + `/traces/<id>` in P4 and `/services` in P6. At the end of P2 a user with telemetry enabled sees four links, three of which route to files that do not exist. Doc 04's `observability.status` already returns the per-signal information needed (`signals: { metrics: boolean; logs: boolean; traces: boolean }`) and doc 09 D15 hoists the capabilities query into the project route context — so the mechanism exists and is simply not wired to the sidebar. Separately, doc 09 D13 states this stream "must not ship the four nav items before that sweep exists" (telemetry deletion), which is a second gate on the same snippet that the snippet does not express.

**Fix.** Gate each sidebar entry on its own signal from `observability.status`/`telemetry.capabilities`, not on `enabled` alone, and add the deletion-sweep gate as an explicit condition in the P2 PR checklist rather than as prose in D13. One-line change now; four broken links in a released P2 otherwise.

### M106. `08-schema-changes.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Project-deletion telemetry erasure — the GDPR path and the wind-down terminus — is specified three times with three different mechanisms, three different table lists and three different ledgers, while `11-testing-strategy.md` D13/I14 requires the table list to be one exported constant shared by the delete path, the retention sweep and the test teardown. `08-schema-changes.md` §14 specifies `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse(projectIds)` called from inside `deleteFromClickhouse`, resolving fingerprints in Node with `FP_CHUNK`/`FP_CAP`, ordering bulk tables before the index, and a `TelemetryErasure` ledger. `05-logs.md` §7.4 specifies `purgeTelemetry({projectId, reason, olderThanNs, signals, resumeJobId})` with a `TelemetryPurgeJob` journal, a `gigapipeTable(name, 'read'|'mutate')` helper, a different ordering rationale, and instructs metrics and traces to "extend its `signals` union rather than writing their own purge". `06-traces-and-correlation.md` §11.6 specifies a third, `deleteTelemetryForProjects` in `delete.service.ts`, using lightweight `DELETE FROM` against `getTelemetryMutationTable()`. All three independently identify that `jobDelete()` has no try/catch and that an unguarded throw stops every project and organisation deletion on the deployment — which is correct and is the strongest argument for there being exactly one implementation.

**Fix.** Name one owner (08 is the natural one, since it owns `delete.service.ts` and the Prisma models) and have 05 §7.4 and 06 §11.6 consume it rather than redefine it. Reconcile the ledger to one model, the table list to one exported constant per `11-testing-strategy.md` I14, and the read/mutate table-name helper to one function — note 05 and 06 already disagree with each other on whether `getReplicatedTableName` may be applied to a gigapipe table. Keep `11-testing-strategy.md` gate 1.9 (run the real `jobDelete()` per push) as the acceptance test, and add a row asserting a throw from the telemetry purge does not prevent any other project or organisation from being deleted.

### M107. `05-logs.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** The one control that stops a telemetry query from taking down the analytics product is assigned three different phases by three documents, and nobody has reconciled them. 05 I10 verifies from source that gigapipe's reader sets no ClickHouse query limits at all — `createDataDBSessions` builds the reader's `clickhouse.Options` with `Settings: nil` (`reader/registry/registry.go:69`) while the *writer's* client does set them — and concludes: "The mitigation is a dedicated ClickHouse user with a settings profile (§7.4). **This is a P0 dependency of the logs work-stream, not a P3 nicety.**" 05:1848 puts the `gigapipe` user, grants and the `gigapipe_reader` profile (including `max_concurrent_queries_for_user`) in its P0 bootstrap. 08 §12 says of the same profile: "it is why that profile is not optional on a shared instance." 10 D16 ships gigapipe as `default` in v1 and makes the scoped `op_gigapipe` user plus profile and quota a **P7** follow-up, "deferred on risk budget, not on knowledge", with §5.3 arguing the residual risk is mitigated upstream instead (gigapipe's hardcoded 30 s PromQL timeout, server-side range/step caps, network isolation). Those upstream mitigations are real for PromQL but 05 I10's finding is specifically about LogQL regex scans, which is the case 05 says is unbounded. The three positions are reconcilable — a settings profile can be attached to `default` without creating a new user, separating the resource cap (cheap, P0) from the schema isolation (needs a generated password and a gitignored XML file, P7) — but no document has done that reconciliation, and the difference is whether one `|~` over a wide window can hurt the analytics product on day one.

**Fix.** 10-ops decides once and 05 and 08 adopt it. The obvious split: land the `<profiles><op_gigapipe>` block (`max_memory_usage`, `max_execution_time`, `max_threads`, `max_concurrent_queries_for_user`) in the tracked `self-hosting/clickhouse/clickhouse-user-config.xml` at P0 applied to the user gigapipe actually connects as, and keep the dedicated `op_gigapipe` user with scoped grants at P7 where 10 D16 puts it. Then make 05 §7.4 and 08 §12 point at that one decision instead of restating it. ~1 day to decide; the work is already budgeted twice.

### M108. `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES` is set to opposite values by two docs and rejected as a default by a third, which determines whether OpenPanel's hand-applied TTLs survive a restart. 05 §8.1's env manifest: `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES: "false"` with the comment "gigapipe owns its schema after our pre-create", and 05 §8.3 relies instead on `rotateTables`' settings-row short-circuit to protect its conditional TTL. 10 D3: "**Every** long-running node sets `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES`", `"true"` in the compose block, "Without this the long-running container re-runs ctrl.Rotate on every restart and overwrites our per-signal TTL". 08 D7: "**Rejected: `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES=true` as the default** … it also disables schema *upgrades*: a gigapipe image bump would run new reader code against an old schema with no error until a query fails." 11 §8.1 sets `"true"` in the CI compose and §11.2 assertion 5 tests that the TTL survives a restart because of it. All three arguments are correct about different things; the combination they need is not stated anywhere.

**Fix.** 10's three-container split already resolves this and the other docs have not caught up: `op-gigapipe-init` runs `MODE=init_only` **without** `OMIT_CREATE_TABLES` (so schema upgrades happen there, answering 08), and `op-gigapipe` runs `MODE=all` **with** it (so `ctrl.Rotate` never re-stamps, answering 10 and 11). Write that pairing explicitly into 10 D3, then rewrite 05 §8.1's manifest to match and delete 08 D7's rejection — replacing it with the note that schema upgrades are the init container's job and that its exit code is the upgrade-health signal.

### M109. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `05-logs.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Four documents depend on `system.query_log`, which is disabled at the server level on every OpenPanel ClickHouse surface, and the one fix proposed pulls the wrong lever. `self-hosting/clickhouse/clickhouse-config.xml` contains `<query_log remove="remove"/>` (line 16 of the dev copy at `docker/clickhouse/clickhouse-config.xml`; the self-hosting copy is identical), alongside `<part_log remove="remove"/>`, `<metric_log remove="remove"/>` and `<asynchronous_metric_log remove="remove"/>`. That file is mounted into `config.d/` on all three surfaces — self-hosting template, `docker-compose.yml` dev (line 29), and `.github/smoke/docker-compose.yml` (line 37). Removing the `<query_log>` server element disables the table entirely; a per-user `log_queries` setting cannot resurrect a table that has no configuration. So 10 §6's remedy — `<log_queries>1</log_queries>` inside the new `<gigapipe>` profile, with the comment "On, deliberately: this is the workload we cannot yet predict" — does nothing, even though 10:585 correctly diagnoses the problem ("because query logging is off there is no `system.query_log` to diagnose from"). Everything built on top then fails silently: 10 §Failure modes line 1908 detects "a runaway telemetry query starves analytics" via "`system.query_log` for the `gigapipe` user (enabled in its profile)"; 05's two blocking pre-P3 spikes (line 2219 on the `samples_v3` sort key, line 2221 on purge-mutation cost) both read `system.query_log`; and 11:1693 reads the reader's `system.query_log` to assert which table a query touched. `part_log`'s removal similarly removes merge-cost forensics, which 05:1891 also wants.

**Fix.** Verified reading of the config files; I could not execute ClickHouse to confirm the runtime behaviour, so budget one command to check (`SELECT count() FROM system.query_log` after a query on the smoke stack). The fix belongs in 10 §6 alongside the profile: re-enable `<query_log>` at server level with a bounded TTL and a small flush interval rather than removing it — e.g. a `<query_log>` block with `<ttl>event_date + INTERVAL 3 DAY DELETE</ttl>` — and keep `log_queries=0` on the `default` profile so analytics still writes nothing, with `log_queries=1` only on `gigapipe`. Consider the same for `part_log` if 05:1891's merge-cost spike is to be answerable. One line of XML plus a note in `environment-variables.mdx` about the added table; under a day, but it gates two of 05's blocking spikes so it should land before P3 planning, not after.

### M110. `06-traces-and-correlation.md`

**Problem.** The session-correlation differentiator propagates identifiers outside the customer's trust boundary and only the browser half of that is bounded. Doc 06 §8 puts `op_session_id` and `op_profile_id` into a W3C `baggage` header, and §11 acknowledges `op_profile_id` "is whatever the customer passed to `identify()` — frequently an email address". The controls listed are all on the browser→server hop: `propagateSession` defaults false, `propagateProfileId` requires a second opt-in, same-origin only by default, `propagateSessionTargets` for cross-origin. But §8.6's `OpenPanelCorrelationProcessor` reads baggage from the OTel context, and OTel's default composite propagator **injects** `baggage` on every outbound instrumented request the customer's backend makes. Once the header is in the server's context, an email address is forwarded to every third-party API that backend calls — payment processors, LLM providers, support tools — with no per-target control, no size bound on the outbound side, and no mention in the doc. §11's list of five places `op_profile_id` lands stops at the customer's own storage; the sixth is other people's logs.

**Fix.** Add a §11 control for the server hop: document that customers adopting `OpenPanelCorrelationProcessor` must restrict baggage propagation on egress (an OTel propagator configuration, or a `baggage` strip in their egress client), and recommend propagating a per-project pseudonymous id rather than the raw `identify()` value. Better: make `op_profile_id` propagation carry a hashed value by default with the mapping resolved server-side at read time, so the identifier that crosses the wire is not the identifier the customer typed. Note it in the privacy documentation the doc already promises, and add it to the `propagateProfileId` opt-in copy.

### M111. `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** There is no way for a customer to get their telemetry out, and the one existing migration tool deliberately excludes it, which sits awkwardly against a published contractual commitment. `apps/public/content/pages/dpa.mdx:58` commits to "At your choice, delete or return all Personal Data upon termination of the service" — the delete half is specified in exhaustive detail (08 §12, 10 §8.6) and the return half appears nowhere in eleven documents. A grep across the set for portability, telemetry export, or a logs download returns nothing. 01's Interfaces table (line 1673) makes the exclusion explicit and deliberate: "`export-for-cloud.sh` / `import-to-cloud.mjs` explicitly exclude telemetry and print that they are doing so", justified by D11 — the project id is inside the series fingerprint and cannot be remapped, which is a correct and unavoidable technical constraint for *migration between installs*. But that constraint does not apply to a plain export: a customer asking for their last 30 days of logs as JSONL, or their traces, does not need fingerprints remapped. 10 §13 also states "Telemetry is not backed up" and proposes telling customers so in the docs page and email footers, which makes the absence of any export path more pointed rather than less: a customer whose only copy is a system with no backup and no export has no recovery of any kind. The analytics side has `apps/api/src/routes/export.router.ts` and `GET /export/events` as precedent for what customers expect.

**Fix.** Decide the position explicitly rather than by omission, and record it in 08's deletion section next to the per-subject-erasure stance. The cheap version is a documented statement — "telemetry is not exportable; retain your own copy at the Collector by adding a second exporter" — which is honest, consistent with the dual-run guidance the migration finding also needs, and costs one paragraph. The fuller version is a `GET /telemetry/export` streaming JSONL over the compiled `zLogQuery`/`zTraceQuery` path, which is mostly reuse of the read path and is maybe 3–4 days, but it needs a rate limit and a size cap or it becomes the unbounded ClickHouse scan 10 §6 exists to prevent. Either way it should be settled before the DPA amendment in the compliance finding, since the DPA text depends on the answer.

### M112. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** 10's retention cron will not compile against 08's schema or exports. 10 §6.2: `await db.telemetrySchemaState.findUnique({ where: { key: 'ttl' } })` and `upsert({ where: { key: 'ttl' }, create: { key: 'ttl', ... } })`, and 10's Interfaces documents the model as `TelemetrySchemaState { key, desiredFingerprint, materialized, lastError }`. 08 §5 declares `model TelemetrySchemaState { id String @id; desiredFingerprint; materialized; lastError; lastErrorAt; appliedAt }` — the primary key is `id`, and 08 says "Exactly one row, id = the telemetry database name." 10 also imports `telemetryDb` from `@openpanel/db` while 08 exports `telemetryDatabase()`.

**Fix.** Rename in 10 to `id` and `telemetryDatabase()`, or rename in 08 to `key`. 08 owns Prisma so 08's names win; this is a five-minute edit but it is exactly the kind of drift that surfaces as a build break at the end of P0.

### M113. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** The gigapipe upgrade story is fully specified for CI and Cloud and entirely absent for self-hosters, and OpenPanel has no way to find out what version an operator is running. 11 §11 is an excellent contract suite pinned to `.github/gigapipe/pin.json` by digest, 04 §13 lists nineteen behaviours that must be re-proved on a digest bump, and 10 §7 tracks drift in `TelemetrySchemaState` including `gigapipeSchemaVer`. All of that protects the CI-tested path. The self-hosted path is different in three ways nobody owns. (a) `self-hosting/docker-compose.yml` is gitignored (`self-hosting/.gitignore:2`) and `./update` is `git pull` plus `docker compose up -d --pull always --remove-orphans` — so bumping `ghcr.io/metrico/gigapipe:v5.4.1` in the checked-in template never reaches an existing install, and 10 §4.1 deliberately makes `get_latest_images` "leave `ghcr.io/metrico/gigapipe` untouched". An operator is therefore frozen at whatever version they installed with, forever, while OpenPanel's code and schema assertions move forward. (b) The reverse case — an operator who edits their compose to a newer gigapipe — has no guard: gigapipe re-runs its own migrations on every boot (`ctrl/qryn/maintenance/maintain.go`), so an upstream schema change lands on a self-hoster before any OpenPanel contract test has seen it. (c) OpenPanel cannot detect either case: 11 §11.1 establishes that both buildinfo endpoints are useless (`/api/v1/status/buildinfo` returns `data.version === ""`, `/api/status/buildinfo` returns a hardcoded `"0.0.1"`), and 02:1376 repeats it. There is no compatibility matrix anywhere in the set stating which gigapipe versions a given OpenPanel version supports.

**Fix.** 10 should own a self-hosted upgrade section with three pieces. First, a published compatibility statement — "OpenPanel vN requires gigapipe v5.4.x" — in `environment-variables.mdx` and the changelog, since it cannot be enforced at runtime. Second, a startup assertion that does not depend on buildinfo: 10 §7 already reads gigapipe's own migration counter (`max(ver) FROM gigapipe.ver`) into `TelemetrySchemaState.gigapipeSchemaVer`, so compare it against a supported range at boot and log a loud warning (not a fatal — a self-hoster's install must not refuse to start over a telemetry mismatch). Third, a decision on `get_latest_images`: either extend it to bump gigapipe's pinned tag along with the three OpenPanel components, which is safer than leaving operators frozen, or document an explicit "upgrading gigapipe" procedure with the schema-assertion re-run. Roughly 2–3 days, and it is genuinely a month-three problem: it costs nothing at P0 and everything the first time upstream changes `samples_v3`.

### M114. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Nobody owns the customer-facing communication surfaces this feature changes. The repo ships four wind-down emails — `packages/email/src/emails/wind-down-{blocked,expired,final-warning,stopping-soon}.tsx` — whose whole purpose is telling a customer what is about to be deleted; 08 §14 correctly makes telemetry deletion ride the same `cron.delete.ts` terminus, and nothing updates those four templates to mention it. 10 P4 adds two *new* templates (`telemetry-usage-near-limit.tsx`, `telemetry-usage-limit-exceeded.tsx`) but no document decides whether telemetry appears in `weekly-digest.tsx`, or whether the data-health pattern in `tracking-no-data.tsx` / `tracking-data-stopped.tsx` (which today deduplicates on `Project.noDataNotifiedAt` / `dataStoppedNotifiedAt`) gets a telemetry analogue — "your collector stopped shipping" is the single most useful alert an observability product can send and it is free given that machinery exists. There is also no release-note plan (`apps/public/content/docs/self-hosting/changelog.mdx` exists) and no in-app announcement, despite 05 F6b establishing that the common state for an existing self-hoster is that gigapipe is simply absent.

**Fix.** Product/growth owns a short list: update the four wind-down templates for telemetry; decide on the weekly digest and a telemetry data-health alert (reusing the `*NotifiedAt` dedupe pattern); write the changelog entry and the in-app announcement for existing installs, pointing self-hosters at the compose instructions. ~3–4 days.

### M115. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** The published minimum-spec claim becomes wrong and nobody owns it. `apps/public/content/docs/self-hosting/deploy-docker-compose.mdx:14` states "At least 2GB RAM (4GB+ recommended)" for the whole stack, and `deploy-kubernetes.mdx:19` says the same per node. 10 §2 adds `op-gigapipe` with `memory: 2G` — itself flagged as "a placeholder to be replaced by the measured RSS ceiling from the load test" — plus a second one-shot container, while deliberately leaving `op-ch` uncapped and noting that "telemetry roughly doubles the insert rate and adds three merge trees". 10 §7.5 sizes even a small tenant's logs at ~24 GB of additional ClickHouse disk at 14 days, and a large one at ~2 TB steady state with ~126 GB/day of churn. So the published floor is wrong for any install with telemetry on, there is no sizing table for self-hosters, no "do not enable this below X" guidance, and no disk-headroom precondition in the connect flow (09 D24) — an operator can turn telemetry on from a Settings tab and fill the volume that `openpanel.events` writes to, which 08 §12 correctly identifies as turning a telemetry incident into an analytics outage.

**Fix.** 10-ops owns a sizing section for the docs page (RAM and disk floor with telemetry on, per the load test's measured numbers), a corrected minimum-spec line on the two deploy pages, and a precondition in the connect flow: 09 D24's Settings tab should refuse to enable telemetry — or warn loudly — when `op_ch_disk_free_ratio` (a gauge 10 §10.1 already defines) is below a threshold. ~1 day, gated on the load test.

### M116. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Internal contradiction in 10 on `SAMPLES_DAYS`, in the direction the doc says it engineered against. §6.1 states "gigapipe's own `SAMPLES_DAYS` | **30** | the ceiling, so a clobber over-retains rather than deletes", but §2's compose block sets `SAMPLES_DAYS=${TELEMETRY_RETENTION_DAYS:-14}` on both gigapipe services. On cloud the metric TTL is 30 d while gigapipe's own clobber TTL would be 14 d, so an F5 clobber deletes metrics sixteen days early — the opposite of the stated bias ("Deliberately biased: the failure direction is cost, not loss").

**Fix.** Set `SAMPLES_DAYS` to the *maximum* of the per-signal windows on every surface, not to `TELEMETRY_RETENTION_DAYS`. On cloud that is 30; self-hosted it can stay the single operator number. Add it to 10's test list beside gate 8 (the env-name agreement assertion).

### M117. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Two shipped, linked self-hosting deployment paths get no answer at all. `apps/public/content/docs/self-hosting/deploy-kubernetes.mdx` documents a community Helm chart (`helm repo add openpanel https://yashGoyal40.github.io/openpanel`, maintained in a third-party repo) and `deploy-dokploy.mdx` documents a Dokploy compose template (also third-party, with an open upstream issue linked). 10 §4 covers `self-hosting/docker-compose.template.yml`, the dev compose at the repo root, `self-hosting/coolify.yml` (P7) and `.github/smoke/docker-compose.yml`. Neither "kubernetes", "k8s", "helm" nor "dokploy" appears anywhere in 10; "kubernetes" appears only in 05 and 11 in unrelated contexts, and "dokploy" appears in no document at all. An operator following either published page will find no `op-gigapipe` and no way to enable the Telemetry tab, and will file an issue.

**Fix.** 10-ops states the position explicitly in the docs, on both pages: telemetry is unsupported on the community Helm chart and the Dokploy template in v1, here is the service definition and env block if you want to add it yourself, and here is who to ask upstream. Half a day for the statement. If the answer is instead "support it", that is a chart contribution and an upstream template PR and should be scoped as such.

### M118. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** The load test that gates P2 measures the write side of the analytics regression risk and not the read side, so the most likely user-visible symptom is untested. 10's "Load test — gates P2" (line 1970) runs five phases mixing analytics `/track` traffic with OTLP metrics, logs and traces, and its six pass criteria are: `/track` p95 within 10% of the phase-A mean; telemetry route p99 under 250 ms; zero loss; active parts under 300; `op-api` RSS growth under 15%; and `buffer_*_count` recovery. Every one of those is an ingest or memory measurement. None of them measures what a customer actually notices — whether an OpenPanel dashboard query gets slower. That is the direction with the most mechanisms behind it: telemetry inserts drive merge activity on the shared instance (background merge pools are server-global and are not bounded by the per-user `<gigapipe>` profile 10 §6 adds), the `type` partition key on `samples_v3` multiplies part counts (10's own criterion 4 acknowledges this), page cache is shared, and 04 §9's metadata queries plus 10 §8's retention mutations run through the *analytics* connection pool as `default` with no limits. 11's tier definitions do not add a read-side workload either. The extraction triggers 10 lists for production (`/track` p99, telemetry CPU share, event-loop lag, throughput, shed rate) are likewise all API-side.

**Fix.** Add a seventh workload to 10's load test — a synthetic analytics read loop replaying a representative dashboard (a `chart.chart` call, a funnel, and an overview page against a seeded project) at a fixed rate through every phase — and a seventh pass criterion: analytics chart-query p95 in phase D within the same A-to-A spread tolerance criterion 1 uses. Measure it from the existing `http_request_duration_seconds` labels rather than new instrumentation. Add the matching production trigger: analytics query p99 above 25% of the 7-day baseline while telemetry volume is above its median. Also worth adding a merge-pressure observation to the phase report (`system.merges` depth and `background_pool_task` saturation) since that is the mechanism the per-user profile cannot bound. Roughly 2 days on top of the existing script; it is the cheapest possible insurance on the plan's largest unmeasured risk.

## Minor (38)

### M1. `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Multi-region and data residency are not mentioned anywhere: zero occurrences of "multi-region" across the eleven documents, and the only "region" hit is an unrelated property-label note in 09. This is probably a correct non-goal — OpenPanel Cloud appears single-region and `dpa.mdx` §6 asserts EEA or SCC-covered sub-processors — but it is the first question an enterprise prospect asks about a data class as sensitive as raw logs, and the answer is not a config flag: gigapipe's reader binds a database pool at boot and picks from it at random per call, so per-region routing means a separate gigapipe deployment per region and a routing layer in `apps/api` that does not exist.

**Fix.** One paragraph in `00-blueprint.md` stating that telemetry is single-region in v1, that residency guarantees follow whatever the analytics database already offers, and what a future regional split would cost (a gigapipe deployment plus a project→region map in `apps/api`, not a config change). ~1 hour.

### M2. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Test-authoring effort is counted twice across the document set. 11's Effort states 'Test-writing only; the code under test is costed in the owning spec', then prices §3.2 ingest matrix (3.5 d), §3.3 auth matrix (1.5 d), §3.4 query matrix (2.0 d), §4 compiler goldens (2.0 d), §5.1/§5.2 FinalChart and mapper (3.0 d) and §7.1 alert state machine (3.5 d) as its own rows. But 02's P1a prices 'Tests 1–15' at 4–6 d, 07 prices 'Tests A1–A44' at 3.0 d, 01 prices 'Integration suite T1.26–T1.31 against a live gigapipe' at 0.8 w plus per-row test work inside four other rows, and 03 folds `compile.test.ts` / `grid.test.ts` / `rank.test.ts` / `shape.test.ts` into its 5–6 w. Roughly 15–20 engineer-days appear in two budgets.

**Fix.** Decide once whether 11 authors the suites or is a standards-and-harness document. Recommend: 11 owns the harness, workspace wiring, CI wiring, the sanitizer golden generator and the cross-stream matrices (§3.1, §3.6, §5, §6); each feature stream owns its own unit suites. Strike the duplicated rows from whichever side loses, and record the de-duplicated total in the blueprint.

### M3. `00-blueprint.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Environment variable names diverge across the specs for the same values. The gigapipe endpoint is `GIGAPIPE_URL` (docs 01, 02, 03, 04, 06, 10, 11), `GIGAPIPE_INTERNAL_URL` (docs 02, 07), `GIGAPIPE_URL_INTERNAL` (one occurrence), and split into `GIGAPIPE_READ_URL`/`GIGAPIPE_WRITE_URL` (doc 05). The basic-auth username is `GIGAPIPE_LOGIN` (02, 10), `GIGAPIPE_USER` (04, 05), `GIGAPIPE_USERNAME` (03) and `GIGAPIPE_BASIC_AUTH_USER` (07). Doc 08 introduces a parallel `CLICKHOUSE_TELEMETRY_URL` / `TELEMETRY_DATABASE` family. This matters more than usual because doc 10 makes `GIGAPIPE_URL` the runtime kill switch ("an unset `GIGAPIPE_URL` disables every telemetry route, both telemetry crons and the quota hook") and doc 03 §18 makes it the metrics-engine rollback mechanism — a variable that is the kill switch cannot have four names, and it must be threaded through five compose surfaces, `.env.template`, `quiz.ts` and `environment-variables.mdx`.

**Fix.** Fix the full env-var set in the name registry, including whether reader and writer are separately addressable (doc 05 is the only doc that assumes they are, and doc 10's compose ships one `all`-mode long-running node, so they are not). Doc 10 §4 already owns the templating work across `quiz.ts`, `.env.template` and the docs surface — it needs the final list before it can do it.

### M4. `00-blueprint.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Cross-references are unroutable. 91 references across docs 03, 04, 06, 07 and 11 point at draft filenames (`tenancy.md`, `ingest.md`, `read-path.md`, `metrics-engine.md`, `alerts.md`, `ops.md`, `ui.md`, `testing.md`), and 24 of doc 06's point explicitly into `_drafts/`. Several carry draft line numbers that no longer resolve — doc 04 D1 cites `tenancy.md:1074` for a symbol that is now in `01-tenancy-and-security.md` at a different line, and doc 06 T4 cites `_drafts/ingest.md §4.5` for the correlation constant registration. Exactly one reference in the entire corpus (`10-ops-retention-billing.md`) points at a numbered file. A reader routing a repair to the right stream cannot follow the links.

**Fix.** Sweep all five docs to the numbered filenames with section anchors rather than line numbers, and either delete `_drafts/` or add a header to each draft saying it is superseded. Establish the convention in the blueprint so the next revision does not reintroduce it.

### M5. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** "Structured spec, no raw PromQL" is the right first move and will still be the top complaint from the users this feature is for. The v1 surface is five functions (`rate`, `increase`, `value`, `avg_over_time`, `histogram_quantile`) × four aggregations (`sum`, `avg`, `min`, `max`) with equality/regex label matchers and `by`-style grouping. There is no `without`, no binary operators, no `absent()`, no `predict_linear`, no `label_replace`, no subqueries, no recording rules, and no `topk` exposed to the user (it is used internally for series selection). An SRE evaluating this against a Grafana panel will hit the ceiling within an hour. 01 D8 correctly gates raw PromQL behind a real parser and a rewriter whose failure mode is a silent cross-tenant read, and 01 §8 does the parser evaluation properly (I confirm `@prometheus-io/lezer-promql@0.314.0` matches gigapipe's vendored `prometheus/prometheus v0.314.0` at go.mod:44). But the product consequence — that the beachhead ships a query surface its target user considers a toy — is stated nowhere.

**Fix.** State it in the blueprint as an accepted product risk with a named mitigation, not as a security decision that happens to have a product side-effect. Two cheap mitigations worth funding in P2 rather than P6: (a) 09 D6's compiled-PromQL disclosure, which turns the structured builder into a teaching surface and is already specified; (b) a read-only "copy as PromQL" affordance so a user can take the generated query elsewhere. Also record 01 §8's own strongest observation — that the safest place for enforcement is gigapipe's `case *parser.VectorSelector: // No-op` at reader/promql/promql_transpiler/transpiler_v2.go:84, inside the engine that executes the query — and note that if the plan ever forks gigapipe for any other reason (per-signal TTL, the ruler), raw PromQL becomes dramatically cheaper and safer, so the fork decision and the raw-PromQL decision should be made together rather than five phases apart.

### M6. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** The contract the plan converges on has no existing tests, and the strategy document does not gate on establishing them before the convergence is relied upon. Verified: no test file in apps/ or packages/ references FinalChart, ChartEngine or executeChart; vitest.workspace.ts is `['packages/*', 'apps/*', '!apps/start']` so apps/start has no harness at all; 09 §Test requirements correctly identifies this and makes the harness bootstrap a named P2 task requiring the repo owner's sign-off (its Q5). 11 D16 answers the front-end risk with expectTypeOf plus a typecheck gate and explicitly cuts a front-end suite. Those two positions are compatible but only if someone states which one wins for T3/T5–T8.

**Fix.** Close 09 Q5 in the blueprint. If the answer is no apps/start harness, 09's T3/T5–T8 become a written manual-QA checklist and its estimate drops by a week — 09 already says so; make the choice rather than leaving it implied. Independently, 11's own ordering note is the right one and should be a blueprint gate: final-chart.contract.test.ts must land before the metrics engine starts, because it is the only artefact that turns 'the same type' from a compile-time claim into a behavioural one.

### M7. `00-blueprint.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `07-alerting.md`

**Problem.** The 'is telemetry available for this project' capability surface has three names and three shapes. Doc 09 D15 specifies `telemetry.capabilities`, hoisted once into the project route context, with a client `staleTime` of 5 min, a server negative cache of 60 s, a 2 s connect timeout and a 5 s deadline — and gates the entire Observability nav and all seven routes on it. Doc 04 specifies `observability.status` returning `{ retentionDays, oldestQueryableAt, signals: { metrics, logs, traces } }`. Doc 07 asks P1 for "a per-project 'telemetry enabled' flag the rule editor can read, to decide whether to offer the Metric rule type". These are the same query with three names, three response shapes and three owners.

**Fix.** Define one procedure in the blueprint with the union of the three response shapes (enabled, per-signal availability, retention clamp) and one owner, and have docs 04, 07 and 09 cite it. Doc 09's caching and timeout values are the most carefully reasoned and should carry over.

### M8. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `00-blueprint.md`

**Problem.** Running observability on the analytics ClickHouse is survivable as specified, but the isolation is weaker than the plan's language implies and the diagnosis story is missing. I verified 10's premises: `self-hosting/clickhouse/clickhouse-user-config.xml` contains exactly one `<profiles><default>` block with `log_queries=0` and no limits, it is mounted into `users.d/` on every surface, and `CLICKHOUSE_SKIP_USER_SETUP=1` is set on `op-ch` — so gigapipe would connect as `default` with unrestricted access to OpenPanel's analytics database, and 10 D16 defers scoped GRANTs "with a named blocker". Meanwhile 04 D4 and 06 T1/T2 have OpenPanel reading gigapipe's database directly. So the "second database" is a namespace, not a boundary, in both directions. And because `log_queries=0`, when a runaway LogQL scan does starve analytics there is no `system.query_log` to diagnose it from — which 10 notes but does not act on.

**Fix.** 10 D16's profile + quota block is the right control and should be P0, not deferred — it is a few lines of XML in a file that is already mounted everywhere. Add `log_queries=1` for the gigapipe profile specifically (not for `default`, which would change analytics behaviour), so contention is diagnosable. Land the scoped GRANTs from 10 §10 rather than deferring them, since 08 §10 already writes them out. And state plainly in the blueprint that storage-level isolation between analytics and telemetry is zero by design, so nobody reads "second database" as a security property.

### M9. `00-blueprint.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Multi-region and data residency appear in none of the eleven documents — a grep for `multi-region` returns zero hits and `region` returns two, both unrelated. That is probably the right answer for now, since OpenPanel Cloud is single-region today (the DPA at `apps/public/content/pages/dpa.mdx` names Hetzner Germany as the sole infrastructure sub-processor and asserts "No data leaves the EEA in the course of normal operations"), and no region or data-residency concept exists in the schema or code. But the plan makes the decision harder to revisit later in two specific ways that are worth recording rather than discovering. First, the ingest endpoint hostname is baked into every customer's Collector config and cannot be redirected per-tenant after the fact (see the endpoint-contract finding). Second, 01 D11 establishes that `op_project_id` participates in the series fingerprint and is therefore immutable, and 01:1673 already draws the consequence for `export-for-cloud.sh` — telemetry cannot be moved between installs. Together those mean that if a US or APAC region is ever added, existing customers' telemetry cannot be migrated to it at all; they would start fresh, and their old data would live out its retention window in the original region.

**Fix.** One paragraph in 00-blueprint recording the position explicitly: single region, EEA, no residency selection, and the two properties above as the reasons a later regional split would be a hard migration rather than a routing change. Cost is a paragraph now; the value is that nobody promises a customer data residency in a sales conversation without knowing that telemetry cannot follow them. If regional expansion is genuinely on the roadmap within a year, the one design change worth making early is a per-organisation ingest hostname (`<org>.telemetry.openpanel.dev`) so the endpoint is redirectable — but that is a real cost and should not be paid speculatively.

### M10. `00-blueprint.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** There is no statement of exposure to gigapipe as a vendor. The plan makes a single AGPL-3.0 project from one company (HEPVEST BV, per `gigapipe/README.md:255`) the storage engine, the query engine and the owner of the on-disk schema for three of the product's signals. 05 §14 and 11 §6 handle *drift* well — an upgrade runbook, a pinned digest, contract tests that fail on a schema change — and 10 §14 handles the licence posture. Nothing addresses abandonment, relicensing, or a decision to leave. Because the tables are gigapipe's and 10 D22 means telemetry is not backed up, an exit is a full re-ingest with no history.

**Fix.** A short paragraph in `00-blueprint.md` naming the exposure, the mitigations that already exist (pinned digest, contract suite, `MODE=init_only` schema bootstrap, no fork), and the fact that the 7–30 day retention window is itself the reason an exit is survivable. ~1 hour.

### M11. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`

**Problem.** Whether `op_project_id` is stamped on OTLP metric *resource* attributes (in addition to data points) is inconsistent, and the consequence is a real if small one. 01 D3 and 02 §4.1 both stamp at two levels and give the same reason: `emitTargetInfo` writes a separate `target_info` gauge from resource attributes, so "Stamping only the data points leaves every project's `target_info` with no tenancy label at all: invisible to every scoped query, and … two projects exporting the same resource attributes collapse onto one stored `target_info` series" (01), and 02 adds the deletion argument — "every row the gateway causes gigapipe to write must carry `op_project_id`, or it cannot be deleted". 04 §2.2's table and §1.1 specify data-point only; 09 §11 concludes "stamp `op_project_id` as a per-data-point attribute" with no resource mention; 03 §Interfaces likewise. 01 also notes the cost the others do not: stamping the resource means "**every** OTLP metrics export now emits a `target_info` series" and "The metric picker should hide `target_info`", which 04 §9.3.1's SQL already does (`AND name != 'target_info'`) but 09's picker does not.

**Fix.** Adopt the two-level stamp (01/02) — the undeletable-orphan argument is decisive given the erasure contract — and correct 04 §2.2's table row and §1.1, 09 §11's position paragraph, and 03 §Interfaces' first row. Add the `target_info` exclusion to 09's `MetricPicker` and to 11 §3.2 as a test row (11 already has row 8 for the resource strip but not for hiding the resulting series).

### M12. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`

**Problem.** Two small exposures nobody owns. (a) `apps/api`'s Prometheus endpoint is reachable unauthenticated from the internet on a default self-host: verified `self-hosting/caddy/Caddyfile.template:4` uses the prefix-stripping `handle_path /api* { reverse_proxy op-api:3000 }` while only the `worker.` vhost carries `basic_auth`, and `apps/api/src/app.ts:372` registers `fastify-metrics` at `/metrics` — so `https://$DOMAIN/api/metrics` serves the dump. `01-tenancy-and-security.md` §Detection(e) calls putting auth on both `/metrics` endpoints "a P0 prerequisite, not a P6 nicety"; `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, which owns the compose and the Caddyfile, does not mention it and deliberately puts every new gauge on the worker side, which sidesteps the question rather than closing it. (b) `02-ingest-gateway.md` §10.4 specifies `GET /telemetry/health` as unauthenticated, returning circuit-breaker state — a confirmation that telemetry is configured plus a liveness oracle for the internal backend. (c) Kill-switch keys are split across two namespaces with no single runbook: `02-ingest-gateway.md` §4 uses `telemetry:disabled:{projectId}` / `telemetry:disabled:*` for ingest, `04-read-path.md` D15 uses `op:gp:off` / `op:gp:off:<projectId>` for reads, and `10-ops-retention-billing.md` §10.3 writes 02's keys and never mentions 04's.

**Fix.** Add the `/api/metrics` exposure to `10-ops-retention-billing.md` §4's Caddyfile work as a P0 item (a `basic_auth` block or a `handle /api/metrics { respond 404 }` rule), and delete the claim in `01-tenancy-and-security.md` that it is already owned. Make `/telemetry/health` content-free or fold it into the existing `/healthz/*` convention. Put all four kill-switch keys in one table in `10-ops-retention-billing.md` §10.3 with the operator commands, since that is the document an on-call engineer will open.

### M13. `05-logs.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** Two smaller contradictions ride along with the logs write-path conflict. First, doc 05 P3.4 budgets an "OTLP-JSON normaliser" while doc 02 D8 rejects OTLP/JSON with a 415 in P1 and defers it to P6 — so the same feature is deferred and scheduled. Second, doc 05 L12 makes OpenPanel's own log self-instrumentation a `LOG_EXPORTER=loki` pino transport posting to `POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push`, but doc 01's budget-halving cut list ranks "Loki push and Prometheus remote-write ingest" as the second thing to cut ("ship OTLP-only ingest and tell customers to use an OTel collector"). Taking that cut silently removes the self-instrumentation path doc 05 depends on.

**Fix.** Resolve OTLP/JSON in one place (doc 02 D8's reasoning — doubling the security-relevant rewrite surface — is the stronger one, so doc 05 P3.4 should drop the normaliser and re-size). Record the Loki-push dependency explicitly in doc 01's cut list so nobody takes cut #2 without noticing it deletes L12.

### M14. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** 09 D14 specifies a rule for a code path every other spec says does not exist. "**Rule: `compiled` is populated only when `ctx.report === null`** (an authenticated project member on the live path) and omitted whenever the request arrived with a `shareId`. Asserted by a test (T9)." But 04 D4/D6 and 01 D12 both reject `shareId` outright on `observability.*` ("The base procedure **hard-rejects** `shareId`; it never falls back to `input`"), and 03 D1 confirms no stored report can hold a metric series in P2. So T9 — a must-row in 09's test table guarding failure mode F12 — can only assert on a request shape the router refuses before the resolver runs.

**Fix.** Keep D14's rule (it is correct and cheap to implement early) but rewrite T9 to assert the rejection instead: any `observability.*` call carrying a `shareId` is `FORBIDDEN`, which is already 11's Q27. Revisit D14 as written when the share path is actually enabled, and note in 09 that until then F12 is unreachable rather than mitigated.

### M15. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`

**Problem.** Test-facing symbol names drift from the specs that own them. 11 §Interfaces requires "`assertPromqlScoped` / `assertLogqlScoped` / `assertTraceqlScoped`, exported from a `testing` entry point" and uses `assertPromqlScoped` throughout §3.4 and §4; 01's Interfaces exposes `assertEnforced(expr, projectId)` for PromQL plus `assertLogqlScoped`/`assertTraceqlScoped`. 11 §4's golden helper calls `compile(q(input), GRID, { projectId })` returning `{ expr }`; 03 defines `compileMetricQuery(q, opts)` returning `CompiledMetricQuery { promql, windowSec, identityLabels, dropLabels, reducer, notices }`. 11 §3.4 Q1 uses `zMetricMatcher`. 11:1411's `OP_PROJECT_LABEL` is already flagged by 01 as drift; 06 §6.1 and its Interfaces use the same `OP_*` naming for the whole label family.

**Fix.** Rename `assertEnforced` to `assertPromqlScoped` in 01 (the three names then form one family, which is 04 D3's stated reason for asking for them) and fix 11 §4's signature to `compileMetricQuery(...)` returning `.promql`. Settle the constant prefix once — `TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL` per 01, or `OP_PROJECT_LABEL` per 06 and 11 — and apply it to all three docs, including 06's `OP_SESSION_LABEL`/`OP_PROFILE_LABEL`/`OP_ROOT_LABEL` family.

### M16. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** Two documents return gigapipe's error text to the client, and gigapipe's parse errors echo the query. Doc 04's failure table maps a 400 to `BAD_REQUEST` "with gigapipe's message", and doc 05 §6.1 does the same ("4xx → `TRPCError BAD_REQUEST` carrying gigapipe's message"). The compiled query contains `op_project_id="<project uuid>"` plus every user matcher value, and on the share path (if doc 03/09's answer wins) the recipient is anonymous. This directly contradicts doc 04's own instrumentation rule five sections later — "**Never** the compiled query at `info` or below — it contains label values" — and doc 06 F8's correct posture ("Never a ClickHouse error string"). Doc 01's status table is silent on read-path error bodies.

**Fix.** Adopt doc 06 F8's rule uniformly: upstream error text is logged server-side with the query and the projectId, and the client receives a fixed message per `GigapipeErrorKind` (doc 04 already enumerates the kinds, so the mapping is a table, not a judgement call). The one exception worth keeping is a compiler-authored validation message that the gateway itself produced and can vouch for. Add a test asserting no tRPC error body from a telemetry procedure contains the string `op_project_id`.

### M17. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** The ClickHouse database that holds all telemetry is spelled four different ways across the set, and it is interpolated into every erasure, retention and metadata statement. Doc 08 says `openpanel_telemetry`. Docs 01 §6 (`GIGAPIPE_CLICKHOUSE_DATABASE ?? 'gigapipe'`), 04 §2.3 (`GIGAPIPE_DB="gigapipe"`), 05 §8.1 (`CLICKHOUSE_DB: gigapipe`, with `gigapipe.` hardcoded in the DDL and purge SQL) and 10 §2 (`CLICKHOUSE_DB: gigapipe`) say `gigapipe`, from three different env vars. Doc 06 §3 adds a fifth reader of the same fact (`TELEMETRY_DB` from `GIGAPIPE_DB`) plus its own identifier validation, and doc 04 §2.4 validates the identifier separately in `telemetry-metadata.service.ts`. Any mismatch makes the retention cron and `deleteTelemetryFromClickhouse` target a database that does not exist — and doc 08 §Design already documents the exact failure mode for a related case: the subquery returns empty, zero rows are deleted, no error is raised, and the job logs success.

**Fix.** One env var (`GIGAPIPE_DB`), one exported constant with one validation (doc 06's `TELEMETRY_DB` + `TELEMETRY_TABLES` is the right shape and doc 06 §Interfaces already asks for doc 04's local copy to collapse into it), one default. Correct doc 08 to `gigapipe`. Add doc 04's §2.3 completeness grep (T-40) as the enforcement, extended to assert that no document and no source file contains a hardcoded telemetry database name.

### M18. `05-logs.md`, `01-tenancy-and-security.md`

**Problem.** Doc 05 L12/§10 ships OpenPanel's own application logs into a customer-visible project on self-hosted deployments. `LOG_EXPORTER=loki` posts `packages/logger` output to `POST /telemetry/loki/api/v1/push` "with an internal telemetry token whose project is the operator's own, `source="op_self"`". On a self-hosted instance the operator's project is a project like any other, readable by every member with `read` access — and OpenPanel's own pino output carries request context, error stack traces, and (per doc 04 §10.1's own analysis of the same logger) request URLs including query strings. Doc 01 §7 anticipates the adjacent case and gets it right for the canary ("The canary is cloud-only… on a self-hosted instance those would appear in the operator's own project list and consume their storage") and states the general principle ("OpenPanel's own telemetry is a tenant like any other. Give it a real `Project` row"), but does not extend the reasoning to what those lines contain.

**Fix.** State in doc 05 §10 what `op_self` lines may contain and require the same envelope redaction the product owes customers (doc 05 open question 12 leaves PII scrubbing out of scope — that is defensible for customer data and not for data OpenPanel writes into a customer-readable project). At minimum: exclude request URLs and headers from `op_self` lines, or make `LOG_EXPORTER=loki` default off on self-hosted with a documented opt-in that names what will be visible to project members.

### M19. `01-tenancy-and-security.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** ADVANCED_OMIT_EMPTY_VALUES is pinned to opposite values by two documents. 01 §3.1 decides `true` (it removes the empty-value fail-open on both the LogQL and PromQL paths for one line of compose config) and explicitly notes the conflict, asking for a one-line edit; 05:323 and 05:1619 still pin `false` so that invariant I2's evidence stays observable. 01 has already done the work of showing that I2 survives, re-justified from I3's analyzeStreamSelect path rather than from the =~'.*' deletion behaviour.

**Fix.** Make the edit in 05 rather than leaving it as a cross-reference. This is the smallest item in the review and also the one most likely to be silently lost, because it is two string literals in a compose manifest inside a 2,000-line document, and it is security-relevant.

### M20. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** Two gigapipe container settings are specified inconsistently across documents in ways that will break a must-gate. (a) 10 D23 requires `QRYN_RULER_ENABLED=false` set explicitly, reasoning that gigapipe's own `Makefile:5` and e2e compose default it to `true`; 11's `test/telemetry/docker-compose.yml` leaves it **unset** with the comment 'deliberately unset', and W7 asserts it is **absent** from the compose file. The moment 10 D23 is implemented in the shipped compose, W7 — a 'must' gate — fails. (b) 10 D2 and §3 use `CLOKI_LOGIN`/`CLOKI_PASSWORD` (correct: verified at `cmd/gigapipe/main.go:172-176`, `CLOKI_*` is assigned after `QRYN_*` so it wins when both are set), while 02:1860, 04:751, 05:1847 and 11's test compose all name `QRYN_LOGIN`/`QRYN_PASSWORD`. Both are read so nothing breaks at runtime, but 04:751's contract — `GIGAPIPE_USERNAME`/`GIGAPIPE_PASSWORD` 'must match `QRYN_LOGIN`/`QRYN_PASSWORD` on the container' — is false if ops sets only `CLOKI_*`.

**Fix.** Pick `QRYN_RULER_ENABLED=false` explicitly everywhere (10 D23's reasoning is the safer default) and change W7 to assert the value rather than the absence. Standardise on `CLOKI_*` per 10 §3 and fix the four other citations.

### M21. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** Doc 02 D8 rejects OTLP/JSON with 415, and its stated reason is a security one: accepting JSON "doubles the security-relevant rewrite surface — every strip/stamp rule in D3 must hold identically on a second code path". Doc 05 §4.1 then registers `/telemetry/v1/logs` for `application/x-protobuf | application/json` and §4.4 builds exactly that second path (`normaliseAnyValue`, handling OTLP-JSON's lowerCamelCase field names, hex `traceId`/`spanId`, and `*Value` unions) — with no corresponding second run of the strip/stamp matrix. Doc 05's test table has one "compiler invariants" suite and one envelope suite; neither is parameterised over the wire encoding. Since doc 05's ingest path decodes and re-emits into a Loki envelope rather than forwarding protobuf, the JSON branch is where an alias like `op.project.id` would have to be caught by a differently-written normaliser.

**Fix.** Settle the encoding question in one place. If OTLP/JSON ships (doc 05's position), doc 02's test 1 must be parameterised over `{protobuf, json}` and doc 05 must adopt it, so the alias matrix runs on both decoders. If it does not ship (doc 02's position), delete §4.4's JSON branch from doc 05 and the `application/json` content type from its route table.

### M22. `02-ingest-gateway.md`, `05-logs.md`

**Problem.** Two different CORS changes are specified for the same file. 02 D16 adds '/telemetry' to corsPaths in apps/api/src/app.ts. 05 D11 argues that is the wrong mechanism and adds a third, explicitly-denied branch. I verified app.ts:109-125: corsPaths is the *restricted* case — a matching path is pinned to dashboardOrigins with credentials: true — so 02 D16 would still permit the dashboard origin to POST telemetry cross-origin with credentials, while 05 D11's `origin: false` denies every browser outright. 05's reading of the code is correct.

**Fix.** Adopt 05 D11's explicit deny branch and rewrite 02 D16 to cite it. The reasoning in 05 is the stronger one — a telemetry credential is a server-side ingest secret and there is no legitimate browser caller — and it removes the risk of two PRs editing the same cors callback in incompatible ways.

### M23. `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** 06's Effort table double-books P1 work into P4's total and misstates its status. The `stampTraces` row reads '2–3 d … High end if P1 has not yet vendored `trace.proto` and stood up the protobufjs codec — **which is estimated in neither document and must be**.' It is estimated: 02's P1a contains 'Proto vendoring: pick versions, `pbjs`/`pbts` codegen, `configure.ts`, the `codegen:proto` CI clean-tree check, `tsdown` externals, direct deps | 2–4 d', and 02:666 lists `opentelemetry/proto/trace/v1/trace.proto` among the vendored files. The 2–3 d `stampTraces` row is P1 gateway work sitting inside a 'P4 total: 28–34 d'.

**Fix.** Correct 06's note to cite 02's proto-vendoring row, and move the `stampTraces` line item out of the P4 total into P1's budget (or into the trace-route-opening phase, per the P1-route-scope resolution) so neither phase carries it twice.

### M24. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `02-ingest-gateway.md`

**Problem.** The public ingest endpoint is printed differently in two documents, and no document treats it as the long-lived external contract it is. 02 D1 (line 27) settles the question with the right reasoning — "Mount at `/telemetry`, OTLP at `/telemetry/v1/{metrics,logs,traces}`", rejecting an `/otlp` prefix because "an OTel exporter appends `/v1/<signal>` to its configured `endpoint`" — and 02 §13.1 prints `endpoint: https://api.openpanel.dev/telemetry`. 09 §10's Quick-start snippet prints `endpoint: https://api.openpanel.dev/otlp`, which would resolve to `/otlp/v1/metrics` and 404. 09 does flag the strings as "this stream's **request** to the ingest work-stream, not a verified contract", so this is a stale copy rather than a disagreement — but 09 also says "this page and `TelemetryEmptyState` are the only two places the strings appear", which is now false since 02 §13.1 and §13.2 print them too. The larger point neither document makes: once this URL is pasted into customers' Collector configs and Kubernetes manifests, it cannot be changed by a deploy — it is the one part of this system with a multi-year deprecation cost, and there is no versioning or deprecation policy for it anywhere in the set.

**Fix.** Make 02 the single source for the endpoint string, header names and required exporter settings, and have 09 §10 render from a shared constant in `@openpanel/constants` rather than a literal — the same discipline 01 already applies to `TELEMETRY_PROJECT_LABEL`. Then add two sentences to 02 §1: that the path is a permanent public contract, and what the deprecation procedure is if it ever changes (dual-mount the old path indefinitely; there is no other option once a customer's cluster manifest has it). Under a day.

### M25. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** The tRPC router namespace is unresolved and reaches every UI call site and test path. 04 D6 and 06 expose `observability.metrics.*` / `observability.traces.*`; 05 exposes `trpc.logs.*` at `packages/trpc/src/routers/logs.ts`; 03's Self-observability section adds `packages/trpc/src/routers/metrics.ts`. 09 Q1 states the consequence — 'the UI cannot import three namespaces for one feature' — and offers a position (one `observability.*` root with `metrics`/`logs`/`traces`/`services`/`capabilities`/`ingestConfig` beneath it, saved-report execution staying on `chart.*`) that the other three documents have not confirmed. 09 D5 additionally asks 04 and 03 to confirm the page-local vs saved-report split.

**Fix.** Adopt 09's position as a P0 decision alongside the package-home decision — the two are the same conversation — and sweep 03, 04, 05 and 06's router paths in one PR. 11's file paths depend on the same answer (its Q1).

### M26. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`

**Problem.** Two live-tail designs and an unresolved router namespace. `04-read-path.md` D6 ships a bespoke polled WebSocket log tail in P3 with its own cursor (overlap-and-dedupe against the ~256 ns float64 grid), ZSET-backed socket caps, 60 s re-authorisation and a 30-minute hard lifetime. `05-logs.md` D5 explicitly rejects a bespoke tail for P3 in favour of a `refetchInterval: 5000` Follow toggle, and defers a differently-designed tail (second-granular `(floorSecNs, seen)` cursor, counted `dropped` frames, a `useWS` change) to P6. Both are careful designs; only one should exist. Compounding it, `09-ui-surfaces.md` Q1 is open: `04-read-path.md` and `06-traces-and-correlation.md` expose `observability.logs.*` / `observability.traces.*`, `05-logs.md` exposes `trpc.logs.*` in `packages/trpc/src/routers/logs.ts`, and `03-metrics-engine.md` adds `packages/trpc/src/routers/metrics.ts`. The security-relevant part is that 04's tail and 05's tail specify different re-authorisation and socket-cap regimes for a stream 04 §7.2 itself argues "must never leave an authenticated context — user emails, tokens, stack traces, request bodies".

**Fix.** Close `09-ui-surfaces.md` Q1 in one sitting (09's own position — one `observability.*` root, saved-report execution staying on `chart.*` — is the workable one) and delete the losing tail design. Whichever survives must keep 04 §10.1's periodic re-authorisation and hard lifetime, which is the property that distinguishes it from `wsProjectEvents`' authorise-once behaviour.

### M27. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`

**Problem.** `ADVANCED_PROMETHEUS_MAX_SAMPLES` is pinned to a value that 10 itself says provides no protection. 03 §5 requires it to be "set explicitly" because gigapipe's `MetricsMaxSamples` has no default and is global across every tenant; 04 §3 pins `50000000`; 10 §3 pins the same and then adds "**UNVERIFIED and possibly a no-op:** 50 M is also Prometheus's own upstream default for `--query.max-samples` … If the goal is to protect the shared ClickHouse, pick a value deliberately *below* the default once it is known (Q6)."

**Fix.** Either resolve 10's Q6 in P0 (it is one `curl /config` against a booted container) and set a value below the discovered default, or state plainly in all three docs that the pin is documentation of intent and that the only real bound on a runaway metric query is gigapipe's fixed, non-configurable 30 s engine timeout.

### M28. `11-testing-strategy.md`, `07-alerting.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`

**Problem.** 11's test matrices are written against revised-away versions of two specs. 11 D4 says it will not design other streams' schemas and then describes 07's model as "`MetricAlertState` with `incidentId`, `lastEvaluatedTick`, `fireCount`, plus an append-only `MetricAlertEvent`" — but 07 D6 explicitly cut `fireCount` along with the flap detector ("Removes `fireCount`, `fireCountSince`, two event kinds and a notification variant"). 11 §10 row 14 tests `missingSeries: 'keep'`, which 07 D6 also cut ("The 'exporter died' case it serves is better expressed as an explicit `absent()`-shaped query"), and 11's P5 SHOULD list carries it forward. 11 §4.4 row 10's `histogram_quantile` fold and row 11's summary-type forcing cite "`metrics-engine.md` §16.4", a section number 03 does not have (03 ends at §19), and 11 §4.4 row 9's fold table lists `min_over_time`/`max_over_time`/`sum_over_time`/`count_over_time`, all of which 03 §0 defers out of v1.

**Fix.** Re-derive 11 §10 and §4.4 against the current 07 and 03: drop `fireCount` and `missingSeries: keep` rows, drop the deferred `*_over_time` fold rows (or mark them as gated on 03's deferred-item table), and fix the `§16.4` reference. More usefully, add a one-line "pinned against `07-alerting.md` §4 and `03-metrics-engine.md` §6.4 as of <date>" header to each matrix so the next revision of either spec has an obvious update site.

### M29. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `03-metrics-engine.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`

**Problem.** The three specs disagree on how many executor dispatch sites exist and on whether the compiler catches a missed one. 09 D6 names **four** (`chart.chart`, `chart.aggregate`, `getReportDataCore`, `agents/tools/dashboard.ts`). 03 §15.3 names **seven** (adding `reports.service.ts:215,219`, `mcp/reports.ts:302,304`, `mcp/reports.ts:349,351` and `export.controller.ts:201`, the last of which 03 then corrects to a no-op). 08 §3 names **nine** sites for the discriminator, a different list again. On the type-safety question, 09 D7 says "`tsc` fails on both lines" of `transformReportEventItem` while 08 F4 says the same omission has detection "none". Verified in the repo: `transformReportEventItem` reads `item.segment` and `item.property` after the formula early-return, so 09 is right at compile time and 08 is right for `listReportsCore` and the untyped read casts.

**Fix.** Merge the three lists into one table in 03 §15.3 (the most complete) and have 09 D6 and 08 §3 reference it. Correct 08 F4's detection column to distinguish the `tsc`-caught site from the silent ones, since 08's own sequencing ("same PR as … the nine whitelist sites") depends on knowing which are mechanical.

### M30. `03-metrics-engine.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** `FinalChart` reuse is a real win and I would keep it, but it is leakier than decision #4 claims and one leak reaches the user with nothing to explain it. I verified the mechanism: `average()` in packages/common/src/math.ts:8-18 takes `includeZero = false` and filters zeros out, and all four call sites in packages/db/src/engine/format.ts (lines 81, 120, 161) omit the argument. For event counts that is deliberate and correct — a zero bucket means "no events", and averaging over active buckets is the intended semantic. For a gauge, an error ratio, or a rate that is legitimately zero, the displayed average is inflated by exactly the proportion of zero buckets, and the number appears on the metric card that 09 §5 puts on dashboards. 03 §10 discloses this honestly ("`average` excludes zeros… That matches what the event engine already does, so it stays") but there is no notice code for it in 03 §12's catalogue and no UI surface in 09. Note this is the *good* kind of leak — it is disclosed, bounded, and cheaper than a parallel formatter — which is why decision #4 survives.

**Fix.** Either pass `includeZero: true` for `dataSource === 'metrics'` (a two-line change confined to the metrics path, since `format()` already receives the definitions), or add a `zeros_excluded_from_average` code to 03 §12's notice catalogue so the number carries its own caveat. Do not build a second formatter. Separately, since 03 §10's `parseMatrixValue` maps `NaN`/`+Inf`/`-Inf` to `null` and D8 refuses to widen `count` to `number | null`, spell out in 03 §11 what a `+Inf` bucket renders as under each `fill` mode — `+Inf` is the normal result of a ratio with a flat denominator, so this is not a corner case, and "carried forward from the previous bucket" is a lie the user should be told about.

### M31. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Live log tail has three designs and only one of them carries the log-PII hazard it creates. Doc 04 D7 ships a server-side 1 Hz WebSocket poller in P3 at `GET /live/logs/:projectId?f=<base64url(json)>`, and correctly notes that `requestLoggingHook` is registered globally (app.ts:133) so the handshake URL — containing the user's raw search string, which is what someone types when hunting for a token or an email in their logs — lands in the API access log; it requires a new redaction rule and test T-27. Doc 05 L5 defers live tail to P6 entirely and ships a 10-second `refetchInterval`, with a stated multi-replica argument. Doc 09 references "/logs Live mode" as if it exists. If doc 05's answer wins, doc 04's redaction requirement is orphaned; if doc 04's wins, doc 05's P6 deferral and its effort model are wrong, and the redaction rule is a P3 blocker nobody owns.

**Fix.** Record the decision in whichever document owns `/logs` (doc 05) and delete the other design. Doc 05's reasoning is stronger (multi-replica pollers, unbounded `TailKey`s per process) and its 10 s refetch has no querystring hazard because filters travel in a tRPC body. If doc 04's tail is revived later, its §10.1 point 2 (redact `f` on `/live/logs/*`, tested) must move with it as a blocking item, not a note.

### M32. `04-read-path.md`, `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Live tail is in two phases at once. 04 D6 designs a server-side poll pushed over a fifth @fastify/websocket route, D7 cuts the pub/sub nudge 'from P3' (implying the tail is in P3), and 04's full-work-stream effort table budgets 'Live tail: the WS route, caps (ZSET + reaper), re-auth, overlap/dedupe, backpressure; T35–T40 | 1.0' week. 05 D5 defers live tail to P6 and chooses for P3 'a Follow toggle that sets refetchInterval: 5000 on the newest page' — with an explicit argument that the bespoke tail 'is a scaling answer to a load nobody has yet'. Separately, 04's config carries a single GIGAPIPE_RETENTION_DAYS as 'an advisory mirror of gigapipe's SAMPLES_DAYS' feeding oldestQueryableAt (D14), but 10 D9 ships one window per signal — metrics 30 d, logs 14 d, traces 7 d — so a single number makes oldestQueryableAt wrong for two of three signals.

**Fix.** Adopt 05 D5 (Follow toggle in P3, WS tail in P6) and remove the 1.0 w tail row from 04's P3 total, or say plainly that 04 owns the tail and 05 D5's argument is overruled. Change 04's retentionDays to a per-signal record keyed the same way 10 D9's windows are, and have observability.status return oldestQueryableAt per signal rather than one value.

### M33. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`, `04-read-path.md`, `08-schema-changes.md`, `11-testing-strategy.md`

**Problem.** Pre-creating gigapipe's tables is the right call and is the plan's largest year-two liability, and only one document budgets for it. 10 D4 has OpenPanel create `samples_v3` and `metrics_15s` before gigapipe's first boot with `type` hoisted into the `PARTITION BY` — necessary, because `PARTITION BY` cannot be altered and `ttl_only_drop_parts = 1` (which `rotate.go:78-79` re-asserts on every boot) makes per-signal retention collapse to `max(log_ttl, metric_ttl)` on mixed parts. The risk is bounded better than I expected: `ctrl/qryn/sql/log.sql` opens with `## APPEND ONLY!!!!!`, so upstream never edits existing statements, and appended `ADD COLUMN IF NOT EXISTS` statements still apply to a pre-created table. But it is not zero — 10 itself notes that gigapipe's `MODIFY ORDER BY (fingerprint, timestamp_ns, type)` on `metrics_15s` "is a no-op because our sort key is already exactly that", which is true today and is a coincidence, not a contract. Combined with 04 D4's and 06 T1/T2's direct SQL against gigapipe's internal schema, every image bump becomes a schema-compatibility review. Only 04 §13 ("gigapipe upgrade qualification") budgets it; 11 §11 has contract tests but no schema-diff gate.

**Fix.** Keep the pre-create — the alternative is worse. Make gigapipe upgrade qualification a standing, owned process in the blueprint rather than a section inside 04: an image-digest pin (which 01 already requires), a checked-in snapshot of `ctrl/qryn/sql/*.sql` diffed on every bump, the sanitizer golden-table regeneration 02 test 2 requires, and the `image digest → vendored Prometheus version → lezer-promql version` mapping 01 §8 and 11 D7/D8 both specify. 11 D8's point that `docker-build.yml:11` has `**/docs/**` in `paths-ignore` — so a pin file under `docs/` triggers no CI at all — applies to that snapshot too.

### M34. `09-ui-surfaces.md`, `05-logs.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** The Services surface and its phase are inconsistent across docs. 09 D12 defers it: "**Services moves to P6, and the `SparklineBars` extraction moves with it**", and 09 §Effort puts it in the P6 row — but 09 §1's sidebar block still renders `<SidebarLink href={'/services'} icon={ServerIcon} label="Services" />` with only a `{/* P6 */}` comment, and 09 §Interfaces still declares `services: { list: … }` in the P2 router surface. 09 §2's route table lists both `/services` files as P6 while the sidebar renders the link unconditionally inside the capability gate, so a P2 ship would put a dead link in the sidebar for every enabled project. Neither 05 nor 10 mentions a services surface at all, so nothing else contradicts it — the inconsistency is internal to 09 but user-visible.

**Fix.** Remove the `/services` `SidebarLink` from 09 §1's P2 block (leave it in the P6 section), and move `telemetry.services.list` out of 09 §Interfaces' P2 router into a P6 subsection. Alternatively keep 09 D12's own one-line P2 replacement — "the metrics explorer defaults `groupBy: ['job']`" — as the only P2 services affordance and say so in §1.

### M35. `06-traces-and-correlation.md`, `09-ui-surfaces.md`

**Problem.** Trace-by-id leaks existence-and-ownership through response time, contradicting an explicit requirement in the sibling spec. `09-ui-surfaces.md` §8 requires `/traces/$traceId` to render "not found" for an unknown id *and* for one belonging to another project "with **no timing difference between the two**, since the id is guessable from a pasted URL". `06-traces-and-correlation.md` T19 specifies that when the caller supplies no `?ts=`, the procedure does a bounded per-partition walk, "newest retention day first, `date = D` with a `timestamp_ns` range covering that day, **stopping at the first hit**". A trace the caller owns returns after one or two partition reads; a trace owned by another project, or a fabricated id, runs the full `retentionDays` sequence. A 128-bit trace id is not guessable, so this is not a discovery primitive — but it is an existence oracle for anyone who obtains an id out of band (a log line, an error page, a support ticket), which is precisely the paste-a-URL case 09 is legislating for.

**Fix.** Either always walk the full retention window on the no-`ts` path and return a constant-shaped result, or make `ts` required and have the UI never emit a link without it (every link source in 06 §6.3 already carries `traceStartUnixMs`). Reconcile the wording in `09-ui-surfaces.md` §8 either way, and add the case to `06-traces-and-correlation.md`'s T-15 fixture.

### M36. `08-schema-changes.md`, `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** A data-losing schema decision is flagged as time-sensitive but assigned to no phase and no owner. 08 §15's one-way-door table lists '`TelemetryUsageDaily`'s FK cascade (Q3) — changing `Cascade` → `SetNull` after rows exist is a data-losing migration — decide before P2 ships', and 08's Effort risk list repeats it as 'Q3 answered as `SetNull` after P2 has shipped: a data-losing migration on a billing table.' But 08's Sequencing table places `<ts>_telemetry_metering` in P2 without resolving the cascade, and 10-ops — which owns the billing semantics — carries no corresponding question.

**Fix.** Add Q3 to the P0 decision list with the billing owner named, since it is a one-line schema choice whose cost is entirely in when it is made. Record the answer in 08's Sequencing row for migration 4.

### M37. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** The trial wind-down lifecycle judges activity purely by analytics events, so a telemetry-heavy customer is described to themselves as inactive and is never told what they are about to lose. `apps/worker/src/jobs/cron.wind-down.ts` selects candidates on subscription state (`subscriptionState === 'trial_expired'`, line ~281), which is correct and unaffected by telemetry. But the two things the customer actually sees are both events-only. `lastEventFor` (line 232, "Newest event across all of an organization's projects") is fed by `getLastEventPerProject()` and drives `still` — whether the org is described as "still tracking" within `STILL_TRACKING_WITHIN_DAYS` (7) — which the file's own comment says "decides both the copy and who gets in first". And the `recentVolume` field, commented at line 75 as "Collected in the last RECENT_VOLUME_DAYS — what blocking would stop", counts events. So an org pushing 20k metric data points per second and no analytics events reads its own wind-down email as "you have not sent anything" and is given a volume figure of zero for what blocking will stop. 10 §11.1 correctly wires the *enforcement* side — `windDownStep ∈ {blocked, final_warning}` refuses telemetry, and the day-51 sweep reaches gigapipe — and says "The wind-down emails gain one sentence naming telemetry alongside events." One sentence is not enough: the activity signal and the volume figure both need a telemetry term or the email actively misinforms.

**Fix.** 10 should extend §11.1's email note into three concrete edits in `cron.wind-down.ts`: include telemetry recency in the `still` determination (the org-level period counter from `telemetry-meter.service.ts` is already the right source and needs no new query), include telemetry units in the `recentVolume` figure the emails render, and add the sentence to all four templates in `packages/email/src/emails/wind-down-*.tsx`. About a day, and it belongs with the P2 metering work since that is when the counters exist.

### M38. `10-ops-retention-billing.md`

**Problem.** Support tooling for telemetry is raw SQL and `redis-cli`. 10 §9.6's runbook for a shed organization is a table of hand-typed statements — `SELECT day, metric_samples, … FROM telemetry_usage_daily WHERE organization_id = $1`, `SET telemetry:disabled:{projectId} 1 EX 3600`, "raise `subscriptionPeriodTelemetryLimit` on the org row, then `clearOrganizationCache(orgId)`" — while the repo already ships an `admin/` CLI with exactly the right shape for this: `lookup-org.ts`, `lookup-project.ts`, `lookup-client.ts`, `clear-cache.ts`, `delete-organization.ts`. No document adds a telemetry command, so support will be pasting SQL into production and hand-composing Redis keys with mandatory TTLs.

**Fix.** 10-ops adds `admin/src/commands/telemetry.ts` covering the six runbook actions (usage lookup, raise limit + cache clear, per-project kill switch with the TTL enforced, fleet kill switch, marker reset, erasure-ledger status), modelled on `lookup-org.ts`. ~1 day, and it removes a whole class of support incident.
